<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Eberron: Forja do Artífice</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
4/162</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
25/11/2025, 19:47 Eberron: Forja do Artífice</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
FICER</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
FORJA DA ARTE</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Quando o hálito da górgona</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Dá vida em vez de morte pétrea,</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Quando o fogo cria em vez de destruir,</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
quando torres se erguem dos escombros</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
e alcançar o céu,</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
então as brasas ganharão vida.</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
na escuridão,</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
e cinco se levantarão</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Provocar a queda de um dragão.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
—A PROFECIA DRACÔNICA</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
O livro dele é um extenso suplemento para Eberron: A Ascensão da Última Guerra. Aquele livro é um guia completo para o mundo de Eberron; este...</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Adiciona novas opções de personagens, novos modelos de campanha e novas regras de veículos para sua campanha em Eberron. Você não precisa desse livro para usar este, mas eles funcionam melhor em conjunto.</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
E você encontrará frequentes referências a Eberron: A Ascensão da Última Guerra ao longo deste livro.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 5/162</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
25/11/2025, 19:47 Eberron: Forja do Artífice</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
UMA CAIXA DE NOVAS FERRAMENTAS</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Eberron: Forge of the Artificer é um conjunto de ferramentas repleto de novas opções para qualquer campanha de Eberron, conforme resumido abaixo.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Revise o ARTÍFICO</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
A classe Artífice foi revisada de acordo com as revisões de classes da versão de 2024 do Livro do Jogador da quinta edição. A classe inclui uma nova magia. Conteúdo adicional que</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
As opções de características de classe que apareciam em versões anteriores da classe agora aparecem como itens mágicos em um apêndice no final do livro.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Esta versão do Artífice inclui uma nova subclasse, o Cartógrafo.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
MARCAS DO DRAGÃO</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Este livro inclui talentos que concedem marcas dracônicas aos personagens e antecedentes que conferem alguns desses talentos. Um quadro lateral no início do capítulo 2 resume as novidades.</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Opções de marca de dragão neste livro.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
ESPÉCIES</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Quatro espécies atualizadas aparecem neste livro: Changeling, Kalashtar, Shifter e Warforged. Khoravar é uma nova espécie, baseada na experiência do povo de Khorvaire, mestiço de humanos e elfos.</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
descida.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Opções de bastião</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
O Capítulo 3 oferece novas instalações especiais para os personagens aprimorarem seus Bastions, além de maneiras de construir um Bastion móvel.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Campanha MopeLs</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Os capítulos 4, 5 e 6 fornecem ferramentas para ajudar os Mestres a iniciar uma campanha em Eberron de forma rápida e fácil. Cada capítulo descreve um tipo de campanha que você pode conduzir em Eberron — a vida árdua de um</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Investigação minuciosa nas ruas perigosas de Sharn, intrigas de alto risco entre as casas marcadas por dragões e as aventuras emocionantes de exploradores eruditos da Universidade de Morgrave. Baseado em</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
As ferramentas fornecidas no Guia do Mestre de 2024, estes capítulos descrevem conflitos importantes, sugerem arcos de campanha, fornecem informações sobre o cenário e oferecem fichas de estatísticas para figuras de NPCs.</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
que os personagens podem encontrar na campanha.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
TUDO SOBRE DIRIGÍVEIS</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
O último capítulo apresenta regras e recursos para aeronaves elementais: como operá-las, usá-las em combate e realizar viagens aéreas.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
25/11/2025, 19:47 Eberron: Forja do Artífice</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
A PROFECIA DRACÔNICA</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
A linguagem da Profecia Dracônica é rica em simbolismo e, em grande parte, condicional. Em vez de apresentar uma série predeterminada de eventos, ela explora a causalidade e as consequências.</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Descreve o que pode acontecer em uma esfera da existência se algo mudar em outra.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Os estudiosos às vezes abordam a Profecia Dracônica como um enigma, cuja solução é a própria recompensa. Eles analisam cada fragmento individual da Profecia como um desafio em si mesmo.</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
E exploram como uma determinada série de ações poderia cumprir a Profecia.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Os seres imortais que estão verdadeiramente investidos na Profecia, no entanto — principalmente os dragões da Câmara e os demoníacos Senhores da Poeira — veem cada fragmento como uma pequena parte de algo muito maior.</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Um todo maior. Dessa perspectiva mais ampla, um fragmento ou verso se desdobra em outro, encenando um drama que se desenrola ao longo de décadas, séculos ou até milênios. Cada verso será</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Esses imortais acreditam que suas vidas serão cumpridas a tempo e seu objetivo é simplesmente inclinar o curso do destino para um lado ou para o outro.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
O versículo da Profecia que inicia esta introdução é típico, pois cada linha admite múltiplas interpretações. Considere estas possibilidades:</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
“O hálito da górgona.” Essas palavras podem se referir a uma górgona literal, talvez uma magicamente alterada ou imbuída de energia radiante, de modo que seu hálito restaure a saúde, traga os mortos de volta à vida, ou</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
anima objetos. Ou podem se referir à Casa Cannith, cujo símbolo é uma górgona. (Membros da Câmara, em particular, acreditam que as marcas de dragão dos mortais apareceram na carne para</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
(Envolver essas pessoas na história da Profecia.) As palavras podem se referir aos artífices de Cannith "infundindo vida" em suas criações forjadas em guerra.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
“Quando o fogo cria.” Essas palavras poderiam facilmente se referir ao fogo da forja de um ferreiro, especialmente quando associadas à Casa Cannith como a górgona na linha anterior. Ou poderiam se referir a</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
O nascimento de algo novo a partir das cinzas do antigo, ou o despertar das paixões mortais (os fogos do coração) para atividades criativas após anos de violência na Última Guerra.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
"Quando torres se erguem dos escombros." Essas palavras podem ser uma referência geral a edifícios reconstruídos após um desastre, ou talvez uma referência específica a um projeto de construção no distrito de Fallen.</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Sharn (onde os destroços de uma torre caída ainda dominam a vizinhança). Ou pode se referir a um ressurgimento em uma linhagem familiar, com descendentes notáveis ​​surgindo de uma família em declínio; seus</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
“Alcançar o céu” poderia refletir suas elevadas aspirações ou seu pilotar literal de um dirigível.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
“Brasas renascem.” A esperança que se acende em meio ao desespero é uma leitura óbvia dessas palavras. No entanto, a referência a fogueiras no início do versículo não exige que as brasas aqui</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Seja positivo — isso pode se referir a um novo incêndio destrutivo que surge após um longo período adormecido, ou talvez até mesmo à eclosão de um ovo de dragão vermelho.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
“Cinco se levantarão para causar a queda de um dragão.” A melhor maneira de interpretar essa frase é como uma referência aos personagens da sua campanha (se houver mais ou menos de cinco personagens, você pode ajustar).</font></font><font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
o número). Isso é especialmente apropriado se um dragão for um antagonista importante em sua campanha — ou uma casa marcada por dragões, um governante tirano ou qualquer outra coisa facilmente simbolizada por um dragão.</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Usando a profecia</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;"><font dir="auto" style="vertical-align: inherit;">
Usando esse fragmento da Profecia como exemplo, como você, enquanto Mestre de Masmorras, poderia incorporá-lo em uma campanha de Eberron?</font></font><font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Start at the end. Assume the last line refers to the characters in your campaign bringing about the fall of one of your campaign’s major antagonists. If you’re using one of the campaign <font></font>
models in this book, the dragon’s fall might refer to one of these antagonists: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Sharn Inquisitives (See Chapter 4). Daask, whose trade in the dangerous drug called dragon's blood is harming Sharn’s population, or a powerful villain whose use of dragon's blood has <font></font>
brought about a draconic transformation. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Dragonmarked Intrigue (See Chapter 5). A dragon connected with the Chamber, or the leader of a dragonmarked house. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Morgrave Expeditions (See Chapter 6). Lady Illmarrow, leader of the Order of the Emerald Claw (detailed in Eberron: Rising from the Last War), who has draconic blood, or a fiendish <font></font>
overlord with a draconic form (perhaps Rak Tulkhesh, also detailed in Eberron: Rising from the Last War). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
With that climactic campaign end in mind, plot out a simple campaign arc—a series of four apparently unconnected events, each one reflecting a line of the Prophecy fragment. At each <font></font>
step of the way, the characters might encounter others trying to steer the fulfillment of the Prophecy in various directions. Some of these figures might want the characters to topple the <font></font>
dragon who is the antagonist of the campaign; others might want the characters to bring about a different dragon's fall. Still others might hope for the five-headed dragon god Tiamat to <font></font>
rise and cause the fall of all the dragonmarked houses in fulfillment of the fragment’s last line. <font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
You have learned how to channel magical energy through objects. See the Player’s Handbook for the rules on spellcasting. The information below details how you use those rules with <font></font>
Artificer spells, which appear in the Artificer spell list later in the class's description. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Tools Required. You produce your Artificer spells through tools. You can use Thieves’ Tools, Tinker's Tools, or another kind of Artisan’s Tools with which you have proficiency as a <font></font>
Spellcasting Focus, and you must have one of those focuses in hand when you cast an Artificer spell (meaning the spell has an M component when you cast it). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Cantrips. You know two Artificer cantrips of your choice. Acid Splash and Prestidigitation are recommended. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Whenever you finish a Long Rest, you can replace one of your cantrips from this feature with another Artificer cantrip of your choice. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you reach Artificer levels 10 and 14, you learn another Artificer cantrip of your choice, as shown in the Cantrips column of the Artificer Features table. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Spell Slots. The Artificer Features table shows how many spell slots you have to cast your level 1+ spells. You regain all expended slots when you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
12/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Prepared Spells of Level 1+. You prepare the list of level 1+ spells that are available for you to cast with this feature. To start, choose two level 1 Artificer spells. Cure Wounds and Grease <font></font>
are recommended. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The number of spells on your list increases as you gain Artificer levels, as shown in the Prepared Spells column of the Artificer Features table. Whenever that number increases, choose <font></font>
additional Artificer spells until the number of spells on your list matches the number on the table. The chosen spells must be of a level for which you have spell slots. For example, if you're <font></font>
a level 5 Artificer, your list of prepared spells can include six Artificer spells of levels 1 and 2 in any combination. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
If another Artificer feature gives you spells that you always have prepared, those don’t count against the number of spells you can prepare with this feature, but those spells otherwise <font></font>
count as Artificer spells for you. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Changing Your Prepared Spells. Whenever you finish a Long Rest, you can change your list of prepared spells, replacing any of the spells there with other Artificer spells for which you have <font></font>
spell slots. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Spellcasting Ability. \ntelligence is your spellcasting ability for your Artificer spells. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Leven 1: Tinker’s Macic <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You know the Mending cantrip. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
As a Magic action while holding Tinker's Tools, you can create one item in an unoccupied space within 5 feet of yourself, choosing the item from the following list: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
See the rules for the item in the Player's Handbook. The item lasts until you finish a Long Rest, at which point it vanishes. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can use this feature a number of times equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of once), and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
LeveL 2: Repuicate Macic ITEM <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You have learned arcane plans that you use to make magic items. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Plans Known. When you gain this feature, choose four plans to learn from the Magic Item Plans (Artificer Level 2+) table (see the Dungeon Master's Guide for the items’ descriptions). Bag <font></font>
of Holding, Cap of Water Breathing, Sending Stones, and Wand of the War Mage are recommended. Whenever you gain an Artificer level, you can replace one of the plans you know with a <font></font>
new plan for which you qualify. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You learn another plan of your choice when you reach certain Artificer levels, as shown in the Plans Known column of the Artificer Features table. When you choose a plan to learn, you <font></font>
choose it from any Magic Item Plans table for which you qualify; your qualification is based on your Artificer level. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Creating an Item. When you finish a Long Rest, you can create one or two different magic items if you have Tinker’s Tools in hand. Each item is based on one of the plans you know for this <font></font>
feature. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
If a created item requires Attunement, you can attune yourself to it the instant you create it. If you decide to attune to the item later, you must do so using the normal process for <font></font>
Attunement. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you reach certain Artificer levels specified in the Magic Items column of the Artificer Features table, the number of magic items you can create at the end of a Long Rest increases. <font></font>
Each item you create must be based on a different plan you know. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can't have more magic items from this feature than the number shown in the Magic Items column of the Artificer Features table for your level. If you try to exceed your maximum <font></font>
number of magic items for this feature, the oldest item vanishes, and then the new item appears. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Duration. A magic item created by this feature functions as the normal magic item, except its magic isn’t permanent; when you die, the magic item vanishes after 1d4 days. If you replace a <font></font>
plan you know with a new plan, any magic item created with the replaced plan immediately vanishes. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
If an item that you created with this feature is a container, such as a Bag of Holding, and it vanishes, its contents harmlessly appear in and around its space. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Spellcasting Focus. You can use any Wand or Weapon created by this feature as a Spellcasting Focus in lieu of using a set of Artisan’s Tools. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https://;www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 13/162 <font></font>

11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
CRAFTING MORE MAGIC ITEMS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Replicate Magic Item feature isn’t the only way for an Artificer to make magic <font></font>
items. The Dungeon Master's Guide provides rules for characters— not just Artificers— <font></font>
who want to make magic items of all sorts. Though many different characters can <font></font>
make magic items, Artificers can do it faster than others; each Artificer subclass <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
improves a character's speed at making items of a certain category <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When an Artificer crafts a magic item using the normal rules in the Dungeon Master's <font></font>
Guide, the character gains no special connection to that item. Class features that refer <font></font>
to items created by your Replicate Magic Item feature don't apply to items you craft by <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
other means <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
LeveL 3: ARTIFICER SUBCLASS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You gain an Artificer subclass of your choice. The Alchemist, Armorer, Artillerist, Battle Smith, and Cartographer subclasses are detailed after this class's description. A subclass is a <font></font>
specialization that grants you features at certain Artificer levels. For the rest of your career, you gain each of your subclass’s features that are of your Artificer level or lower. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 15/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
LeveL 4: Apitity Score IMPROVEMENT <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You gain the Ability Score Improvement feat or another feat of your choice for which you qualify. You gain this feature again at Artificer levels 8, 12, and 16. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levet 6: Macic Item TINKER <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Your Replicate Magic Item feature gains the following options. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Charge Magic Item. As a Bonus Action, you can touch a magic item within 5 feet of yourself that you created with Replicate Magic Item and that uses charges. You expend a level 1+ spell <font></font>
slot and recharge the item. The number of charges the item regains is equal to the level of spell slot expended. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Drain Magic Item. As a Bonus Action, you can touch a magic item within 5 feet of yourself that you created with Replicate Magic Item and cause the item to vanish, converting its magical <font></font>
energy into a spell slot. The slot is level 1 if the item is Common or level 2 if the item is Uncommon or Rare. Once you use this feature, you can’t do so again until you finish a Long Rest. Any <font></font>
spell slot you create with this feature vanishes when you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Transmute Magic Item. As a Magic action, you can touch one magic item within 5 feet of yourself that you created with Replicate Magic Item and transform it into a different magic item. <font></font>
The resulting item must be based on a magic item plan you know. Once you use this feature, you can’t do so again until you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Level 7: FLASH oF GENtus <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you or a creature you can see within 30 feet of you fails an ability check or a saving throw, you can take a Reaction to add a bonus to the roll, potentially causing it to succeed. The <font></font>
bonus equals your Intelligence modifier (minimum of +1). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can take this Reaction a number of times equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of once). You regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levet 10: Macic Item ADEPT <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can now attune to up to four magic items at once. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
LeveL 11: Spewt-STorING ITEM <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Whenever you finish a Long Rest, you can touch one Simple or Martial weapon or one item that you can use as a Spellcasting Focus, and you store a spell in it, choosing a level 1, 2, or 3 <font></font>
Artificer spell that has a casting time of an action and doesn't require a Material component that is consumed by the spell (you needn't have the spell prepared). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
While holding the object, a creature can take a Magic action to produce the spell’s effect from it, using your spellcasting ability modifier. If the spell requires Concentration, the creature <font></font>
must concentrate. Once a creature has used the object to produce the spell’s effect, the object can’t be used this way again until the start of the creature's next turn. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The spell stays in the object until it's been used a number of times equal to twice your Intelligence modifier (minimum of twice) or until you use this feature again to store a spell in an <font></font>
object. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
LeveL 14: Apvancep ARTIFICE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You gain the following benefits. <font></font>
Magic Item Savant. You can now attune to up to five magic items at once. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Refreshed Genius. When you finish a Short Rest, you regain one expended use of your Flash of Genius feature. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
LeveL 18: Magic Item Master <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can now attune to up to six magic items at once. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lever 19: Epic Boon <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You gain an Epic Boon feat or another feat of your choice for which you qualify. Boon of Energy Resistance is recommended. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Level 20: Sout oF ArtiFice <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You have developed a mystical connection to your magic items, which you can draw on for aid. You gain the following benefits. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Cheat Death. |f you're reduced to 0 Hit Points but not killed outright, you can disintegrate any number of Uncommon or Rare magic items created by your Replicate Magic Item feature. If <font></font>
you do so, your Hit Points instead change to a number equal to 20 times the number of magic items disintegrated. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Magical Guidance. When you finish a Short Rest, you regain all expended uses of your Flash of Genius if you have Attunement to at least one magic item. <font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIFICER SUBCLASSES t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
An Artificer subclass is a specialization that grants you features at certain Artificer levels, as specified in the subclass. This section presents the Alchemist, Armorer, Artillerist, Battle Smith, <font></font>
and Cartographer. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ALCHEMIST <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Craft Magic Elixirs and Potions <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: JOHN GRELLO <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
An Alchemist is an expert at combining reagents to produce magical effects. Alchemists use their creations to give life and to <font></font>
leech it away. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
LEVEL 3: TOOLS OF THE TRADE <font></font>
You gain the following benefits. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Tool Proficiency. You gain proficiency with Alchemists Supplies and the Herbalism Kit. If you already have one of these <font></font>
proficiencies, you gain proficiency with one other type of Artisan’s Tools of your choice (or with two other types if you have <font></font>
both). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Potion Crafting. When you brew a potion using the crafting rules in the Dungeon Master's Guide, the amount of time required <font></font>
to craft it is halved. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Level 3: ALCHEMIST SPELLS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you reach an Artificer level specified in the Alchemist Spells table, you thereafter always have the listed spells prepared. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ALCHEMIST SPELLS <font></font>
Artificer Level Spells <font></font>
3 Healing Word, Ray of Sickness <font></font>
5 Flaming Sphere, Melf's Acid Arrow <font></font>
9 Gaseous Form, Mass Healing Word <font></font>
13 Death Ward, Vitriolic Sphere <font></font>
17 Cloudkill, Raise Dead <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ALCHEMIST SUBCLASS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
LeveL 3: EXPERIMENTAL ELIXIR <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Whenever you finish a Long Rest while holding Alchemist’'s Supplies, you can use that tool to magically produce two elixirs. For each elixir, roll on the Experimental Elixir table for the elixir's <font></font>
effect, which is triggered when someone drinks the elixir. The elixir appears in a vial, and the vial vanishes when the elixir is drunk or poured out. If any elixir remains when you finish a Long <font></font>
Rest, the elixir and its vial vanish. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Drinking an Elixir. As a Bonus Action, a creature can drink the elixir or administer it to another creature within 5 feet of itself. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Creating Additional Elixirs. As a Magic action while holding Alchemist’s Supplies, you can expend one spell slot to create another elixir. When you do so, you choose its effect from the <font></font>
Experimental Elixir table rather than rolling. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 20/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you reach certain Artificer levels, you can make an additional elixir at the end of each Long Rest: a total of three at level 5, four at level 9, and five at level 15. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
EXPERIMENTAL ELIXIR <font></font>
1d6 Effect <font></font>
1 Healing. The drinker regains a number of Hit Points equal to 2d8 plus your Intelligence modifier. The number of Hit Points restored increases by 1d8 when you reach Artificer <font></font>
levels 9 (3d8) and 15 (48). <font></font>
2 Swiftness. The drinker’s Speed increases by 10 feet for 1 hour. This bonus increases when you reach Artificer levels 9 (15 feet) and 15 (20 feet). <font></font>
3 Resilience. The drinker gains a +1 bonus to AC for 10 minutes. The duration increases when you reach Artificer levels 9 (1 hour) and 15 (8 hours). <font></font>
4 Boldness. The drinker can roll 1d4 and add the number rolled to every attack roll and saving throw it makes for the next minute. The duration increases when you reach Artificer <font></font>
levels 9 (10 minutes) and 15 (1 hour). <font></font>
5 Flight. The drinker gains a Fly Speed of 10 feet for 10 minutes. The Fly Speed increases when you reach Artificer levels 9 (20 feet) and 15 (30 feet). <font></font>
6 You determine the elixir’s effect by choosing one of the other rows in this table. <font></font>

<font></font>
LeveL 5: ALCHEMICAL SAVANT <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Whenever you cast a spell using your Alchemist’s Supplies as the Spellcasting Focus, you gain a bonus to one roll of the spell. That roll must restore Hit Points or be a damage roll that <font></font>
deals Acid, Fire, or Poison damage. The bonus equals your Intelligence modifier (minimum bonus of +1). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Level 9: Restorative REAGENTS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can cast Lesser Restoration without expending a spell slot and without preparing the spell, provided you use Alchemist's Supplies as the Spellcasting Focus. You can do so a number <font></font>
of times equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of once), and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Level 15: CHEMICAL MasTERY <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You gain the following benefits. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Alchemical Eruption. When you cast an Artificer spell that deals Acid, Fire, or Poison damage to a target, you can also deal 2d8 Force damage to that target. You can use this benefit only <font></font>
once on each of your turns. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Chemical Resistance. You gain Resistance to Acid damage and Poison damage. You also gain Immunity to the Poisoned condition. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Conjured Cauldron. You can cast Tasha’s Bubbling Cauldron without expending a spell slot, without preparing the spell, and without Material components, provided you use Alchemist's <font></font>
Supplies as the Spellcasting Focus. Once you use this feature, you can’t use it again until you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>

hone the Armorer's magic, unleash potent attacks, and generate a formidable defense. <font></font>

craft it is halved. <font></font>

Lever 3: ARCANE ARMoR <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
As a Magic action while you have Smith's Tools in hand, you can turn a suit of armor you are wearing into Arcane Armor. The armor continues to be Arcane Armor until you don another suit <font></font>
of armor or you die. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You gain the following benefits while wearing your Arcane Armor. <font></font>
<font></font>
No Strength Requirement. \f the armor normally has a Strength requirement, the Arcane Armor lacks this requirement for you. <font></font>
Quick Don and Doff. You can don or doff the armor as a Utilize action. The armor can't be removed against your will. <font></font>
Spellcasting Focus. You can use the Arcane Armor as a Spellcasting Focus for your Artificer spells. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Level 3: Armor Mopet <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can customize your Arcane Armor. When you do so, choose one of the following armor models: Dreadnaught, Guardian, or Infiltrator. The model you choose gives you special benefits <font></font>
while you wear it. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Each model includes a special weapon. When you attack with that weapon, you can add your Intelligence modifier, instead of your Strength or Dexterity modifier, to the attack and damage <font></font>
rolls. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can change the armor's model whenever you finish a Short or Long Rest if you have Smith's Tools in hand. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Dreadnaught. You design your armor to become a towering juggernaut in battle. It has the following features: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Force Demolisher. An arcane wrecking ball or sledgehammer projects from your armor. The demolisher counts as a Simple Melee weapon with the Reach property, and it deals 1d10 Force <font></font>
damage on a hit. If you hit a creature that is at least one size smaller than you with the demolisher, you can push the creature up to 10 feet straight away from yourself or pull the creature <font></font>
up to 10 feet toward yourself. <font></font>
<font></font>
Giant Stature. As a Bonus Action, you transform and enlarge your armor for 1 minute. For the duration, your reach increases by 5 feet, and if you are smaller than Large, you become Large, <font></font>
along with anything you are wearing. If there isn’t enough room for you to increase your size, your size doesn’t change. You can use this Bonus Action a number of times equal to your <font></font>
Intelligence modifier (minimum of once). You regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>
<font></font>
Guardian. You design your armor to be in the front line of conflict. It has the following features: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Thunder Pulse. You can discharge concussive blasts with strikes from your armor. The pulse counts as a Simple Melee weapon and deals 1d8 Thunder damage on a hit. A creature hit by <font></font>
the pulse has Disadvantage on attack rolls against targets other than you until the start of your next turn. <font></font>
<font></font>
Defensive Field. While Bloodied, you can take a Bonus Action to gain Temporary Hit Points equal to your Artificer level. You lose these Temporary Hit Points if you doff the armor. <font></font>
Infiltrator. You customize your armor for subtler undertakings. It has the following features: <font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
Lightning Launcher. A gemlike node appears on your armor, from which you can shoot bolts of lightning. The launcher counts as a Simple Ranged weapon with a normal range of 90 feet <font></font>
and a long range of 300 feet, and it deals 1d6 Lightning damage on a hit. Once on each of your turns when you hit a creature with the launcher, you can deal an extra 1d6 Lightning damage <font></font>
to that target. <font></font>
<font></font>
Powered Steps. Your Speed increases by 5 feet. <font></font>

Guardian. The damage die of your Thunder Pulse increases to 1d10 Thunder damage. <font></font>

against your spell save DC. On a failed save, you pull the creature up to 25 feet directly toward you to an unoccupied space. If you pull the target to a space within 5 feet of yourself, you can <font></font>
make a melee weapon attack against it as part of this Reaction. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can take this Reaction a number of times equal to your Intelligence modifier (minimum of once), and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Infiltrator. The damage die of your Lightning Launcher increases to 2d6 Lightning damage. Any creature that takes Lightning damage from your Lightning Launcher glimmers with magical <font></font>
light until the start of your next turn. The glimmering creature sheds Dim Light in a 5-foot radius, and it has Disadvantage on attack rolls against you, as the light jolts it if it attacks you. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Additionally, as a Bonus Action, you can gain a Fly Speed equal to twice your Speed until the end of the current turn. You can take this Bonus Action a number of times equal to your <font></font>
Intelligence modifier (minimum of once), and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>

LeveL 3: EvorircH CANNON <font></font>

Using Smith's Tools or Woodcarver's Tools, you can take a Magic action to create a Small or Tiny Eldritch Cannon in an unoccupied space on a horizontal surface within 5 feet of yourself. <font></font>
The cannon’s game statistics appear below. You determine its appearance, including whether you carry it or not (and your choice of legs or wheels, for the latter). It disappears if it is <font></font>
reduced to 0 Hit Points or after 1 hour. You can dismiss it early as a Magic action. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Once you create a cannon, you can't do so again until you finish a Long Rest or expend a spell slot to create one. You can have only one cannon at a time and can't create one while you <font></font>
already have one. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
EvoritcH CANNON <font></font>
Small or Tiny Object <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

Immunities: Poison, Psychic <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Activate Cannon (Requires You to Be within 60 Feet of the Cannon). As a Bonus Action, you order the cannon to use the Flamethrower, Force Ballista, or Protector option below; you can <font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
Flamethrower. The cannon blasts fire in a 15-foot Cone. Each creature in that area makes a Dexterity saving throw against your spell save DC, taking 2d8 Fire damage on a failed save or <font></font>
half as much damage on a successful one. Flammable objects in the Cone that aren't being worn or carried start burning. <font></font>
<font></font>
Force Ballista. Make a ranged spell attack originating from the cannon at one creature or object within 120 feet of it. On a hit, the target takes 2d8 Force damage, and if the target is a <font></font>
creature, it is pushed up to 5 feet away from the cannon. <font></font>
<font></font>
Protector. The cannon emits a burst of positive energy that grants itself and each creature of your choice within 10 feet of the cannon a number of Temporary Hit Points equal to 1d8 plus <font></font>
your Intelligence modifier (minimum of +1). <font></font>
<font></font>

Level 5: ARCANE FIREARM <font></font>

<font></font>
AN ARTIFICER AND HIS CREATION MEET EACH OTHER FOR THE FIRST TIME <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
HIS CHAPTER OFFERS NEW BACKGROUNDS, SPECIES, AND FEATS. THE KHORAVAR SPECIES IN THIS CHAPTER IS A NEW GAME OPTION, REPRESENTING EBERRON FOLK WHO <font></font>
are sometimes called half-elves. The Changeling, Kalashtar, Shifter, and Warforged species are new versions of species that have appeared in other books; in consultation with your <font></font>
DM, choose the version you prefer! The Dragonmark feats in this chapter offer a new way to join the dragonmarked houses. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 30/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
DRAGONMARKS t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Dragonmarks are mysterious and magical symbols that appear on the skin of some people <font></font>
across Khorvaire. The twelve established dragonmarks are tied to specific family lines, and <font></font>
the scions of those families work hard at consolidating the power of the marks in order to <font></font>
maximize the profit and influence they gain from them. The Mark of Healing, for example, <font></font>
has given the halflings of House Jorasco an edge in providing medical care; the house has <font></font>
exploited that edge to the point where it remains virtually the only provider of healing <font></font>
services. Anyone outside the house who manifests the Mark of Healing is quickly pressured <font></font>
to join, regardless of their family line or species. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
LEFT TO RIGHT: MARK OF DETECTION, MARK OF SHADOW, MARK OF MAKING <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
GREATER DRAGONMARKS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Most dragonmarks are small—no more than the size of the bearer’s palm—and grant limited <font></font>
magical power. And even these minor marks are rather rare. But rarer and more powerful <font></font>
marks appear on some individuals, both within and outside the dragonmarked houses. <font></font>
Greater dragonmarks manifest only on people who already possess an ordinary mark. When <font></font>
<font></font>
.) a greater dragonmark manifests, it grows from an ordinary mark and expands outward to <font></font>
two or three times the original mark’s size. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The dragonmarked houses view greater marks as more significant than ordinary marks in <font></font>
every way—more important to the livelihood of the house, more essential to keep under the <font></font>
<font></font>
) house's control, and potentially more significant in terms of fulfilling the Draconic Prophecy <font></font>
that some believe is written in the marks. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
LEFT TO RIGHT: GREATER MARK OF DETECTION, GREATER MARK OF SHADOW, <font></font>
GREATER MARK OF MAKING <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
SIBERYS DRAGONMARKS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
While the people of Khorvaire have a limited understanding of dragonmarks, Siberys <font></font>
dragonmarks remain a complete mystery to them. The magic of these powerful marks <font></font>
clearly relates—visually and thematically—to the other marks in one’s house. But a <font></font>
dragonmarked heir may spend years perfecting and mastering the powers of a mark <font></font>
without ever manifesting a Siberys mark. Meanwhile, an unmarked scion of a dragonmarked <font></font>
house might suddenly display one against all expectations. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Siberys marks are mighty, unpredictable, and rare, and the dragonmarked houses keep a <font></font>
careful eye on anyone who manifests one. If its power can serve the house, fine—but an heir <font></font>
of Siberys who doesn't conceal the dragonmark’s ability or remain firmly aligned with the <font></font>
dragonmarked house is likely to become the target of assassins. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https://;www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 31/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
LEFT TO RIGHT: SIBERYS MARK OF DETECTION, SIBERYS MARK OF SHADOW, <font></font>
SIBERYS MARK OF MAKING <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Boon of Siberys, described in the “Feats” section later in this chapter, can reflect either a Siberys mark or a powerful aberrant mark. Alternatively, at a player’s discretion and with the <font></font>
DM's approval, a character's acquisition of a different Epic Boon feat can represent the manifestation of a Siberys mark. A character with ties to House Lyrandar, for example, might choose <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
the Boon of Energy Resistance, gaining Resistance to Lightning and Thunder damage, and manifest the Siberys mark of Storm. A character with ties to House Phiarlan or House Thuranni <font></font>
might represent the Siberys mark of Shadow by choosing the Boon of the Night Spirit. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Eberron: Rising from the Last War. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Dragonmark Feats <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Player's Handbook (2024) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Marks of Mystery <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Centuries of manipulation and consolidation have caused the established dragonmarks to manifest primarily in members of certain family lines. However, exceptions have always existed, <font></font>
and they've grown increasingly common in recent years. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
In Eberron: Rising from the Last War, only a character of a particular species could have each dragonmark. Those rules reflected the common perception that dragonmarks run only in <font></font>
family lines. This book instead allows a character of any species to manifest any dragonmark. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
If you choose a dragonmark for your character, consider these four options for its origin. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
DRAGONMARKED HEIR <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You are a member of a dragonmarked house and its family line—for example, if you bear the Mark of Healing, you could be a Halfling member of House Jorasco and related to the other <font></font>
dragonmarked heirs of the house. In this case, you have much in common with the NPC heirs of your house. The actions of your house influence you, although you and your DM can <font></font>
determine to what extent you become drawn into house politics. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https://;www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 32/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
Taking the background associated with your house (such as House Jorasco Heir) is the best way to build your character as a dragonmarked heir. Alternatively, you can take the House <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Agent background, or some other background reflecting your character's history, and choose a Dragonmark feat later in your career. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Since dragonmarks typically appear for the first time in situations of stress or peril where the power of the mark could prove useful, heirs of the dragonmarked houses undergo a rite of <font></font>
passage called the Test of Siberys in adolescence. Each house's test is different, but they all involve some danger designed to evoke the power of the house’s mark. Usually, a dragonmark <font></font>
appears during the test, if it ever manifests at all, but heirs sometimes manifest their marks years after failing the Test of Siberys. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Distant OFFSHOOT <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You are a distant offshoot of a dragonmarked family line, sharing the species that most commonly manifests the mark but unencumbered by ties to its house. For example, you could be a <font></font>
Halfling with the Mark of Healing but with no ties to House Jorasco. You're an anomaly; if House Jorasco became aware of you, it would try to bring you and your family line into the house. <font></font>
To build this kind of character, choose a Dragonmark feat at level 4 or later. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Mark OF PROPHECY <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You might bear a mark unrelated to your species. For example, you can choose to bear the Mark of Healing even if you're not a Halfling. Your dragonmark has nothing to do with the house's <font></font>
family line. It might be a direct mark of the Draconic Prophecy, or it could be an aberrant mark that happens to duplicate an established one. You'll probably never meet an NPC in the world <font></font>
like you, and House Jorasco might not claim you. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
To build a character with a mark like this, choose a Dragonmark feat at level 4 or later or select a house background (or the Aberrant Heir background). With your DM's permission, you can <font></font>
alter some of the background’s proficiencies to better reflect your character's origin. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ABERRANT MARK <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You bear an aberrant dragonmark. These rare marks appear on members of all species. Aberrant dragonmarks disturb the houses—you would be wise to avoid their attention. The Aberrant <font></font>
Heir background is the best way to build a character with an aberrant mark. Alternatively, choose another background and take the Aberrant Dragonmark feat at level 4 or later. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https://;www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 33/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
BACKGROUNDS t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This section presents seventeen backgrounds using the same format as those in the Players Handbook. The fourteen that represent dragonmarked heirs offer Dragonmark feats rather <font></font>
than Origin feats; the Dragonmark feats appear at the end of the chapter. Choosing one of these backgrounds is the only way to acquire a Dragonmark feat at level 1; characters who select <font></font>
a different background can choose Dragonmark feats anytime they have an unrestricted feat choice. The House Agent background grants house affiliation without a dragonmark. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
In addition to the backgrounds connected to dragonmarks and their houses, the Archaeologist and Inquisitive backgrounds support the campaigns presented in this book (see chapters 4 <font></font>
and 6). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ABERRANT HEIR <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A <font></font>
» <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
‘Wiis <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Ability Scores: Strength, Constitution, Charisma <font></font>
<font></font>
Feat: Aberrant Dragonmark (see “Dragonmark Feats”) <font></font>
Skill Proficiencies: History and Intimidation <font></font>
<font></font>
Tool Proficiency: Disguise Kit <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Equipment: Choose A or B: (A) Dagger, Disguise Kit, Costume, Traveler's Clothes, 16 GP; or (B) 50 GP <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Your aberrant dragonmark has made life challenging since it manifested. You might have hidden it successfully for most of your life or managed to avoid notice. Alternatively, you might <font></font>
have encountered suspicion and fear, perhaps coupled with the outright antagonism of one or more dragonmarked houses. You might have formed an association with the House <font></font>
Tarkanan criminal organization (named for one of the most notorious leaders of the aberrant dragonmarked in the War of the Mark twenty-five centuries ago). Or you might struggle to find <font></font>
your own way in the world, relying on your wits and the power of your mark. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARCHAEOLOGIST <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Ability Scores: Dexterity, Intelligence, Wisdom <font></font>
Feat: Skilled <font></font>
<font></font>
Skill Proficiencies: History and Survival <font></font>
<font></font>
Tool Proficiency: Cartographer's Tools <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Equipment: Choose A or B: (A) Cartographer's Tools, Bullseye Lantern, Map, Map or Scroll Case, Shovel, Tent, Traveler’s Clothes, 17 GP; or (B) 50 GP <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You've made a lifelong study of the lost and fallen cultures of the past, visiting their ruins, deciphering their written records, and examining their surviving masterworks. Perhaps you <font></font>
studied at Morgrave University or a similar institution, supplementing your time in the library with fieldwork amid ancient ruins in remote locations. Some archaeologists plunder the <font></font>
treasures of the past in search of wealth or fame, but most consider it their calling to learn from the past. In any case, the archaeologists of Eberron combine the qualities of learned <font></font>
historians with the grit of treasure hunters. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM <font></font>
House AGENT <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Strength, Intelligence, Charisma <font></font>
Lucky <font></font>
Investigation and Persuasion <font></font>
Choose one kind of Artisan’s Tools <font></font>
Choose A or B: (A) Artisan’s Tools (same as above), Fine Clothes, 20 GP; or (B) 50 GP <font></font>
You are connected to one of the dragonmarked houses, but you haven't (yet) manifested a dragonmark. You might be a member of the family by birth or an employee of the house with no <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
familial connection. You've earned your living by doing the business of the house, serving as the hands, feet, and eyes of house leadership in the world. You might have many old friends, <font></font>
mentors, and rivals in the house you serve, plus allies or enemies in other houses. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House CANNITH HEIR <font></font>

<font></font>

<font></font>

<font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
This section presents new versions of the four species introduced in Eberron: Rising from the Last War, as well as the Khoravar species. All these species have been revised to align with <font></font>
the species in the Player's Handbook (2024). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
CHANGELING <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
With ever-changing appearances, changelings reside in many societies undetected. Each changeling can supernaturally adopt any face they like. For some changelings, a new face may <font></font>
reveal an aspect of their soul. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The first changelings in the multiverse appeared in the Feywild, and the wondrous, mutable essence of that plane lingers in changelings today—even in those who have never set foot in the <font></font>
fey realm. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
In their true form, changelings appear faded, their features almost devoid of detail. It is rare to see one in that form, for a typical changeling alters their shape the way others might change <font></font>
clothes. However, many changelings develop identities with more depth, crafting a persona complete with history and beliefs to go with each shape. A changeling adventurer might have <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
personas for many situations, including negotiation, investigation, and combat. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Multiple changelings can share a persona. Personas can even pass down through a family, allowing a younger changeling to take advantage of contacts established by the persona’s <font></font>
previous users. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
CHANGELING TRAITS <font></font>
Creature Type: Fey <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Size: Medium (about 4-7 feet tall) or Small (about 2-4 feet tall), chosen when you select this species <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Speed: 30 feet <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
As a Changeling, you have these special traits. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Changeling Instincts. Thanks to your connection to the fey realm, you gain proficiency in two of the following skills of your choice: Deception, Insight, Intimidation, Performance, or <font></font>
Persuasion. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Shape-Shifter. As an action, you can shape-shift to change your appearance and your voice. You determine the specifics of the changes, including your coloration, hair length, and sex. You <font></font>
can also adjust your height and weight and can change your size between Medium and Small. You can make yourself appear as a member of another playable species, though none of your <font></font>
game statistics change. You can’t duplicate the appearance of an individual you've never seen, and you must adopt a form that has the same basic arrangement of limbs that you have. <font></font>
This trait doesn’t change your clothing and equipment. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
While shape-shifted with this trait, you have Advantage on Charisma checks. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You stay in the new form until you take an action to revert to your true form. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 42/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
KALASHTAR <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Kalashtar (pronounced kal-ASH-tar) are created from the union of humanity and renegade spirits called quori from the plane of dreams. Kalashtar appear human, but their spiritual <font></font>
connection affects them in a variety of ways. They have symmetrical, slightly angular features, and their eyes often glow when they are concentrating or expressing strong emotions. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Kalashtar can't communicate directly with their quori spirits. Rather, kalashtar might experience them as a source of instinct and inspiration, drawing on the spirits’ memories when the <font></font>
kalashtar sleep. This connection grants kalashtar minor psionic abilities, as well as protection from psionic attacks. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
KALASHTAR TRAITS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Creature Type: Aberration <font></font>
Size: Medium (about 6-7 feet tall) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Speed: 30 feet <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
As a Kalashtar, you have these special traits. <font></font>
Dual Mind. You have Advantage on Wisdom and Charisma saving throws. <font></font>
Mental Discipline. You have Resistance to Psychic damage. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Mind Link. You have telepathy with a range in feet equal to 10 times your level. When you're using this trait to speak telepathically to a creature, you can take a Magic action to give that <font></font>
creature the ability to speak telepathically with you for 1 hour or until you take another Magic action to end this effect. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Severed from Dreams. You can't be the target of the Dream spell. In addition, when you finish a Long Rest, you gain proficiency in one skill of your choice. This proficiency lasts until you <font></font>
finish another Long Rest. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https://;www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 43/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
KHORAVAR <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: DAARKEN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Over the course of centuries, those descended from both humans and elves have developed their own communities and traditions in Khorvaire. The rise of House Lyrandar and House <font></font>
Medani has strengthened this identity. Members of these communities call themselves Khoravar, an Elvish term meaning “children of Khorvaire,” as they dislike the term “half-elf.” <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Many Khoravar espouse the idea of being “the bridge between,” believing they are called to facilitate communication and cooperation between members of different cultures or species. <font></font>
Khoravar who follow this philosophy often become bards, diplomats, mediators, or translators. Others, fascinated by their distant connection to the Fey, seek to build bridges between the <font></font>
Material Plane and the Feywild of Thelanis. These Khoravar often become druids or warlocks with archfey patrons. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
KHORAVAR TRAITS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Creature Type: Humanoid <font></font>
Size: Medium (about 4-6 feet tall) or Small (about 2-4 feet tall), chosen when you select this species <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Speed: 30 feet <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
As a Khoravar, you have these special traits. <font></font>
Darkvision. You have Darkvision with a range of 60 feet. <font></font>
Fey Ancestry. You have Advantage on saving throws you make to avoid or end the Charmed condition. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Fey Gift. You know the Friends cantrip. Whenever you finish a Long Rest, you can replace that cantrip with a different cantrip from the Cleric, Druid, or Wizard spell list. Intelligence, <font></font>
Wisdom, or Charisma is your spellcasting ability for the spell you cast with this trait (chosen when you select this species). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lethargy Resilience. When you fail a saving throw to avoid or end the Unconscious condition, you can succeed instead. Once you use this trait, you can't do so again until you finish 1d4 <font></font>
Long Rests. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Skill Versatility. You gain proficiency in one skill or with one tool of your choice. Whenever you finish a Long Rest, you can replace it with another skill or tool proficiency. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 44/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
SHIFTER <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: JOHN THACK <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Shifters—sometimes called “weretouched”—descend from people who contracted full or partial lycanthropy. Humanoids with a bestial aspect, shifters can’t change shape fully, but they <font></font>
can enhance their animalistic features temporarily in a process they call “shifting.” <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Shifters resemble humans in height and build but are typically more lithe and flexible. Their facial features have a bestial cast, often with large eyes and pointed ears; most shifters also <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
possess prominent canine teeth. They grow fur-like hair on nearly every part of their bodies. While a shifter’s appearance might remind an onlooker of an animal, the shifter remains clearly <font></font>
identifiable as a Humanoid even when at their most feral. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
SHIFTER TRAITS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Creature Type: Humanoid <font></font>
Size: Medium (about 4-7 feet tall) or Small (about 2-4 feet tall), chosen when you select this species <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Speed: 30 feet <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
As a Shifter, you have these special traits. <font></font>
Bestial Instincts. Channeling the beast within, you gain proficiency in one of the following skills of your choice: Acrobatics, Athletics, Intimidation, or Survival. <font></font>
Darkvision. You have Darkvision with a range of 60 feet. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Shifting. As a Bonus Action, you can shape-shift to assume a more bestial appearance. This transformation lasts for 1 minute or until you revert to your normal appearance as a Bonus <font></font>
Action. When you shift, you gain Temporary Hit Points equal to 2 times your Proficiency Bonus. You can shift a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all <font></font>
expended uses when you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Whenever you shift, you gain the benefit of one of the following options (choose when you select this species): <font></font>
Beasthide. You gain 1d6 additional Temporary Hit Points. While shifted, you have a +1 bonus to your Armor Class. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Longtooth. When you shift and as a Bonus Action on your other turns while shifted, you can use your elongated fangs to make an Unarmed Strike. If you hit with this Unarmed Strike and <font></font>
deal damage, you can deal Piercing damage equal to 1d6 plus your Strength modifier, instead of the normal damage of an Unarmed Strike. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Swiftstride. While you are shifted, your Speed increases by 10 feet. Additionally, you can move up to 10 feet as a Reaction when a creature ends its turn within 5 feet of you. This reactive <font></font>
movement doesn't provoke Opportunity Attack action. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Wildhunt. While shifted, you have Advantage on Wisdom checks. Additionally, no creature within 30 feet of you can have Advantage on an attack roll against you unless you have the <font></font>
Incapacitated condition. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
WARrFORGED <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: IGNACIO BAZAN-LAZCANO <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ae | (&gt; eel eo: <font></font>
| on " <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
&lt;@ em Vey <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Warforged are mechanical beings built as weapons to fight in the Last War. An unexpected breakthrough produced sentient beings made from wood and metal that nevertheless can feel <font></font>
pain and emotion. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Warforged comprise a blend of organic and inorganic materials. Rootlike cords infused with alchemical fluids serve as their muscles, wrapped around a framework of steel, darkwood, or <font></font>
stone. Armored plates form a protective outer shell and reinforce joints. The more a warforged cultivates their individuality, the more likely they are to modify their body, seeking out an <font></font>
artificer to customize the look of their face, limbs, and plating. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
WarrorGeD TRAITS <font></font>
Creature Type: Construct <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Size: Medium (about 6-8 feet tall) or Small (about 3-4 feet tall), chosen when you select this species <font></font>
Speed: 30 feet <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
As a Warforged, you have these special traits. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Construct Resilience. You have Resistance to Poison damage. You also have Advantage on saving throws to avoid or end the Poisoned condition. <font></font>
Integrated Protection. You gain a +1 bonus to your Armor Class. In addition, armor you have donned can’t be removed against your will while you're alive. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Sentry’s Rest. You don't need to sleep, and magic can't put you to sleep. You can finish a Long Rest in 6 hours if you spend those hours in an inactive, motionless state. During this time, <font></font>
you appear inert but remain conscious. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Specialized Design. You gain one skill proficiency and one tool proficiency of your choice. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Tireless. You don't gain Exhaustion levels from dehydration, malnutrition, or suffocation. <font></font>
<font></font>

FEATS t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This section presents twenty-eight new feats by category. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
DRAGONMARK Feats <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
These feats belong to the Dragonmark category. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ABERRANT DRAGONMARK <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Dragonmark Feat (Prerequisite: Eberron Campaign, Can't Have Another Dragonmark Feat) <font></font>
You gain the following benefits. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Aberrant Fortitude. When you fail a Constitution saving throw, you can take a Reaction to roll 1d4 and add the number rolled to the save, potentially turning the failure into a success. Once <font></font>
you've used this benefit, you can't use it again until you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Aberrant Magic. You know one cantrip of your choice from the Sorcerer spell list. Also, choose a level 1 spell from that spell list. You always have that spell prepared. You can cast it once <font></font>
without a spell slot, and you regain the ability to cast it in that way when you finish a Short or Long Rest. You can also cast this spell using any spell slots you have. Constitution is your <font></font>
spellcasting ability for this spell. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Aberrant Surge. When you cast the level 1 spell from this feat, you can expend one of your Hit Point Dice and roll it. If you roll an even number, you gain a number of Temporary Hit Points <font></font>
equal to the number rolled. If you roll an odd number, one creature within 30 feet of you (not including you) takes Force damage equal to the number rolled. If no other creatures are in <font></font>
range, you take the damage. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Mark oF DETECTION <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Dragonmark Feat (Prerequisite: Eberron Campaign, Can't Have Another Dragonmark Feat) <font></font>
You gain the following benefits. <font></font>
Deductive Intuition. When you make an Intelligence (Investigation) or Wisdom (Insight) check, you can roll 1d4 and add the number rolled to the ability check. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Magical Detection. You always have the Detect Magic and Detect Poison and Disease spells prepared. You can cast each spell once without a spell slot, and you regain the ability to cast it <font></font>
in that way when you finish a Long Rest. You can also cast these spells using any spell slots you have of the appropriate level. Intelligence, Wisdom, or Charisma is your spellcasting ability <font></font>
for these spells (choose when you select this feat). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you reach character level 3, you also always have the See Invisibility spell prepared and can cast it the same way. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Spells of the Mark. lf you have the Spellcasting or Pact Magic class feature, the spells on the Mark of Detection Spells table are added to that feature’s spell list. <font></font>

GREATER ABERRANT Mark <font></font>

whenever you finish a Short or Long Rest. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Mark of Inspiration. When you cast a cantrip, you can roll one or two of your unexpended Hit Point Dice. You gain a number of Temporary Hit Points equal to the number rolled plus your <font></font>
Constitution modifier, and one creature of your choice within 30 feet of you (not including you) takes Force damage equal to the number rolled. Those dice are then expended. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can use this benefit a number of times equal to your Proficiency Bonus, and you regain all expended uses when you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>

Dragonmark Spellcasting. You have one extra spell slot to cast the spells granted by your Dragonmark feat. The spell slot’s level is half your level (round up), to a maximum of level 5. You <font></font>
regain the expended slot when you finish a Short or Long Rest. You can use this spell slot to cast only a spell you have prepared because of your Dragonmark feat or the Dragonmark <font></font>
Preparation benefit of this feat. <font></font>
