<font></font>
ARTIST: CULLEN COLE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ADVENTURERS BRING A MYSTERIOUS ARTIFACT RECOVERED FROM XEN'DRIK <font></font>
BACK TO THEIR MOBILE BASTION ABOARD A LYRANDAR SAILING SHIP <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
HE DUNGEON MASTER'S GUIDE CONTAINS THE RULES FOR BASTIONS, WHICH ALLOW CHARACTERS TO ESTABLISH A BASE OF OPERATIONS FOR THEIR ADVENTURES. THIS <font></font>
chapter adds further options for characters to establish Bastions that use Eberron’s special qualities and resources. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 57/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
MOBILE BASTIONS t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Bastion rules in the Dungeon Master's Guide assume that characters establish their Bastions in a fixed location and that their adventures might take them away from their Bastions at <font></font>
times, perhaps even for long periods. But in Eberron (and perhaps other settings), characters might establish a permanent base of operations that isn’t linked to a fixed location—maybe on <font></font>
a sailing ship, a lightning rail cart, or even an airship. The DM decides whether to allow characters to establish a mobile Bastion, which uses the following alterations to the normal Bastion <font></font>
rules. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A mobile Bastion has two basic requirements: it must be built in a vehicle form, and one of its special facilities must allow for propulsion. (The Lyrandar Helm, Navigator's Helm, and Orien <font></font>
Helm described in this chapter fulfill that requirement.) If multiple characters combine their Bastions into a single vehicle, each Bastion must include the same kind of special facility that <font></font>
allows for propulsion, and in order for the vehicle to travel, the characters must all issue the Empower order to those facilities at the same time. In this case, however, the combined Bastion <font></font>
can travel more than 8 hours per day; the facility hirelings can operate the vehicle in shifts. Thus, a combined Bastion with at least three Navigator's Helm facilities can sail for 24 hours a <font></font>
day as the three hirelings trade off navigation in 8-hour shifts. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
If characters are traveling with their mobile Bastions, they can also have encounters and face obstacles just as if they were traveling away from their Bastions. When the DM is planning <font></font>
obstacles for such a journey (as described in the Dungeon Master's Guide), a potential consequence of an encounter or obstacle is the temporary loss of access to one or more of the <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Bastion's facilities, including the facility that allows propulsion. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Players and the DM should work together to create a Bastion map that fits within the chosen vehicle. Facilities can be slightly larger or smaller than the rules in the Dungeon Master's Guide <font></font>
specify, at the DM’s discretion, in order to fit. A mobile Bastion can’t have defensive walls. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https://;www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 58/162 <font></font>

ARTIFICER’S ForGe <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Level 13 Bastion Facility <font></font>
<font></font>
Prerequisite: Ability to use Artisan’s Tools as a Spellcasting Focus <font></font>
Space: Roomy <font></font>
<font></font>
Hirelings: 2 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Order: Craft <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
An Artificer's Forge includes two magewright hirelings who are skilled in creating magic items. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Order <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Empower <font></font>
Trade <font></font>
Empower <font></font>
Empower <font></font>
Craft <font></font>
Research <font></font>
Empower <font></font>
Empower <font></font>
Research <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Recruit <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Crafting Assistants. \f you create a magic item in your Bastion using the rules in the Dungeon Master's Guide, one or both of this facility’s hirelings can assist you, reducing the time <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
required for the crafting. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Craft: Magic Item. When you issue the Craft order to this facility, you commission the facility's hirelings to craft a Common or an Uncommon magic item chosen by you from the Dungeon <font></font>
Master's Guide. The facility has the tool required to craft the item, and the hirelings have proficiency with that tool as well as proficiency in the Areana skill. The two hirelings assist each <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
other, reducing the time required to craft the item. If the item allows its user to cast any spells from it, the hirelings have those spells prepared. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Charge Refreshment. Whenever a magic item in your Bastion regains charges, it regains 1 extra expended charge. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Construct Force <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Level 17 Bastion Facility <font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
time and costs you the amount listed in the table. <font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
Constructs made in this facility count as Bastion Defenders. Deduct any you lose from your Bastion Defenders roster. You can choose not to count one or more of these creatures as <font></font>
Bastion Defenders, in which case they can’t be called on to defend the Bastion. Instead, they act in accordance with their nature and use their stat blocks in the Monster Manual. <font></font>

Order: Empower <font></font>
<font></font>
A Dragonmark Outpost is an area set aside for the use of your dragonmarked house. The hireling is a house agent and serves as a liaison between you and your superiors in the house. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you add this facility to your Bastion, your Renown Score with your house increases by 1. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Dragonmark Empowerment (Requires Any Dragonmark Feat). When you finish a Long Rest in your Bastion, you gain one extra spell slot of level 2. You can use this spell slot to cast only a <font></font>
spell you have prepared because of a Dragonmark feat. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Empower: House Favor. When you issue the Empower order to this facility, you instruct your hireling to call in a favor from your dragonmarked house. You can use any benefit of your house <font></font>
that your Renown Score affords you (as described in chapter 5), and this favor doesn't count against the total number of favors available to you. Once you use this feature, you can't use it <font></font>
again until your Renown Score with your house increases. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
INouisitive AGENCY <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Level 13 Bastion Facility <font></font>
Prerequisite: None <font></font>
Space: Roomy <font></font>
Hirelings: 1 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Order: Research <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This facility is an office for an inquisitive agency—either the headquarters of an agency you control or a branch office for a larger agency (such as Information Acquisition or Karr’Aashta’s <font></font>
Investigations, both of which are discussed in chapter 4). Its one hireling is a secretary who maintains an extensive directory of useful contacts and helpful agents. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Research: Information Gathering. When you issue the Research order to this facility, you commission the agency's secretary to gather information from contacts and agents regarding a <font></font>
specific individual you designate, assembling a comprehensive picture of that person’s activities during the previous 7 days and over the course of the next 7 days, within 10 miles of your <font></font>
Bastion. Unless the DM deems that certain information is beyond these agents’ ability to uncover, you learn the following information: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Where the person is and has been staying <font></font>
<font></font>
What they've spent money on <font></font>
<font></font>
Whom they've met with <font></font>
<font></font>
Where they've shown identification papers to the authorities <font></font>
<font></font>
If your Bastion is mobile, it takes time to establish this network of agents in a new place. When your Bastion is traveling, you can’t issue the Research order to this facility. After it arrives at <font></font>
its destination, the first time you issue the Research order, this facility’s hireling spends 7 days establishing a new network, after which you can use the Research order normally. <font></font>

KuNDARAK VAULT <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Level 9 Bastion Facility <font></font>
<font></font>
Prerequisite: Renown 15+ with House Kundarak <font></font>
Space: Cramped <font></font>
<font></font>
Hirelings: 1 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Order: Trade <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A Kundarak Vault occupies space in your Bastion like any other special facility, but it is an extradimensional space that can be accessed from other places. As a Kundarak facility, it <font></font>
provides exceptionally secure storage for your trade goods and other treasures. Only you and your Bastion’s hirelings can open the vault door. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Kundarak Access. Because the vault is an extradimensional space, you (and only you) can access its contents from any Kundarak bank facility in the world. These banks are found in cities <font></font>
across Khorvaire (and also in Stormreach, in Xen’drik). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Trade: Goods. When you issue the Trade order to this facility, its hireling spends the next 7 days procuring nonmagical items that have a total value of 2,000 GP or less and stores them in <font></font>
the Kundarak Vault, or the hireling uses those 7 days to sell goods in the Kundarak Vault. You bear the total cost of any purchases, and the maximum value of the items purchased <font></font>
increases to 5,000 GP when you reach level 13. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you sell goods from your Kundarak Vault, the buyer pays you 20 percent more than the standard price; this profit increases to 50 percent when you reach level 13 and 100 percent <font></font>
when you reach level 17. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
LYRANDAR HELM <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Level 13 Bastion Facility <font></font>
<font></font>
Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with House Lyrandar <font></font>
Space: Cramped <font></font>
<font></font>
Hirelings: 1 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Order: Empower <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A Lyrandar Helm allows a mobile Bastion built within an elemental airship or an elemental galleon to travel. This facility's hireling is a dragonmarked heir of House Lyrandar. On an <font></font>
elemental airship, the airship's Helm crew station is part of this facility (see chapter 7). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Empower: Travel. When you issue the Empower order to this facility, you instruct its hireling to pilot the Bastion to a destination you specify. The Bastion travels at the vehicle's speed, 8 <font></font>
hours per day, for the duration of the Bastion turn. The Bastion stops early when it reaches the specified destination or if something happens to stop your travel. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you issue the Empower order to this facility, the DM rolls on the Bastion Events table as if you had issued the Maintain order to your Bastion. <font></font>

<font></font>
Manirest ZONE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Level 13 Bastion Facility <font></font>
<font></font>
Prerequisite: Ability to use a Spellcasting Focus <font></font>
Space: Vast <font></font>
<font></font>
Hirelings: 1 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Order: Empower <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A Bastion with a Manifest Zone is infused with the planar energy of one of Eberron’s planes of existence. Choose the plane when you build this facility. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Empower: Manifest Charm. When you issue the Empower order to this facility, the facility’s hireling conducts magical rites for the next 7 days. At the end of that period, you gain a special <font></font>
Charm (see “Supernatural Gifts” in the Dungeon Master's Guide) called a Manifest Charm, which lasts until you use it. You don’t need to be in the Bastion while the rites are performed to <font></font>
gain their benefit. The effects of the Charm depend on the facility's linked plane: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Daanvi, the Perfect Order. You can expend the Charm (no action required) to end the Blinded, Deafened, Paralyzed, or Poisoned condition on yourself. <font></font>
<font></font>
Dolurrh, the Realm of the Dead. When you use a spell slot to cast a spell from the Necromancy school, you can expend the Charm (no action required) to treat the spell as if it were cast <font></font>
using a spell slot 1 level above the slot expended. <font></font>
<font></font>
Fernia, the Sea of Fire. When you use a spell slot to cast a spell that deals Fire damage, you can expend the Charm (no action required) to treat the spell as if it were cast using a spell slot <font></font>
1 level above the slot expended. <font></font>
<font></font>
Irian, the Eternal Dawn. When you use a spell slot to cast a spell that restores Hit Points or deals Radiant damage, you can expend the Charm (no action required) to treat the spell as if it <font></font>
were cast using a spell slot 1 level above the slot expended. <font></font>
<font></font>
Lamannia, the Twilight Forest. When you use a spell slot to cast a spell that conjures or summons a Beast, an Elemental, or a Fey (including Conjure Animals, Conjure Elemental, Conjure <font></font>
Fey, Conjure Minor Elementals, Conjure Woodland Beings, Find Steed, Summon Beast, Summon Elemental, or Summon Fey ), you can expend the Charm (no action required) to treat the <font></font>
spell as if it were cast using a spell slot 1 level above the slot expended. <font></font>
<font></font>
Mabar, the Endless Night. When you use a spell slot to cast a spell that animates or summons an Undead (including Animate Dead, Create Undead, or Summon Undead), you can expend <font></font>
the Charm (no action required) to treat the spell as if it were cast using a spell slot 1 level above the slot expended. <font></font>
<font></font>
Risia, the Plain of Ice. When you use a spell slot to cast a spell that deals Cold damage, you can expend the Charm (no action required) to treat the spell as if it were cast using a spell slot <font></font>
1 level above the slot expended. <font></font>
<font></font>
Shavarath, the Battleground. When you score a Critical Hit, you can expend the Charm (no action required) to deal an extra 10 damage of one of the types dealt by the attack. <font></font>
<font></font>
Syrania, the Azure Sky. You can expend the Charm as a Bonus Action to gain a Fly Speed equal to your Speed for 1 minute. <font></font>
<font></font>
Thelanis, the Faerie Court. When you cast a spell from the Enchantment or Illusion school, you can expend the Charm (no action required) to give one target of the spell Disadvantage on <font></font>
its saving throw against the spell. <font></font>
<font></font>
Xoriat, the Realm of Delirium. When you use a spell slot to cast a spell, you can expend the Charm (no action required) to roll on the Wild Magic Surge table in the Player’s Handbook. <font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
If your Bastion is mobile, its connection to the linked plane of existence is suspended while the Bastion moves, and you can't issue the Empower order to this facility while it is in transit. <font></font>
When the Bastion reaches a destination, you can issue the Empower order to establish a new planar connection, linking the Bastion either to the same plane of existence or to a different <font></font>
one you choose. Establishing the new connection takes 7 days, after which you can issue the Empower order normally. <font></font>

<font></font>
Museum <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Level 13 Bastion Facility <font></font>
Prerequisite: None <font></font>
Space: Roomy <font></font>
Hirelings: 1 <font></font>
<font></font>
Order: Research <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A Museum contains relics and treasures brought back from expeditions you participated in or sponsored, carefully cataloged and displayed. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Research: Specialized Lore. When you issue the Research order to this facility, you commission the hireling to study particular treasures in the museum. The work takes 7 days. The <font></font>
hireling gains knowledge regarding some question of ancient history as if they had cast the Legend Lore spell, then shares this knowledge with you the next time you speak with them. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Secrets of the Past. Your Museum contains one treasure that gives you a magical Charm (see “Supernatural Gifts” in the Dungeon Master's Guide) when you spend 7 days studying it and <font></font>
then finish a Long Rest in your Bastion. The Charm lasts for 7 days or until you use it. You can't gain another Charm from this facility while you still have one. The effect of the Charm <font></font>
depends on the origin of the treasure in your Museum. You can choose one of the following options (your DM might make more options available or limit the existing options based on your <font></font>
past expeditions): <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Charm of Argonnessen. While you have this Charm, you know Draconic. You can expend the Charm to cast Dragon's Breath (level 4 version), targeting yourself only. <font></font>
<font></font>
Charm of Ashtakala. While you have this Charm, you know Abyssal and Infernal. You can expend the Charm to cast Ray of Sickness (level 4 version). <font></font>
<font></font>
Charm of Dhakaan. While you have this Charm, you know the Goblin language. You can expend the Charm to cast Stagger ing Smite. <font></font>
<font></font>
Charm of Khyber. While you have this Charm, you have Darkvision with a range of 120 feet. You can expend the Charm to gain Truesight with a range of 60 feet for 1 minute. <font></font>
<font></font>
Charm of Xen‘drik. While you have this Charm, you know the Giant language. As a Bonus Action, you can expend the Charm to change your Strength score to 25 for 1 hour. <font></font>
<font></font>
Enlarging the Facility. You can enlarge your Museum to a Vast facility by spending 2,000 GP. If you do so, the Museum can have two treasures with different origins. A Vast Museum gains <font></font>
two additional hirelings, for a total of three. Assign names and personalities to them as you see fit. <font></font>

<font></font>
ARTIST: KUNO <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A BATTLE SMITH INQUISITIVE TRAILS HIS QUARRY <font></font>
THROUGH THE STREETS AND TOWERS OF SHARN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
HE INQUISITIVES OF SHARN ARE A DISTINCTIVE BREED OF ADVENTURERS: PART PRIVATE INVESTIGATOR, PART POLICE DETECTIVE, AND PART SPY. TO THEM, ADVENTURE <font></font>
means investigating crimes and making sure the criminals get their due. Inquisitives probe murders, find missing people, stop blackmailers, and break up smuggling rings. And they <font></font>
don't necessarily limit their investigations to such mundane problems. They might unmask a rakshasa posing as a city councilor, find a possessed ancient mask that forced a professor to <font></font>
<font></font>
commit grisly murders, or dispel the magic keeping a noble scion in comatose slumber. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 64/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Run a Sharn inquisitives campaign if you want to emulate the film noir adventures of detectives like Philip Marlowe, Sam Spade, Mike Hammer, and Dick Tracy in a fantastical environment. <font></font>
If you decide to run this kind of campaign, it’s a good idea to discuss with your players how serious you intend the game to be. There's a fine line between playing an archetype and <font></font>
parodying it; the “hard-boiled detective” noir archetype has been parodied so often that a campaign based on it can easily lean on the comedic side. Talk with your players early and often <font></font>
about the tone you're aiming for, as well as what they want from the campaign. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https://;www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 65/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
INQUISITIVE CHARACTERS t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A Sharn inquisitives campaign revolves around the idea of the characters as investigators who might work for an inquisitive agency at the start of the campaign but might run their own in <font></font>
time. In any case, the inquisitive agency functions as a patron to the adventuring party, becoming a source of both missions and rewards. You can use the group patron rules in Eberron: <font></font>
Rising from the Last War or Tasha’s Cauldron of Everything to give characters the benefits of this patronage. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Inquisitive background in chapter 2 is a good option for characters in this campaign, along with backgrounds from the Player's Handbook such as Charlatan, Criminal, Guard, and <font></font>
Wayfarer. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
INquISITIVE AGENCIES OF SHARN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Inquisitive agencies run the gamut from a handful of private investigators working together to networks of detectives supported by dragonmarked houses. If the characters want to <font></font>
establish their own inquisitive agency, they should consider two key factors: where to locate the agency, and whether to associate with an established guild. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: KUNO <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
INQUISITIVES IN SHARN KEEP CONSTANT WATCH OVER CRIMINAL ACTIVITY IN THE CITY, NO MATTER WHERE IT OCCURS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
AceNcy Location <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Depending on what levels of the City of Towers an inquisitive agency focuses on, its members might get involved in very different sorts of investigations. Lower Sharn means poor clients <font></font>
and lawless streets. Middle Sharn means merchant rivalries and better pay. Upper Sharn means uncovering the secrets hidden behind the veneer of respectability. See the “Sharn <font></font>
Neighborhoods” section later in this chapter for more details about the quarters where characters might establish their headquarters. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
FINDERS GUILD AND WARNING GUILD <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
best inquisitives in t <font></font>
Neighborhoods’ s <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
agency readily accepts walk <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
n Upper Dura). This agency's principal is Brina d’'Medan <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
settled in Sharn after running afoul of the Emer Claw on a mission in Ka <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Other member f the Warning Guild re ma ealthy clients referred t em <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Guitp AFFILIATION <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Whether or not any of the characters possess dragonmarks, their agency can choose to affiliate with either House Tharashk’s Finders Guild or House Medani’s Warning Guild. The choice <font></font>
can influence the kinds of cases the characters take and the attitude the Sharn Watch takes toward their activities. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A dragonmarked house's seal of approval, whether it comes from House Tharashk’s Finders Guild or House Medani’s Warning Guild, promises a certain standard of quality and professional <font></font>
ethics. However, that guarantee comes at a price: most guild-sponsored inquisitives charge at least 2 GP per day, plus expenses—and the use of magic can increase that cost significantly. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The inquisitives best at finding lost people and things belong to the Finders Guild of House Tharashk. Finders Guild agencies often end up as rivals to the Sharn Watch and can rarely count <font></font>
on the Watch for support. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Inquisitives of House Medani’s Warning Guild detect and disrupt the theft of goods and information from their clients. They often partner with law enforcement agencies across Khorvaire, <font></font>
deploying training in keen observation and logical deduction. Though the Sharn Watch calls on these agencies to consult in difficult cases, members of the Watch tend to find Medani mind <font></font>
games infuriating and their idealism naive. Medani inquisitives also serve as bodyguards and sentries, focusing on protection against imminent threats. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The characters’ agency need not affiliate with either guild; going independent is always an option. For the many people who can't afford the fees of the guild-sponsored agencies, <font></font>
independent inquisitives offer a cheaper alternative. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 66/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
Orner Deralts <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Players can use the information about inquisitive agencies in Eberron: Rising from the Last Warto flesh out more details about their agency, including allies, enemies, contacts, roles for <font></font>
each character in the agency, type of work, a signature case, and clientele. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 67/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
SHARN INQUISITIVES CONFLICTS t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can select the conflicts in a Sharn inquisitives campaign from the abundant criminal enterprises that dominate the seamy underworld of the City of Towers. While inquisitives deal with <font></font>
countless petty thugs and lone criminals, sooner or later their investigations will lead them to cross paths with the masterminds of crime in Sharn. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
As described in Eberron: Rising from the Last War, four criminal gangs are responsible for much of the illegal and shady activity in the City of Towers. This section focuses on two of them: <font></font>
The Boromar Clan and Daask. In addition, the sinister forces of the Dream Dark have infiltrated many places of power within the city. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can replace one or more of these threats with other groups from Eberron: Rising from the Last War—such as the Tyrants, House Tarkanan, the Aurum, or the Sharn Watch—or ones of <font></font>
your own devising. If the characters’ inquisitive agency aligns with a dragonmarked house whose operations include inquisitive work (House Tharashk’s Finders Guild or House Medani’s <font></font>
Warning Guild), you also can incorporate conflicts with rival houses, as described in chapter 5. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This section outlines the three conflicts suggested above, the goals of the villainous groups involved, and possible plot arcs tied to them. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Tue Boromar CLAN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Boromar Clan is the most powerful criminal organization in Sharn, with a reach that includes the city council, the Sharn Watch, House Jorasco, the elite social circles of Skyway, the <font></font>
shipping trade, and a wide array of illegal activity to boot—particularly extortion, gambling, smuggling, and thievery. The clan began as a family of halfling immigrants from the Talenta <font></font>
Plains, and its headquarters and key holdings lie in the Little Plains district of Middle Menthis, where many such migrants dwell. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: JORDAN KERBOW <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
THE CRIMINALS OF SHARN COME IN DIFFERENT SHAPES, IN DIFFERENT <font></font>
SIZES, AND FROM DIFFERENT PLANES OF EXISTENCE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
GOALS OF THE Boromar CLAN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
For generations, the goals of the Boromar Clan have been simple: to maintain an iron grip on shady and illegal activities in Sharn. The Boromars extend their influence into places of power <font></font>
and bribe officials of the Sharn Watch, all to maximize profits and power. Short-term goals come and go in service of these overarching objectives—blackmailing a councilor here, <font></font>
purchasing a shipping company there. But with the rise of Daask, the Boromars have the new and urgent goal of eliminating their upstart rival once and for all. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The fierce enmity between the Boromar Clan and Daask creates interesting options for inquisitive characters who stand (at least nominally) on the side of the law against both criminal <font></font>
organizations. Will they accept help from the Boromars against the much more obviously dangerous Daask? Or might they disregard Daask operations clearly meant to undermine the <font></font>
Boromars? The inquisitives might even find a way to ensure the conflict between the two criminal groups weakens them both, making Sharn safer in the process. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A Boromar Cian Arc <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The conflict between inquisitives and The Boromar Clan might follow this broad outline. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The characters investigate petty crimes—pickpocketing, burglary, blackmail, and such—and help bring several Boromar Clan members to justice. The inquisitives find the Sharn <font></font>
Watch unhelpful in dealing with these criminals, though, and some legitimate businesses and law-abiding citizens start shunning or insulting the characters, angry at their interference with <font></font>
the “hometown heroes” of the Boromar Clan. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
. Ostensibly trying to mend the poor relationship between the Sharn Watch and the inquisitives, a Watch officer recruits the characters to bring a Daask gang to justice. The <font></font>
Watch helps, and the characters catch several Daask criminals. However, the characters’ Watch allies don’t seem concerned about whether any Daask members are hurt or killed in the <font></font>
final confrontation. Indeed, it turns out that the Boromar Clan arranged the operation to remove a dangerous group of rivals. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
5. Boromar leaders try to recruit the characters to their side, offering exorbitant fees and extravagant gifts as payment for simple jobs. If bribery doesn’t work, the gang tries to <font></font>
coerce the characters into helping them, using friends, family members, or contacts as leverage. Along the way, the characters learn that a trusted NPC ally is firmly in the pocket of the <font></font>
Boromar Clan. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
g . Assuming the characters haven't joined the Boromars, the clan leadership tries to eliminate them. The Boromars can’t muster a physical threat to challenge characters of <font></font>
this level, so they wield their political power instead. Under pressure from Boromar leaders, the city council declares the adventurers a threat to Sharn’s safety and security. Officials revoke <font></font>
their inquisitive agency's operations permit and ask the characters to leave Sharn. <font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
Boromar OPERATIVES <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Boromar Clan began as a single family of halflings. Though its influence has expanded widely, its key operatives are still family members. Boromar operatives prefer deception and <font></font>
bribery over outright violence, though they defend their interests when necessary. Besides those described here, other Boromar operatives include bandits, commoners, spies, toughs, and <font></font>
warriors (see the Monster Manual). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Boromar Smuggler. Smugglers employed by the Boromar Clan (mostly halflings) are notoriously hard to catch. These speedy criminals traffic everything from narcotics to illegal arcane <font></font>
weaponry across Sharn. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Boromar Underboss. Underbosses supervise larger clan operations, such as gambling halls and warehouses of smuggled goods awaiting transport. Accustomed to the criminal lifestyle, <font></font>
these hardy individuals (mostly halflings) dismiss and dispose of threats with calculated ease. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Daask <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Daask is a violent gang of muggers, extortionists, looters, cultists, and drug dealers supported by such monsters as harpies, medusas, minotaurs, ogres, and trolls. It seems like a simple <font></font>
criminal gang engaged in a battle for territory and power against the Boromar Clan and the forces of law and order—but the truth is more complicated. In fact, the members of Daask take <font></font>
their orders from Sora Katra, one of the three hags (the Daughters of Sora Kell) who rule the nation of Droaam. Daask’s operations in Sharn are only a small part of a much larger scheme. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Goats or Daask <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The goals of Daask, like those of the Daughters of Sora Kell, are shrouded in mystery. As described in Eberron: Rising from the Last War, the reason for Daask’s existence depends on the <font></font>
path you choose for the Daughters of Sora Kell—whether they seek peace and stability for their young nation of mistrusted monsters, desire to end humanity’s dominance over Khorvaire <font></font>
and let monsters rule, or merely follow the visions of Sora Teraza wherever they lead. Regardless of Daask's larger purpose, however, one immediate goal remains: to eliminate the Boromar <font></font>
Clan, or at least break its hold on Sharn's criminal underworld. Its methods range from street violence—mugging, armed robbery, and even pillaging—to extortion and trade in drugs. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

A Daask Arc <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The conflict between inquisitives and Daask might follow this broad outline. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 1-4. The characters investigate crimes perpetrated by Daask against businesses they eventually discover are affiliated with the Boromar Clan. The Sharn Watch might hire the <font></font>
characters to help bring a Daask gang to justice, but the inquisitives eventually learn that the Boromar Clan seeks to use the Watch and the characters to strike back at Daask. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 5-10. What seems like a routine case of a wealthy noble disappearing into a drug den while looking for thrills leads the characters to dig into Daask’s trade in dragon's blood, a <font></font>
mysterious and dangerous new drug. The investigation drives the characters into conflict with increasingly powerful monsters affiliated with the gang. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 11-16. Daask operatives kidnap a prominent figure in Sharn, but the freed “victim” turns out to be a doppelganger. The characters are hired to retrieve the real victim, who is <font></font>
undergoing a ritual that will eventually transform them into a hag. <font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
the explosives before Sharn is thrown into utter chaos. <font></font>

<font></font>
Tue Dreamine Dark <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The sinister and mysterious Dreaming Dark—a metaphysical force trying to prolong the current age of despair and violence—seeks to extend its reach toward Khorvaire. Over a century ago, <font></font>
the Dream Dark compromised House Deneith’s enclave in Upper Tavick’s Landing. Although the enclave seems normal to outsiders, and even to many in House Deneith, it is now a <font></font>
stronghold of shadows. Under the tutelage of the Dreaming Dark, the Sharn Deneith have become masters of intrigue and subtlety. They carefully conceal their psionic power from <font></font>
outsiders while using it to gather information from the wealthy citizens they ostensibly guard. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
While the enclave serves the Dreaming Dark, most of its members have no idea they are in league with the Inspired. Over a century of indoctrination, the Inspired dominator Tirashana has <font></font>
taught the Sharn Deneith to see themselves as superior because of their dragonmarks and raised above the other dragonmarked houses by their psionic abilities. Even members of the <font></font>
house who work directly with the Inspired believe that the Inspired consider them equal partners, but in fact the Inspired view them merely as useful pawns. Now quori spirits have <font></font>
possessed fifteen members of the house. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
In addition to the leaders of House Deneith in Sharn, the Dreaming Dark has agents strategically planted throughout the City of Towers. They include one member of the Sharn Council <font></font>
(Bestan ir’Tonn, who also has close ties to the Boromar Clan) and a leader in the Sharn Watch (Warden Maira ir’Talan of the Blackened Book). The Dreaming Dark also commands the <font></font>
allegiance of the Riedran ambassadors in Sharn. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

Goats oF THE DREAMING DARK <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Building from the description in Eberron: Rising from the Last War, the Dream Dark has three objectives in the City of Towers: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
To cause chaos and strife by undermining what little faith in law enforcement exists in Sharn—turning the forces of law and order against one another, fueling the feuds between criminal <font></font>
organizations, and spreading fear among the populace. <font></font>
<font></font>
To promote their chosen champions, which in Sharn means the subverted and possessed agents of House Deneith. <font></font>
<font></font>
To undermine the kalashtar throughout the city—especially in the Overlook district of Upper Dura and its Path of Light temple. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A Dreamine Dark Arc <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Dream Dark poses a significant threat, which might not manifest fully until the characters can handle the danger. The conflict could play out according to this outline. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 1-4, Early in their adventures, the inquisitives have vivid dreams that reveal key details about cases they're working on—easily interpreted as subconscious insights bringing <font></font>
overlooked information to light. (The discussion of the Dreaming Dark in Eberron: Rising from the Last War includes suggestions for incorporating dreams into your game.) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 5-10. Investigating a series of grisly crimes, the characters are aided by a Sentinel Marshal of House Deneith (see stat block in chapter 5). When captured, the criminal insists that <font></font>
recurring dreams led to the crimes. The Sentinel Marshal dismisses this claim. If the inquisitives investigate further, they find themselves drawn into a nightmare scenario involving a quori. <font></font>
In the course of that adventure, they glean clues that the Sentinel Marshal played a more sinister part in events than previously suspected. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 11-16. The characters experience increasingly frequent nightmares, individually and as a group. Meanwhile, the Sentinel Marshals and other members of House Deneith interfere <font></font>
with their investigations. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 17-20. The inquisitives uncover the corruption at the heart of the House Deneith enclave in Sharn, which reaches all the way to its lord, Sadran d’Deneith—a human man possessed <font></font>
by a powerful quori spirit. Their investigations lead to confrontations with Deneith leaders in the waking world, but eventually the inquisitives must fight the quori in the dream world of Dal <font></font>
Quor. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

DREAMING Dark AGENTS <font></font>

stat block. The two agents presented here are Inspired agents of the Dark who wield terrifying psionic power. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
prowess against those enemies. <font></font>
<font></font>

Dreaming Dark Mindkiller. Mindkillers are the assassins of the Dreaming Dark, sent to terrify their foes before striking to kill. Mindkillers force foes to attack their own allies, causing <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
physical and emotional devastation to grow the power of the Dreaming Dark. <font></font>

11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
SHARN NEIGHBORHOODS t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Sharn is divided into geographical quarters—Central Plateau, Dura, Menthis Plateau, Northedge, and Tavick’s Landing—and each quarter has three levels. The combination of quarter and <font></font>
level defines a ward: Upper Dura, Middle Central, Lower Northedge, and so on. Each ward comprises several districts or neighborhoods, each with its own distinctive flavor. A few areas lie <font></font>
outside Sharn’s main geographical quarters, including Skyway, Cliffside, the Cogs, and the City of the Dead. (See Eberron: Rising from the Last War for more details.) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Every neighborhood has its own flavor of crime. This section offers suggestions for mysteries, intrigue, and adventure across Sharn’s wards. It also includes brief descriptions of inquisitive <font></font>
agencies connected to House Tharashk’s Finders Guild. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: KUNO <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
SOMETHING IS ALWAYS HAPPENING IN THE SHADOWS OF THE CITY OF TOWERS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Upper CENTRAL <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Upper Central is defined by wealth and prestige and heavily patrolled by both the Sharn Watch and private security agents, so crime there leans more toward elaborate heists than back- <font></font>
alley muggings. Lucrative targets such as the Kundarak Bank of Sharn (with its supposedly impenetrable storage facility called the Vaults) and Aurora Gallery (a magic item auction house) <font></font>
remain far beyond the reach of ordinary bank robbers and safecrackers, but they draw the attention of brilliant schemers who concoct elaborate plots, often with help from the inside. When <font></font>
something goes missing, whether from House Kundarak or from any of the ward's mansions and luxury shops, the victims call on the very best inquisitives to retrieve the stolen goods. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
One opportunity for less renowned inquisitives is the chance to bring Sharn’s elite to justice for a type of immoral behavior that remains fully legal: plundering the poor. Residents of the <font></font>
lower wards have little hope of securing redress for wrongs done by exploitative nobles, corner-cutting businesses, and government bureaucracies—unless persistent and skillful <font></font>
inquisitives provide them with leverage. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Mippte CENTRAL <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
International intrigue is the most common form of criminal activity in Middle Central, which includes Ambassador Towers (with its embassies and consulates from across the world), the <font></font>
headquarters of the King’s Citadel, and the primary enclaves of the dragonmarked houses. Spies and other shady characters remain alert for loose lips, unguarded communications, and <font></font>
opportunities to blackmail diplomats and dragonmarked heirs. The victims of these crimes sometimes hire less well-known inquisitives, who can make their problems go away without <font></font>
attracting attention. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Though Middle Central doesn't enjoy the wealth of the upper ward, it has its share of lucrative targets for elaborate heists, including the Brelish Museum of Fine Art (in Ambassador <font></font>
Towers) and the well-furnished temples in Sovereign Towers. Dragon Towers is the home of Thora Tavin and the headquarters of her criminal gang known as House Tarkanan. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
GLOBE INFORMATION AGENCY <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Kava Velderan, principal investigator at the Globe Information Agency, prides herself on her ability to find anyone, anywhere. A human dragonmarked heir of House Tharashk, Kava eschews <font></font>
the “d’” prefix she’s entitled to use before her surname, as she values her clan more than house traditions. Her agency is located in the Dragon Towers district. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lower CENTRAL <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lower Central seems to exist in blissful ignorance of the power struggles raging above it, and it’s a largely peaceful place. One exception is a street gang called the Broken Mirror, active in <font></font>
the residential district of Vallia Towers. The members of this gang often target people with violent attacks meant to disfigure their faces. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lower Central attracts nonconformists, artists, and radicals. Most of these folk hold nonviolent ideals that contribute to the ward’s peacefulness, but at least one cult devoted to a radiant <font></font>
idol (described in Eberron: Rising from the Last War) meets in the inn district called Boldrei’s Hearth. The radiant idol at the heart of this cult seduces its followers into acts of robbery, <font></font>
kidnapping, and even ritual murder to prove their loyalty. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
City oF THE Deap <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The necropolis in the cliffs north of Sharn, called the City of the Dead, feels peaceful because most of its residents are dead. But some of the dead are restless, and some of the living have <font></font>
an unholy fascination with the dead. Grave robbing is an issue here but rarely amounts to more than adolescent pranks done on a dare. More serious issues arise when necromancers <font></font>
create Undead servants, when restless spirits curse intruders, or when Gath—the Lich-Priest of the Keeper—conducts evil rites that introduce plague into Sharn. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
CLIFFSIDE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Despite the Sharn Watch’s presence in Cliffside, this rough-and-tumble dockside district remains one of the city’s most crime-riddled areas. While legitimate businesses of all stripes serve <font></font>
the needs of transient sailors, less legitimate groups and individuals seek to profit off those sailors by theft. Kidnapping is common here as well; some ship's captains willingly ransom their <font></font>
crew members, while other captains regularly ransom sailors that no one else wants in exchange for a term of service. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Tue Coas <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Cogs includes the industrial districts of Ashblack and Blackbones and the tenements of Khyber's Gate. It lies just above the city’s lawless expanse of sewers and the ancient ruins of <font></font>
Old Sharn. Most residents of Sharn, however, make little distinction between the various realms that lie deep below the city and just call it all “the Cogs.” The Sharn Watch maintains a <font></font>
presence in water purification plants in the sewers as well as elsewhere in the Cogs, but street gangs run rampant nevertheless—sometimes wreaking havoc, sometimes maintaining their <font></font>
own semblance of order and harsh law. The Quiet Folk, a group of thieving goblins, operates in the sewers below Tavick’s Landing, while the Red Jackals, a more violent gang of shifters <font></font>
and orcs, fiercely defends its territory below Menthis Plateau. Daask also keeps its headquarters in Khyber's Gate. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 75/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
In the depths of the Cogs, inquisitive work can blend seamlessly into dungeon-delving adventure. While investigating strange occurrences among the refugees from Cyre in High Walls (in <font></font>
Lower Tavick’s Landing), characters might find clues that draw them down to the Citadel of the Closed Circle in Khyber’s Gate, where a mind flayer explores the relics left by an ancient <font></font>
group of wizards devoted to the study of the daelkyr. Or inquisitives investigating the corruption of the Cathedral of the Cleansing Flame in Sovereign Towers (in Middle Central) might <font></font>
discover the archierophant's ties to a rakshasa dwelling in Khyber’s Gate. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Upper Dura <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Upper Dura, though quite different from the seedy and poverty-stricken wards below it, retains some of the rough edges of those neighborhoods. Adventurers bound for Xen’drik <font></font>
expeditions congregate in Upper Dura, and many businesses cater to their particular needs. A large cohort of the Sharn Watch patrols the Daggerwatch district, and the Citadel of Light (a <font></font>
temple to Dol Arrah) remains a beacon of hope in the city. Upper Dura is also the home of Sharn’s largest kalashtar population (in the Overlook district), making it useful to inquisitives <font></font>
working against the Dream Dark. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A street gang called the Brokenbridge Brawlers has become a nuisance in Upper Dura. Made up of Brelish veterans who frequent the Gold &amp; White tavern in Daggerwatch, these ruffians <font></font>
harass, rob, and sometimes assault Cyrans and former soldiers from other nations. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Mipove Dura <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Middle Dura might be safer than the districts below it, but that’s not saying much. A scent of danger lingers in the air of this ward. The Bazaar, Sharn’s largest commercial district, has the <font></font>
highest concentration of illegal activity too—pickpockets, bullies, con artists, and even illicit services and trades (many controlled by the Boromar Clan). A street gang called the Little <font></font>
Fingers, made up mostly of impoverished children but led by halflings with ties to the Boromars, picks pockets in and around the Bazaar. Pawnshops in the Bazaar are also known to fence <font></font>
stolen goods. The Underlook district, with its dingy inns and restaurants, makes an excellent place to hire inquisitives, including members of House Tharashk’s Information Acquisition <font></font>
agency and many unaffiliated investigators. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
INFORMATION ACQUISITION <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Kalaash’arrna, a cunning orc man with the Mark of Finding, runs Information Acquisition, an inquisitive agency affiliated with the Finders Guild, in the district of Underlook. Information <font></font>
Acquisition (sometimes abbreviated to “InAc”) specializes in shadowing people, unearthing evidence of illegal or immoral behavior, and learning secrets that others would prefer to keep <font></font>
hidden. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lower Dura <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Once the heart of Sharn, Lower Dura has decayed as the rest of the city has risen higher into the sky. Now it is a dark and dangerous place, where Daask and the Boromar Clan clash <font></font>
regularly, a gang of bugbears runs a protection racket, and street violence remains an everyday occurrence. House Tarkanan and the Tyrants have holdings in Callestan and other districts <font></font>
of Lower Dura. Smugglers hide their goods in the warehouses of Precarious and the Stores, and the district of Fallen is a haunted ruin right in the midst of the city. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Upper MENTHIS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Upper Menthis is strongly flavored by the presence of Morgrave University (see chapter 6). Academics, sages, scribes, and students come to the school from across Khorvaire and settle in <font></font>
the ward's residential districts, lvy Towers or (if they made their fortune in Xen’drik) Platinate. Given the nature of Morgrave University, when precious artifacts go missing from the college's <font></font>
museums or storage, it’s hard to say whether to blame art thieves or unscrupulous faculty members. Six rival antiquities dealers operate in a single tower in the Seventh Tower district, and <font></font>
at least one of them is suspected of hiring burglars to steal goods from rivals. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Mippte MeNTHIS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Middle Menthis, the most diverse ward in Sharn, boasts a vibrant mix of immigrant cultures from across Khorvaire. The halfling community of Little Plains is the birthplace and <font></font>
headquarters of the Boromar Clan, which most district residents hold in high esteem as protectors and “hometown heroes.” The Cyran refugees of Smoky Towers have a reputation for <font></font>
activism and protest; the Sharn Watch keeps a close eye on them. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A gang of changeling pickpockets called the Five Faces operates primarily in Middle Menthis, preying indiscriminately on orcs in Cassan Bridge, halflings in Little Plains, Cyrans in Smoky <font></font>
Towers, Lhazaar immigrants in Warden Towers, and Morgrave students commuting from cheap residences in Lower Menthis to their classes high above. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
THURANNE D’VELDERAN’S INVESTIGATIVE SERVICES <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Thuranne d'Velderan, a human investigator, operates an inquisitive agency in the Warden Towers district as part of her house's Finders Guild. Her greatest asset is an extensive network of <font></font>
contacts within the Watch garrison that keeps order throughout the entire Menthis Plateau. Thuranne specializes in criminal investigation and frequently consults for the Watch to repay <font></font>
the many favors her contacts have done for her. Other members of House Tharashk don't like her, as she prefers running her own business to taking on jobs for them. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lower MeNTHIS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Many Sharn folk love the inexpensive entertainment available in Lower Menthis. The ward's streets stay crowded night and day, its buildings huddle close together, and its lurid offerings <font></font>
nurture criminal activity. The alleys of Torchfire are notoriously dangerous, especially for intoxicated revelers. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Burning Ring, an illegal operation that puts on gladiatorial combats at shifting locations throughout Lower Menthis (most often in the Firelight district), has ties to Daask. But betting on <font></font>
the Burning Ring's fights is big business for many of Sharn’s major criminal organizations, and fight rigging is rampant. Gangs also commonly use fights as a cover for assassinations. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Upper NorTHEDGE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Upper Northedge is a quiet, largely residential ward with little overt crime. Some of Sharn’s wealthiest citizens call it home; many of them have dedicated their lives to increasing their <font></font>
personal power at any cost. Inquisitives might unearth the skeletons in the closets of such individuals—the corpses of those trampled on the road to riches. Or inquisitives might take <font></font>
lucrative but mind-numbing jobs following unfaithful spouses, reining in or bailing out spendthrift heirs, and dealing with similar problems of the rich. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
MippLe NorTHEDGE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A peaceful residential ward inhabited by hardworking folk, Middle Northedge boasts clean and quiet streets—it hides its crime behind the closed doors of well-to-do homes. The seemingly <font></font>
respectable citizens of this comfortable ward might include a factory overseer who embezzles funds from an employer in the Cogs; a serial killer who targets actors in Lower Menthis <font></font>
during sprees out on the town; and a cultist of the Dragon Below who worships the daelkyr Belashyrra, serves a hidden beholder, and hides a tattooed eye under a hat. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lower NorTHEDGE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lower Northedge is home to the hardworking laborers of Sharn—generally decent folk who believe in an honest day's work and can afford to stay out of Lower Dura. Crime is less rampant <font></font>
here than in other lower wards, and residents look out for each other. That close-knit sense of community can make it hard for inquisitives to learn anything in Lower Northedge. It <font></font>
sometimes also fosters insular cults dedicated to the Dragon Below or a radiant idol (described in Eberron: Rising from the Last War). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 76/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
SKYWAY <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The residents of Skyway are the ludicrously wealthy and privileged: aristocrats whose feet literally never touch the ground and who believe that no crime could ever trouble them in their <font></font>
lofty estates. Indeed, though such luxurious homes can tempt thieves, burglaries remain rare—but are all the more notable when they do occur. What's more, those complacent aristocrats <font></font>
might not realize their neighbors include some of the most powerful criminal masterminds in Sharn, who used their ill-gotten wealth to purchase estates seized from foreign owners during <font></font>
the Last War. The Tain Gala, held every month in the ir’Tain family mansion, always provides a hotbed of intrigue. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
UPPER Tavick’s LANDING <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
With its restrictive laws and aristocratic pride, Upper Tavick’s Landing is openly hostile not just to street crime but to any display of violence, loud noise, and even rudeness—not to mention <font></font>
poverty and dirt. And yet, some of the most insidious evil lurks behind the facade of nobility in this ward. House Deneith has a fortified enclave in the Copper Arch district, where elite <font></font>
soldiers hone the psionic abilities they learn from their quori masters in the Dream Dark. A family of Karrnathi vampires inhabits an ancient manor in the opulent Ocean View district, <font></font>
serving the Order of the Emerald Claw as well as their own sanguine appetites. And members of the Aurum here pursue wealth at all costs. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Mipbte Tavick’s LANDING <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A crossroads for travelers, traders, and explorers, Middle Tavick’s Landing also hosts smugglers, fugitives, and exiles. The rowdy adventurers of the Deathsgate Guild start fights on the <font></font>
streets, criminal organizations control betting on the sporting events in Cornerstone, and followers of the Blood of Vol among Graywall’s Karrnathi families aid and abet spies of the <font></font>
Emerald Claw. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Karr’ AASHTA’S INVESTIGATIONS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Karr’Aashta, a human investigator affiliated with the Finders Guild, runs a small agency in the Deathsgate district whose inquisitives can find out just about anything a client wants to know. <font></font>
Karr’Aashta takes on cases that other Finders Guild inquisitives won't touch; he specializes in jobs involving the Cogs far below the towers of Sharn. This willingness to venture into the <font></font>
depths of the earth might reflect Karr’'Aashta’s affiliation with the Cults of the Dragon Below. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lower Tavick’s LANDING <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Anyone who comes to Sharn by land enters through Lower Tavick’s Landing—and most travel on to the upper wards as quickly as possible. This rough ward caters to workers from the <font></font>
Cogs, teamsters, and other tough folk. The Cogsgate district holds warehouses owned by the Boromar Clan and used for smuggling and other illicit activity. The Dragoneyes district, home <font></font>
to a large population of changelings, is the base of operations for the Tyrants. And the High Walls district, used to intern suspicious travelers and foreigners during the Last War, now <font></font>
houses many Cyran refugees. Among the refugees, some have turned to the nihilistic worship of the Dragon Below or radiant idols. Others plot how and where they might reestablish their <font></font>
fallen nation. All the while, a street gang called the Mourners—ostensibly vigilantes who protect their fellow Cyrans from harassment—engage in their own fair share of bullying and <font></font>
extortion. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
INauIsiTIVe ADVENTURE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This section presents a sample adventure in the same format as those in the Dungeon Master's Guide—except that it features the opening situation as a sensational extract from a <font></font>
broadsheet. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 77/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
THE ENEMY OF MY ENEMY t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
An adventure for Level 4 characters <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
THE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
SHARN INQUISITIVE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
GOLD DRAGON BURNS! <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
THE PEACEFUL GOLD DRAGON INN NESTLED IN THE LOWER TOWERS OF TERMINUS <font></font>
IS ABLAZE, AND VICIOUS MARAUDERS ARE ATTACKING PATRONS FLEAING THE <font></font>
BURNING BUILDING! <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ill the Sharn Watch investigate this vicious assault not just upon our city, but upon the <font></font>
<font></font>
dragonmarked houses themselves? Or will they continue to ignore the crime running <font></font>
<font></font>
rampant in our lower wards? Or, as too often seems to happen these days, will some dis- <font></font>
reputable inquisitives, working for a private agency, accomplish more than the Watch does in a <font></font>
month, raising the question once again of why our tax money is being spent on such an ineffectual <font></font>
organization? <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Hook: The Gold Dragon Inn might be a favored hangout of the inquisitive characters, if you want to begin the adventure with the action of the fire. Alternatively, you can start with the <font></font>
halfling innkeeper, Irida d’Ghallanda (Small, Lawful Evil Spy) hiring the characters to investigate the situation. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ENCOUNTERS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The adventure consists of these encounters. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Fire Inside. Daask acquired two Hell Hounds and got them inside the inn to start the fire and terrorize the folks inside. If the characters concentrate on fighting the monsters, the other <font></font>
people inside the inn can extinguish the flames. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Panic Outside. Outside the burning inn, eight Daask Raiders (gnolls whose stat blocks appear in this chapter) are attacking terrified people fleeing from the fire as well as those trying to <font></font>
help put it out. Each gnoll tries to flee when Bloodied. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
No Great Mystery. |\dentifying the gnolls as agents of Daask isn't difficult—it’s the conclusion most people jump to when they see gnolls involved in violence, and a number of clues back up <font></font>
that assumption: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Interrogating the Gnolls. If any of the gnolls are captured alive (or questioned via Speak with Dead), they freely admit their involvement with Daask and growl slogans about throwing off <font></font>
the yoke of “the manged”—a term they use to describe furless Humanoids. <font></font>
<font></font>
Boromar Witness. A halfling named Gressie Gorben (Boromar Smuggler; this stat block appears in this chapter)—was attacked by a gnoll that called her a “filthy Boromar.” Gressie denies <font></font>
any ties to the Boromar Clan, but she admits to knowing that Daask and the Boromar Clan are involved in a long-running feud. <font></font>
<font></font>
Freight Inquiries. Inquiring with freight companies in Lower Tavick’s Landing reveals that a pair of gnolls brought a massive iron crate, large enough to hold the hell hounds, into the city <font></font>
from Graywall in Droaam the day before the incident. The gnolls’ papers gave their residence as a tenement in Malleon’s Gate, a district where Daask is very active. <font></font>
<font></font>
Boromar Connection. In addition to Gressie Gorben’s testimony, several clues point to a connection between the Gold Dragon Inn and the Boromar Clan: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Irida d’Ghallanda. The innkeeper, who hired the characters to investigate the attack, denies any involvement with the Boromar Clan. However, widespread rumors claim that she is a <font></font>
frequent guest of Bestan ir’Tonn, Upper Tavick Landing’s representative on the Sharn Council—who is a known ally of the Boromar Clan. <font></font>
<font></font>
Business Records. Prying deeper into the inn’s business reveals that it receives many deliveries from a warehouse in the nearby district of Cogsgate—a warehouse owned by the Boromar <font></font>
Clan. <font></font>
<font></font>
Contraband. A careful search of the inn (without Irida’s knowledge) reveals that it offers contraband items to its guests, including Aundairian wine and discreet deliveries of the dreamlily <font></font>
drug. <font></font>
<font></font>
Watch Involvement. The Sharn Watch seems unusually eager to cooperate with the inquisitives, as long as the characters’ attention is focused on Daask and not on any connection <font></font>
between the inn and the Boromar Clan. If they ask about Boromar ties, their Watch contacts urge them to focus on Daask. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Daask Hideout. The characters’ inquiries about Daask lead them to a hideout where some gang members await their next instructions. They include an Ogre, two Harpies, and four Daask <font></font>
Raiders (they include any survivors from the inn incident). Use the eastern half of the surface level portion of the Dungeon Hideout map in the Dungeon Master's Guide for this hideout. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Reward. If the characters bring these Daask members to justice for their crimes, Irida d’Ghallanda pays them 500 GP (total) and politely requests that they end their investigation. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
DRAGONMARKED INTRIGUE T <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
MEMBERS OF DIFFERENT DRAGONMARKED HOUSES SET ASIDE THEIR <font></font>
DIFFERENCES TO INVESTIGATE A MURDER IN SHARN, THE CITY OF TOWERS. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
HE PROSPERITY OF KHORVAIRE IS BUILT ON MAGIC, WITH THE DRAGONMARKED HOUSES AS THE MORTAR THAT HOLDS THIS MAGICAL ECONOMY TOGETHER. THE <font></font>
cornerstones of Khorvaire’s economy—communications, transport, banking, animal husbandry, security, and more—fall under the ownership and control of the dragonmarked houses. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
For most of Khorvaire’s history, the authority of a strong central monarchy in the kingdom of Galifar balanced the power of these houses. The Last War upset that balance, and the <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
squabbling leaders of Khorvaire's many nations can hardly check these houses’ grasping reach. Many whisper that if the nations of Khorvaire ever united again, they would be ruled not by a <font></font>
king, but by the heir of a dragonmarked house. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https://;www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 79/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Run a dragonmarked intrigue campaign if you want to emulate the schemes and power struggles of the Renaissance—merchant dynasties growing to rival royal families in influence—or <font></font>
stories of family power dynamics like The Sopranos or Succession. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
