<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
DRAGONMARKED CHARACTERS t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A dragonmarked intrigue campaign assumes that the characters all have some connection to a dragonmarked house. They might all connect to the same house, or they could associate <font></font>
with different houses but have a common purpose. (See the “Building a Party” section below for suggestions on bringing together a party from different houses.) If the characters work for <font></font>
a single house or an organization, like the Twelve, that unites them in a mutual purpose, use the group patron rules in Eberron: Rising from the Last War to provide benefits from their <font></font>
shared mission. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The House Agent and various house heir backgrounds in chapter 2 suit characters in this campaign, giving them benefits and capabilities related to their house affiliation. Certain other <font></font>
backgrounds can reflect the mercantile specialties of the characters’ houses, such as Artisan for House Cannith, Sailor for House Lyrandar, Entertainer for House Phiarlan or House <font></font>
Thuranni, or Scribe for House Sivis. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
DRAGONMARKED House RENOWN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can use the renown rules in the Dungeon Master's Guide to measure a character's influence and standing within a dragonmarked house. If your campaign focuses on the houses and <font></font>
the characters join one, track each character’s Renown Score with their house. A character's background and feats can increase their Renown Scores: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House Agent. The House Agent background grants a starting Renown Score of 3 with a character’s house. <font></font>
<font></font>
House Heir. A house heir background (such as House Cannith Heir) grants a starting Renown Score of 5 with a character's house. <font></font>
<font></font>
Dragonmark Feat. Acquiring a Dragonmark feat (except the Aberrant Dragonmark feat) after level 1 increases a character's Renown Score with the associated house by 1. <font></font>
<font></font>
Greater Dragonmark. Acquiring the greater dragonmark feat associated with a house (such as Greater Mark of Making) increases a character's Renown Score with that house by 2. <font></font>
Siberys Dragonmark. Acquiring the Boon of Siberys feat, or another feat in the Epic Boon category that represents a Siberys dragonmark, increases a character's Renown Score with the <font></font>
associated house by 3. <font></font>
<font></font>
Aberrant Dragonmark. Acquiring any aberrant dragonmark reduces a character's Renown Score with each dragonmarked house by 2 when that house's leadership learns of the mark. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
RENOWN PERKS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Characters who gain renown with a dragonmarked house are eligible for the following perks, allowing them to call in favors from members of that house. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
FavoreD IN House <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Prerequisite: Renown 3+ with a Dragonmarked House <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can request a favor from your house, which can take one of the following forms: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A dragonmarked member of the house casts for you a cantrip or level 1 spell associated with the house's mark (see the Dragonmark feats in chapter 2) at no cost. <font></font>
<font></font>
A member of the house casts for you a different spell of level 3 or lower at half the usual cost. <font></font>
<font></font>
The house provides a service or item associated with its business at reduced or no cost. See suggestions in each house’s description later in this chapter. <font></font>
<font></font>
You can request such a favor a number of times equal to your Renown Score. If your Renown Score falls below the total number of favors you have requested, a higher-ranking member of <font></font>
the house might call in a favor from you, sending you on a mission for house or personal business. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
In addition, your house pays you a stipend that supports your Modest lifestyle (1 GP per day). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
RECOGNIZED IN House <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Prerequisite: Renown 10+ with a Dragonmarked House <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you request a favor from your house (as decribed under “Favored in House”), it can take one of the following additional forms: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A dragonmarked member of the house casts for you a level 2 spell associated with the house’s mark at no cost. <font></font>
<font></font>
A member of the house casts for you a different spell of level 4 or lower at half the usual cost. <font></font>
<font></font>
Whenever you take a Bastion turn, you can issue an order to your house as if it were a special facility in your Bastion. Your house determines the type of special facility available to you, as <font></font>
noted in each house's description later in this chapter. <font></font>
<font></font>
In addition, your house stipend increases to support your Comfortable lifestyle (2 GP per day). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
CELEBRATED IN House <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Prerequisite: Renown 25+ with a Dragonmarked House <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you request a favor from your house (as decribed under “Favored in House”), it can take one of the following additional forms: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A dragonmarked member of the house casts for you a level 3 or 4 spell associated with the house's mark at no cost. <font></font>
<font></font>
A member of the house casts for you a different spell of level 6 or lower at half the usual cost. <font></font>
<font></font>
You gain a bodyguard for the duration of your next mission for the house (maximum of 1 week). The bodyguard—a creature with a Challenge Rating no higher than half your level (round up) <font></font>
—is appropriate to your house. <font></font>
<font></font>
In addition, your house stipend increases to support your Wealthy lifestyle (4 GP per day). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
PREEMINENT IN House <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Prerequisite: Renown 50+ with a Dragonmarked House <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you request a favor from your house (as decribed under “Favored in House”), it can take one of the following additional forms: <font></font>
A dragonmarked member of the house casts for you a level 5 spell associated with the house’s mark at no cost. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A member of the house casts for you a different spell of level 8 or lower at half the usual cost. <font></font>
In addition, your house stipend increases to support your Aristocratic lifestyle (10 GP per day). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https://;www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 81/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
BUILDING A Party <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can assemble a diverse party of D&amp;D characters drawn from a single dragonmarked house. Conversely, your party can include members of different houses united by alliances or <font></font>
common principles. Or they could be childhood friends who ended up working for different houses, or just a haphazard collection of individuals thrown together by unforeseen <font></font>
circumstances. The Party Makeup table suggests ways to justify why the characters are cooperating. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Party Makeup <font></font>
1d8 Composition <font></font>
1 One-House Party. Choose a dragonmarked house and connect all characters to it. <font></font>
2 Bodyguards. A Deneith Fighter, a Jorasco Cleric, a Medani Wizard, and a Phiarlan Monk are assigned to protect important people. <font></font>
3 Bounty Hunters. A Sivis Bard, a Tharashk Barbarian, a Tharashk Ranger, and a Vadalis Druid venture into the wild to pursue fugitives. <font></font>
4 Dragonshard Prospectors. House Tharashk leads a dragonshard-mining expedition, sending a Druid and a Ranger accompanied by a Lyrandar Sorcerer and a Thuranni Bard. <font></font>
5 Lawkeepers. A Deneith Fighter, a Deneith Paladin, a Lyrandar Sorcerer, and a Medani Rogue enforce the law—particularly the terms of the Treaty of Thronehold, which governs <font></font>
international law. <font></font>
6 Medical Extraction Team. A Cannith Artificer, a Deneith Paladin, a Jorasco Cleric, and a Thuranni Monk venture into war zones and disaster areas to protect civilians, heal them, <font></font>
and escort them away from danger. <font></font>
Z Researchers. A Cannith Artificer, a Deneith Paladin, a Medani Rogue (Arcane Trickster), and a Sivis Wizard carry out arcane research for the Twelve. <font></font>
8 Strike Force. A Cannith Artificer, a Deneith Fighter, a Jorasco Cleric, and a Thuranni Rogue strike hard and fast against enemies of the dragonmarked houses or the Five Nations <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
as a whole. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Although conflicts among the dragonmarked houses drive much of the action in this campaign, it's important not to let that tension cause too much friction in a party of adventurers. The <font></font>
D&amp;D game relies on cooperation among the players, so it’s helpful for the player characters to find common ground that unites them despite their differences in house affiliation and <font></font>
agenda. Many house members have friends and acquaintances among other houses. Those positive associations can bind an adventuring partytogether. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 82/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
DRAGONMARKED CONFLICTS t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A dragonmarked intrigue campaign might feature three significant campaign conflicts tied to the nature of dragonmarks: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Rival Houses. The most significant and persistent conflicts take place among the dragonmarked houses—longstanding rivalries, short-term disputes, and family grudges can define the <font></font>
“intrigue” part of this campaign. Significant rivalries also exist within some houses. <font></font>
<font></font>
House Tarkanan. Though it appears as nothing more than an ordinary criminal organization, House Tarkanan—which is made up of people who carry aberrant dragonmarks—can threaten <font></font>
all dragonmarked characters and the continued existence of their houses. <font></font>
<font></font>
The Chamber. As dragonmarks are thought to be an expression of the Draconic Prophecy, the Chamber—a secretive group of dragons interested in studying and manipulating the Prophecy <font></font>
—could easily become involved in interhouse intrigue. <font></font>
<font></font>
You can replace one or more of these conflicts with others fromEberron: Rising from the Last War or ones of your own devising. For example, if your campaign involves the issues between <font></font>
House Deneith and House Tharashk over the latter's expansion into the mercenary trade, the powers of Droaam could drive a significant conflict. You might emphasize the rivalry between <font></font>
the economic strength of the dragonmarked houses and the waning political power of the nations of Khorvaire, with one or more political leaders seeking to curtail the houses, or an <font></font>
ambitious dragonmarked leader working to supplant a nation’s sovereign. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This section describes the three suggested conflicts, the goals of the villainous groups involved, and possible plot arcs tied to them. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: MICHAL IVAN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Rivat Houses <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Conflicts, rivalries, and feuds among the thirteen dragonmarked houses drive a dragonmarked intrigue campaign. As tremendously powerful magical and mercantile forces, the houses <font></font>
seek economic dominance, which often comes at the expense of other houses and sometimes with little concern for ethics. Ambitious forces within the houses—if not the houses as a <font></font>
whole—can lead them to endanger both people and property. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Goats oF Rival Houses <font></font>
Rivalries among the dragonmarked houses are described below. <font></font>
Cannith and Tharashk. In its interactions with the other houses, House Cannith has a reputation for being domineering and openly advocating for a position of privilege. No one resents <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
that attitude more than House Tharashk. These two houses clash not over competing economic interests but to gain political influence within the Twelve and over Khorvaire as a whole. To <font></font>
complicate matters, Cannith relies on the dragonshards mined by Tharashk—and Tharashk doesn't want to lose its best customer. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Cannith and Vadalis. Though they have much in common as producers of goods, House Cannith and House Vadalis remain philosophically, temperamentally, and morally opposed. <font></font>
Members of House Vadalis distrust the city folk of House Cannith, whose machines (including the warforged) threaten to replace magebred animals entirely. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Deneith and Medani. House Deneith and House Medani both offer personal protection services. Extremely wealthy individuals have been known to hire bodyguards from both houses, but <font></font>
even then, house agents often compete to prove their services the more valuable. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Deneith and Tharashk. House Tharashk has been encroaching on House Deneith’s economic territory by recruiting the monstrous residents of Droaam to serve as mercenaries. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Ghallanda and Everyone. Many people underestimate the subtle influence of House Ghallanda. The House of Hospitality has spent centuries building connections in all the cities of the <font></font>
Five Nations. A Ghallanda concierge knows ways to get whatever a client needs. But the house also knows about the other houses’ rivalries and schemes. It tugs on these strings more <font></font>
than other houses realize. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Kundarak and Thuranni. The enmity between House Kundarak and House Thuranni dates back to the Shadow Schism. Elar d’Thuranni claimed that the Paelion line of House Phiarlan were <font></font>
traitors engaged in a diabolical plot, and the Thuranni and their allies assassinated the entire family line. Baron Morrikan d’Kundarak and Lord Tolar Paelion were blood brothers, and <font></font>
Morrikan refuses to believe the accusations made against the Paelions. He has sworn to uncover the truth and avenge his friend. To this end, he has assigned the Ghorad’din—the <font></font>
“Manticore’s Tail,” the secret assassins of House Kundarak—to dog the heels of Thuranni and its Shadow Network. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lyrandar and Orien. Before the airship’s invention, House Orien had a monopoly on travel across Khorvaire. Lyrandar’s expansion into air travel threatens that dominance. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Medani and Tharashk. House Medani and House Tharashk both operate guilds of inquisitives, vying for a limited field of business. Each house has its specialties, but the inquisitives of <font></font>
both houses compete fiercely for customers. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Phiarlan and Thuranni. The two houses that carry the Mark of Shadow separated less than thirty years ago, and tensions remain high between them. They generally avoid open conflict by <font></font>
operating in distinct territories. When their agents cross paths, rivalries can trigger confrontation—which manifests in artistic competitions or duels between assassins. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
INTERNAL TENSIONS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The schism of House Phiarlan and House Thuranni marks the first time a house has split among family lines, but it might not be the last. Tensions within a single dragonmarked house can <font></font>
provide campaign conflicts, especially if the characters in your campaign all belong to the same house. The following houses have significant internal conflicts. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Cannith. The leaders of House Cannith perished in the Mourning, and three powerful barons now vie for control. Each one wants to unite the house under their own leadership, but it's <font></font>
entirely possible the house could fracture as House Phiarlan did. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Sivis. House Sivis is known for its neutrality in struggles between nations and dragonmarked houses alike—it often helps settle disputes and maintain order when two houses differ. But its <font></font>
own internal affairs involve a tremendous amount of family rivalry. The house's twelve families include the Corralyns, Lyrrimans, Santors, Severins, Syrralans, and Torralyns. Only because of <font></font>
her expertise at games of intrigue has Doyenne Lysse Lyrriman d’Sivis managed to head the house for ninety years. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Tharashk. An ancient conflict lies hidden within the foundations of House Tharashk. The powerful Torrn clan has ties to the Gatekeeper druids, while the Aashta clan aligns with the Cults <font></font>
of the Dragon Below. Most modern heirs have only a casual knowledge of these traditions, but zealous factions within the clans pursue esoteric agendas that sometimes boil out into <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 83/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
conflict within the house. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A Rivat Houses Arc <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This example arc presupposes that the characters generally stay neutral in disputes among the dragonmarked houses. The characters might act as agents of the Twelve, House Sivis, or <font></font>
some other group or patron that pursues peace among the houses. Or the characters might represent different houses; their prior relationship to each other motivates them to maintain <font></font>
peace. If the characters belong to a single house, you can adapt this example by using their house as one of the rival factions. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Based on that initial assumption, the conflict among rival dragonmarked houses might unfold according to this broad outline. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 1-4. As prominent but not yet powerful figures in the dragonmarked landscape, the characters become the targets of two rival houses. Both houses try to befriend and influence <font></font>
them, perhaps hiring them for various missions. Meanwhile, the conflict between the houses simmers on a back burner. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 5-10. The conflict between the two rival houses erupts into a trade war, a concerted effort to take over business from each other, or even violent conflict. With the characters now <font></font>
emerging as a force to be reckoned with, both houses call on them for help, trying to force the characters to choose sides. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 11-16. As the conflict between the two houses rages on, a third group gets involved, hoping to take advantage of the warring parties’ distraction or weakness. Figures both within <font></font>
and outside the houses plead with the characters to help end the conflict before it engulfs other houses or even nations. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 17-20. The chaos spreads, and the characters have a choice to make. They can work to restore the delicate balance of power among the houses, as well as between the houses <font></font>
and the Five Nations. Or they can try to reshape the world order according to a different pattern. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: JORDAN KERBOW <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A TARKANAN MARAUDER AND A TARKANAN RUFFIAN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House TARKANAN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House Tarkanan is a criminal gang in Sharn, founded when Thora Tavin assembled the survivors of a suicide squad of Brelish spies who bore aberrant dragonmarks. She adopted the <font></font>
surname of Lord Halas Tarkanan, who used his aberrant dragonmark to wreak havoc in Sharn during the War of the Mark, and fashioned this group of thieves and assassins into a mockery <font></font>
of the dragonmarked houses. However, the gang lacks the recognition, power, and resources of the actual houses. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Goats of House TARKANAN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House Tarkanan is a gang of thieves and assassins operating within the city of Sharn, but its name points to its founder's vision for building the gang into a larger movement. Most <font></font>
members have no higher aspirations than using their aberrant dragonmarks to profit from a criminal lifestyle. But the gang's leaders seek to amplify their dragonmarks’ power to the world- <font></font>
shaking level of the aberrant champions of the War of the Mark, Halas Tarkanan and the Lady of the Plague. With such awesome power at their disposal, they plan to reawaken the ancient <font></font>
conflict over aberrant dragonmarks and topple the dragonmarked houses from their position of power. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Thora Tarkanan (still known about town under her original name, Thora Tavin), who founded the gang, recently stepped aside from its leadership, ceding power to a mysterious figure called <font></font>
the Son of Khyber. Little is known about him, but he bears the strongest aberrant mark seen in modern times along with the skills of a military commander. Some call him Halas Tarkanan <font></font>
born again, believing he is mustering an aberrant army to lead into battle against the dragonmarked houses. Thora remains the Son of Khyber’s second-in-command and the public face of <font></font>
the gang. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A House TARKANAN Arc <font></font>
The conflict between adventurers and House Tarkanan might follow this broad outline. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 1-4. A patron asks the characters to get involved in a criminal investigation into a rash of murders in Sharn because all the victims have dragonmarks. Both the Sharn Watch and <font></font>
one or more private inquisitives already squabble over access to evidence and witnesses—the characters’ attempt to get involved merely heightens the tension. The perpetrators turn out to <font></font>
be members of House Tarkanan, whom the characters must bring to justice. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 5-10. After the characters’ success in dealing with the Tarkanan killers, the Watch comes to them for additional help. Petty thieves belonging to the gang have offered inside <font></font>
information into House Tarkanan’s plans in exchange for leniency in their sentencing. These thieves don't share their leaders’ delusions of grandeur or hatred of the dragonmarked houses. <font></font>
The information points to a gathering of aberrant heirs deep below Sharn. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 11-16. After the characters disrupt House Tarkanan’s schemes in the depths of Sharn, the gang sends powerful assassins with fearsome aberrant dragonmarks after the party. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 84/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 17-20. The Son of Khyber leads a small army of aberrant-marked warriors and assassins to challenge the leadership of Sharn—and plots to conjure a Blob of Annihilation to level <font></font>
the city’s skyscraping towers. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ABERRANT Heirs <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House Tarkanan incorporates members with a wide variety of aberrant dragonmarks. Any Humanoid in the Monster Manual or another source could be a House Tarkanan agent with a <font></font>
mark that doesn’t affect the creature's use in combat or that is reflected in the creature's existing abilities. For example, a Vampire Familiar might gain its unique abilities from an aberrant <font></font>
mark rather than from any connection to a vampire, and a Bandit Deceiver ’s spellcasting could derive from such a mark. On the other hand, an ordinary Bandit or Tough might have an <font></font>
aberrant mark that allows the NPC to cast Disguise Self, Silent Image, or a similar spell with little impact on the NPC’s combat abilities. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The two stat blocks presented here, along with the Tarkanan Assassin in Eberron: Rising from the Last War, illustrate members of House Tarkanan who wield the power of their aberrant <font></font>
marks in combat. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Tarkanan Marauder. This powerful assassin relies on teleportation to catch targets unaware and deal out rapid death. <font></font>

<font></font>
THE CHAMBER <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Chamber is a secretive network of dragons that have monitored the peoples of Khorvaire for thousands of years. These dragons consider people merely pawns in an ancient game that <font></font>
revolves around the Draconic Prophecy. Each dragon of the Chamber watches over a small corner of the world and maintains a network of agents who can move freely in the cities and <font></font>
nations of Khorvaire. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
GOALS OF THE CHAMBER <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Though most members of the Chamber simply observe events in Khorvaire and gather information about new aspects of the Prophecy, many dragons seek to understand the connection <font></font>
between dragonmarks and the Prophecy—some even conduct magical experiments on dragonmarked people. Dragons of the Chamber take a particular interest in the most powerful <font></font>
dragonmarks, such as Siberys marks and mighty aberrant marks (see chapter 2). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
These goals don't necessarily put the Chamber in conflict with dragonmarked characters or their houses. Even when they do, this conflict need not be violent. Fundamentally, though, the <font></font>
dragons of the Chamber count the value of dragonmarked individuals only in terms of the marks they carry and their place in the Prophecy. This means the dragons consider dragonmarked <font></font>
characters expendable and their personal goals inconsequential compared to the long-range views of the Chamber. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A Camper Arc <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The conflict between adventurers and the Chamber might unfold according to this broad outline, starting around the time the characters reach level 5. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 5-10. Dragonmarked characters come to the attention of a dragon belonging to the Chamber. The dragon sends an agent to meet them, become a friend or patron to them, and <font></font>
keep an eye on their activities. Occasionally the agent might send them on strange errands intended to fulfill the Prophecy in a particular way. Other times, the agent provides helpful <font></font>
advice, useful spells, or even magic items to aid the characters in adventures. Some missions, particularly later in this level range, might feel distasteful to the characters or directly conflict <font></font>
with their other goals. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 11-16. The dragon monitoring the characters becomes convinced that the characters’ other activities are corrupting the Prophecy. The dragon's friendly agent disappears, and a <font></font>
group of hostile agents begins to undermine the characters’ work. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 17-20. When the dragon alerts other members of the Chamber that the characters pose a threat to the Prophecy, multiple dragons attempt to eliminate the group. <font></font>
<font></font>

DRAGONMARKED HOUSES t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Eberron: Rising from the Last War contains basic information about the dragonmarked houses, and chapter 2 of this book includes character backgrounds representing heirs of these <font></font>
houses. This section provides an overview of each house, focusing on opportunities for intrigue within and among the houses. It also includes additional favors available to characters who <font></font>
belong to the houses, using the renown rules from “Dragonmarked House Renown’ earlier in this chapter. <font></font>
<font></font>

ARTIST: LEROY STEINMANN <font></font>

<font></font>
AGENTS OF (LEFT TO RIGHT) HOUSE DENEITH, HOUSE GHALLANDA, HOUSE LYRANDAR, <font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
House CANNITH <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: CLAUDIO POZAS <font></font>
Since the destruction of Cyre and the headquarters of House Cannith in the Last War, the <font></font>
house has functioned as three distinct organizations while the three Cannith barons vie for <font></font>
control. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
CANNITH East <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Headquartered in Korth (in Karrnath) and led by Zorlan d’Cannith, Cannith East enjoys close <font></font>
ties to the government of Karrnath and the faith of the Blood of Vol. This branch of the <font></font>
house specializes in siege weaponry and defenses, though its most innovative experiments <font></font>
explore the creation of constructed undead—a hybrid of warforged and zombie. In addition <font></font>
to Karrnath, Cannith East has enclaves in the Mror Holds and the Lhazaar Principalities. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House CANNITH <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
CANNITH SOUTH <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Under the leadership of Merrix d’Cannith, the Cannith South holdings in Sharn have come to rival the old house headquarters in Whitehearth as a hotspot of innovation. Additional cells in <font></font>
Breland, Zilargo, Darguun, and even Stormreach (in Xen’drik) carry out Merrix’s plans and produce inventions to fill the house's coffers. Rumor has it the Sharn enclave conceals the last <font></font>
creation forge capable of producing warforged—and that Merrix plans to build another one in Stormreach, where the Treaty of Thronehold halds no sway. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
CANNITH WEST <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Headquartered near the Aundairian capital of Fairhaven, Cannith West follows Jorlanna d’Cannith, whose leadership claim is hindered by an indiscretion from her youth. Some thirty years <font></font>
ago, Jorlanna had a star-crossed romance with an heir of House Deneith—an affair forbidden by the protocols of the dragonmarked houses that date back to the War of the Mark. Though <font></font>
the relationship ended quickly, and rumors of a child have never been proven, Jorlanna’s reputation for poor judgment haunts her. Despite that, her strong vision for a united house, her <font></font>
allies among the Twelve, and her personal magnetism make her a powerful contender for control of the house. Under her leadership, Cannith West conducts investigations in enclaves and <font></font>
magical sites across Aundair, Thrane, and the Eldeen Reaches. She maintains friendly relations with other houses and with the nobility in and around Aundair. <font></font>
<font></font>

Recognized in House (Renown 10+). When you take a Bastion turn, you can issue the Trade order to House Orien as if it were a Storehouse facility in your Bastion, using your level to <font></font>
determine the maximum value of items purchased and the profit you get from selling them. If you are level 9+, you instead can issue the Recruit order to House Orien as if it were a <font></font>
Teleportation Circle facility within your Bastion. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House PHIARLAN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The five Demesnes of House Phiarlan are each devoted to a specific artistic tradition: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Demesne of Memory (in Fairhaven in Aundair) encompasses the written word, including <font></font>
prose, poetry, and propaganda. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Demesne of Motion (in Wroat in Breland) focuses on the arts of the body, including dance, <font></font>
gymnastics, wrestling, acrobatics, and massage. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Demesne of Music (in Flamekeep in Thrane) teaches the arts of sound, including song and <font></font>
instrumental music. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Demesne of Shape (in Thaliost in Thrane) focuses on material arts, including the practical <font></font>
construction of props, costumes, instruments, and the like, as well as sculpture, painting, <font></font>
and pottery. <font></font>

House PHIARLAN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Demesne of Shadow (in Sharn) teaches the art of deception, which includes illusions, puppetry, and shadow plays, as well as acting, oratory, and impersonation. Baron Elvinor Elorrenthi <font></font>
d’Phiarlan makes her headquarters in the Shadow enclave. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A sixth head to the Phiarlan hydra, the Serpentine Table, is the house’s espionage wing and one of the finest intelligence networks in Khorvaire. Few people know the group exists, and <font></font>
fewer still can use its services—the house grants access only to guildmasters, nobles, and players in the great game of power. <font></font>
<font></font>

House PHIARLAN RENOWN PERKS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you request a favor from your house, it can take one of these special forms. <font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
Recognized in House (Renown 10+). When you take a Bastion turn, you can issue the Craft order to House Phiarlan as if it were a Workshop facility in your Bastion. If you are level 9+, you <font></font>
instead can issue the Empower order to the house as if it were a Theater facility, and if you are level 13+, you instead can issue the Research order to the house as if it were a Pub facility. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House Sivis <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Under the leadership of Doyenne Lysse Lyrriman d'Sivis, House Sivis does everything it can <font></font>
to stabilize a world torn apart by war. Some write off the importance of a house of scribes, <font></font>
while others wonder whether the much-vaunted neutrality of House Sivis conceals a far- <font></font>
reaching hidden agenda. Whatever the truth of the house's ambition, House Sivis remains <font></font>
an invaluable, integral part of modern civilization. Its services make communication <font></font>
possible across long distances, and its partnership with House Kundarak facilitates the <font></font>
most essential financial transactions of the Five Nations. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House Sivis <font></font>

House THARASHK <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House Tharashk is an alliance of three great families (with smaller associated clans), each <font></font>
with different origins and agendas. The alliance remains only as strong as the personal <font></font>
bonds that link the heads of the families. These three leaders, called the Triumvirate, <font></font>
manage the house together from the city of Zarash’ak in the Shadow Marches. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
AASHTA <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Aashta and their allies have ancient ties to at least two Cults of the Dragon Below and <font></font>
the power of Khyber. One could charitably describe the Aashta leaders (including their <font></font>
triumvir, Khandar’aashta) as opportunists who take every chance to advance their interests <font></font>
and those of their family—no matter who stands in the way. <font></font>

<font></font>
TorRN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Torrn and their allied families perpetuate the traditions of the Gatekeepers—a druidic sect dedicated to protecting the world from aberrations and other unnatural creatures. This puts <font></font>
the Torrn at odds with the most devout Aashta followers of the Dragon Below. Triumvir Maagrim Torrn secretly supports efforts of some family members to channel house resources <font></font>
toward the traditional mission of the Gatekeepers. <font></font>

VELDERAN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Velderan and their allies have no particular ties to any spiritual tradition, and they are generally altruistic and willing to cooperate with other families and houses. Some within the clan <font></font>
whisper that Triumvir Daric Velderan seems too accommodating and too willing to placate the other leaders of the house. <font></font>

four. In addition, if you are level 9+, you can issue the Research order to House Tharashk as if it were a Trophy Room facility within your Bastion. <font></font>

House THURANNI <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: CLAUDIO POZAS <font></font>
The story of the Shadow Schism that sundered House Thuranni from its kindred in House <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Phiarlan is a tangled knot of mystery. The surface details of the tale seem simple enough. <font></font>
About twenty years ago, Baron Elar d’Thuranni ordered the assassination of the entire <font></font>
Paelion line, one of the branches of House Phiarlan. Supposedly, he acted to prevent the <font></font>
Paelions from carrying out a wicked plot that would have ended the Last War and <font></font>
exterminated the leaders of the other dragonmarked houses, leaving the Paelions as <font></font>
absolute rulers of Khorvaire. <font></font>
<font></font>
House THURANNI <font></font>
Was the Paelion plot real or fabricated by the sinister Order of the Emerald Claw? Was the <font></font>
schism orchestrated by agents of the Chamber to fulfill aspects of the Draconic Prophecy <font></font>
that speak of thirteen houses? <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Did the barons of House Phiarlan and House Thuranni plan the whole thing together to eliminate a mutual enemy? Whatever the truth behind the schism, it stands as the most significant <font></font>
consequence of the Last War to the dragonmarked. Just as the kingdom of Galifar shattered into smaller nations, the Last War left the dragonmarked houses broken, unsure of their role in <font></font>
the new world and their ties to the new political powers of Khorvaire, and the division of Thuranni from Phiarlan embodies that new reality. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
From his enclave in Regalport (in the Lhazaar Principalities), Baron Elar d’Thuranni rules a house that is a shadow of its united predecessor, similar in many ways to House Phiarlan. But <font></font>
while spies and assassins are contained within a secret sixth branch of House Phiarlan, separate from the house's artistic endeavors, the entertainers and artisans of House Thuranni are <font></font>
also its spies and assassins—and its assassins outnumber its spies. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House THURANNI RENOWN PERKS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you request a favor from your house, it can take one of these special forms. <font></font>
Favored in House (Renown 3+). You can purchase one dose of poison (any kind) or other illegal item (such as stolen goods) at half price. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Recognized in House (Renown 10+). When you take a Bastion turn, you can issue the Craft order to House Thuranni as if it were a Workshop facility in your Bastion. If you are level 9+, you <font></font>
instead can issue the Empower order to the house as if it were a Theater facility, and if you are level 13+, you can issue the Research order to the house as if it were a Pub facility. <font></font>

House VADALIS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: CLAUDIO POZAS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House Vadalis has a carefully cultivated reputation as the most innocuous of the <font></font>
dragonmarked houses. Its leader, Dalin d’Vadalis, disdains use of a title and governs the <font></font>
house from a remote estate near Varna in the Eldeen Reaches—far from the seats of power <font></font>
in the Five Nations. Breeding superior animals for agriculture hardly seems threatening to <font></font>
most of Khorvaire. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
And yet House Vadalis has stirred up more than its fair share of controversy. Tales say that <font></font>
during the War of the Mark, House Vadalis bred arcane beasts so horrifying they were later HH OUSE VA DALIS <font></font>
banned and destroyed. During the Last War, the house bred fierce bears to serve as mounts <font></font>
for elite Brelish troops. Persistent rumors suggest that the house might seek to apply their <font></font>
techniques of magical enhancement to human subjects. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House Vapatis RENowN Perks <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When you request a favor from your house, it can take one of these special forms. <font></font>
Favored in House (Renown 3+). You can purchase any animal for half the usual cost, or you can secure free feed and stabling for animals owned by you and your party for up to 1 week. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Recognized in House (Renown 10+). When you take a Bastion turn, you can issue the Research order to House Vadalis as if it were a Library facility in your Bastion, as long as the topic of <font></font>
research is some kind of living creature. In addition, when you issue the Recruit order to a Menagerie facility in your Bastion, you pay half the usual cost for the creature you acquire. <font></font>

<font></font>
DRAGONMARKED INTRIGUE ADVENTURES t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Eberron: Rising from the Last War includes suggestions for building adventures focused on individual dragonmarked houses, but the most interesting adventures involve interaction <font></font>
between multiple houses. Use the House Intrigue table to inspire plots involving two or more houses. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
House INTRIGUE <font></font>
1d6 Adventure Goal <font></font>
1 Dragonmarked houses vie for influence over a prominent individual (perhaps one of the characters). <font></font>
2 Dragonmarked houses compete for control of a key site. <font></font>
3 Tension builds between two rival dragonmarked houses, threatening to erupt into a deadly feud. <font></font>
4 Villains from two or more dragonmarked houses form a dangerous alliance. <font></font>
5 A villain tries to disrupt an alliance between two or more dragonmarked houses. <font></font>
6 Splinter factions from two dragonmarked houses try toform a new house. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
DraconmarKep NPCs <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can use the stat blocks in this section for dragonmarked heirs of the houses in your adventures. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

and the artificers’ force-empowered maces function only in their creator’s hands. <font></font>

LYRANDAR SCION <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Khoravar dragonmarked scions of House Lyrandar include swashbuckling sky captains, seafaring adventurers, and sinister cultists of the Hurricane Harvest. The power of their <font></font>
dragonmarks manifests as easy control of wind, thunder, and lightning, which they wield as readily in combat as in navigating the sea and sky. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Members of the Hurricane Harvest believe that the Mark of Storm is not a gift of the Sovereign Host, as others in their house teach, but the sign of the Devourer. They maintain that the <font></font>
greatest ancestors of their house live on as krakens in the deep and guide the cult through visions to use their power for destruction and domination. They sometimes partner with kuo-toa, <font></font>
sahuagin, and other monsters of the deep. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

MEDAN! INQUISITIVE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The most prized inquisitives of House Medani, a house of mostly Khoravar, employ their dragonmarks and their keen intellects to anticipate threats from any quarter. Some are part of a <font></font>
secretive order called the Basilisk's Gaze, dedicated to hunting down fugitive war criminals who committed atrocities during the Last War. <font></font>

SENTINEL MARSHAL <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Sentinel Marshals of House Deneith, who are typically human, hold the honor of the house in their hands. The Treaty of Thronehold upheld their authority to pursue criminals across <font></font>
international borders, giving them the unique privilege and responsibility of upholding the law across Khorvaire. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Only the most trusted heirs of the house are honored with a position in the Sentinel Marshals, either after serving in the house's other guilds for several years (as bodyguards in the <font></font>
Defenders Guild and mercenary officers in the Blademarks Guild) or by special dispensation of House Deneith. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Sentinel Marshals are never authorized to break local or international laws, but neither are they accountable to the legal authorities of any nation. A rogue marshal can be punished only by <font></font>
the leadership of House Deneith, but some nations (such as Darguun and Droaam) ignore these conventions. <font></font>
<font></font>

SHADOW-MarKED AGENT <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Agents of House Phiarlan and House Thuranni, who are primarily elves, combine stealth and espionage with mastery of the performing arts—and dragonmarked heirs augment those skills <font></font>
with shadow magic. This stat block can serve for an agent of either house. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Even though House Phiarlan agents don't typically work as entertainers or artists in addition to their espionage activities, they receive extensive training in those arts alongside other <font></font>
members of their house. House Thuranni agents, in contrast, are always both artists and spies. <font></font>

ST: MICHAEL BROUSSARD <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
AR <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
AN ARMORER EXPLORER VENTURES INTO AN ANCIENT RUIN <font></font>
IN XEN'DRIK IN SEARCH OF MAGICAL SECRETS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ORGRAVE UNIVERSITY IN SHARN, ONE OF THE LARGEST INSTITUTIONS OF HIGHER LEARNING IN KHORVAIRE, HAS A MIXED REPUTATION. ON THE ONE HAND, ITS FOUNDER— <font></font>
Lord Lareth ir'Morgrave—envisioned the university as “a beacon of knowledge shining from the tallest towers of the city, illuminating the forgotten secrets of the past.” Generations of <font></font>
students and scholars have tried to help the university live up to that inspiring vision over the past two and a half centuries. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
On the other hand, ir Morgrave had a reputation as a treasure hunter who built his fortune by selling artifacts and treasures stolen from archaeological digs in Darguun, Xen’drik, and other <font></font>
sites. And countless students, scholars, and adventurers over the centuries have contributed to the university's reputation as a cover for smuggling activities and plundering the past. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 105/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Run a Morgrave expeditions campaign if you want to emulate the pulp-action adventures of Professor Challenger, Indiana Jones, Lara Croft, and other explorer-scholars. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 106/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
MORGRAVE CHARTACTERS t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A Morgrave expeditions campaign assumes that characters are connected to Morgrave University, which is a patron to the adventuring party. This arrangement allows the university to <font></font>
assign the characters to specific missions and reward them for achieving certain objectives without directing their every move. In return, the characters can draw on the resources of the <font></font>
university, and they gain other benefits from having a group patron. (If you use the group patron rules in Eberron: Rising from the Last War or Tasha’s Cauldron of Everything, you can use <font></font>
the benefits listed here instead of the ones in those books; you can use the information on the University and Academy patrons to flesh out the characters’ contacts, fields of study, and so <font></font>
on.) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Archaeologist background described in chapter 2 is a good option for characters in this campaign, alongside Player's Handbook backgrounds such as Guide, Sage, and Scribe. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Group Patron BENEFITS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Characters in a party whose patron is Morgrave University gain the following benefits. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Group ASSISTANCE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Having a group patron gives an adventuring party a common purpose—a patron's guidance and encouragement inspire better coordination. As a result of this unity, each member of your <font></font>
party can grant Advantage to a D20 Test made by another member of the party. To grant Advantage in this way, you and your target must be able to see or hear each other, and neither can <font></font>
have the Incapacitated condition. After granting this Advantage, you can't do so again until you finish a Long Rest. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
COMPENSATION <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The university pays for the work you do on its behalf. The nature of your employment influences how you are paid. On average, the university pays each member of your group 1 GP per day <font></font>
(enough to sustain a [Tooltip Not Found] lifestyle). Or you might be paid a bounty (at least 250 GP) for each ancient relic you bring back from your adventures and give to the university. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
DocuMENTATION <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Each member of your group has identification papers noting their affiliation with Morgrave University, which carries clout in academic circles. The university also acquires documentation, <font></font>
letters of introduction, and traveling papers for you if your work requires them. Finally, if your adventures take you to Xen’drik, the university secures the necessary letters of marque issued <font></font>
by the king of Breland, which grant you permission to explore the ruins there. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
RESEARCH <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The university has abundant resources to facilitate any research your mission requires. Whenever your group takes a Bastion turn, you can (as a group) issue the Research order to the <font></font>
university as if it were a Library special facility in your Bastion. Once your group reaches level 13, you instead can issue the Research order to the university as if it were an Archive. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
RESOURCES <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Morgrave University has extensive libraries and museums. You can call in a favor to gain access to resources that are generally not on exhibit: dangerous relics, magic items, spellbooks, <font></font>
and the like. Additionally, you can consult with experts in various fields among the university faculty—so long as you can coax them away from their work. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
TRAINING <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You have access to continuing education at no cost. You can spend 30 days in classes to gain one of the following benefits: <font></font>
You gain proficiency in the Arcana, History, Nature, or Religion skill. You can gain proficiency in only one skill in this way. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You gain proficiency with a tool. <font></font>
You learn a language. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https://;www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 107/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
MORGRAVE CONFLICTS t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A campaign centered on Morgrave University suggests three significant campaign conflicts: <font></font>
Characters can explore the tension between legitimate scholarship and greed as they undertake expeditions and explorations in competition with rival adventurers. <font></font>
Adventures can bring characters into conflict with sinister forces such as the Order of the Emerald Claw, which seeks to use ancient magic to advance its evil purposes. <font></font>
t. The adventurers might—through accident or deception—release ancient evils into the world. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You can replace or supplement these conflicts with others from Eberron: Rising from the Last War —such as the Dreaming Dark, the Heirs of Dhakaan, or Cults of the Dragon Below—or <font></font>
scenarios of your own devising. The sites where characters conduct their explorations might suggest particular conflicts. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This section describes the three suggested conflicts, the goals of the villainous groups, and possible plot arcs tied to them. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Riva EXPLORERS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Morgrave University teems with unscrupulous adventurers and researchers driven by avarice. Additionally, the university itself has academic rivals—the University of Wynarn in Aundair, the <font></font>
Library of Korranberg, and others—whose scholars might become personal foils to adventurers associated with Morgrave. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
GOALS OF Rivat EXPLORERS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Create a team of rival explorers who oppose the characters in different ways. If the characters are altruistic researchers seeking to expand the frontiers of knowledge, their rivals should be <font></font>
greedy, selfish, and ruthless—the worst representatives of Morgrave's bad reputation. They seek fame, wealth, and power by plundering the secrets of ancient civilizations and claiming the <font></font>
magic and money they find for themselves. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
On the other hand, if the characters seem motivated by greed, choose as their rivals high-minded scholars from Wynarn. These foes work to discover the secrets of the past, contribute to <font></font>
the sum of knowledge, and prove that their university deserves more renown than the “institute of relic hunting and grave robbing” that is Morgrave University. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A Rivat Exptorers Arc <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The rivalry between the characters and their foils might unfold according to this outline. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
/. The characters first encounter their adversaries between adventures, at Morgrave University. The rivals might be receiving a coveted accolade, such as a research grant or an <font></font>
academic award. Or they might be conspicuously spending ill-gotten wealth, boasting of their accomplishments, or directly taunting the characters. (A rival group at these levels might <font></font>
consist of a or stain, al I ,a , and a Spy.) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
In later adventures, the characters encounter their rivals in the field. Seeing the adventurers’ growing success, unscrupulous adversaries might engage in sabotage, theft, and <font></font>
similar escalating (but not violent) attacks. Or the characters might use such tactics themselves as their rivals enjoy a series of lucky finds and major successes. (A rival group at these <font></font>
levels might consist of an A ,aG ator,aM anda .) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
As the adventurers reach high levels, the rivalry comes to a head through an unfortunate event. The adversaries acquire a cursed item that possesses or otherwise corrupts <font></font>
them, turning the other group into outright antagonists. Ideally, the characters solve this problem by breaking the curse rather than killing the rivals, but either way these foils no longer <font></font>
trouble the characters after they resolve this situation. (A rival group at these levels might consist of an lage, an priest, a E , and a Wa .) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Orber oF THE EMerALD CLAw <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: DAARKEN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
TWO EMERALD CLAW KNIGHTS JOIN THEIR COMMANDER IN A HUNT FOR POWERFUL MAGIC <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Driven by fanatical evil and bolstered by legions of undead soldiers, the Order of the Emerald Claw is a villainous organization whose aims often pit its agents against expeditions from <font></font>
Morgrave University. The order's methods range from military raids using undead soldiers to subtle espionage and assassination. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
GOALS oF THE EMERALD CLAW <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
In service of its undead leader, L I , the Order of the Emerald Claw seeks powerful magic items, particularly those created with necromantic magic. Often its agents use these <font></font>
items simply to spread terror and destruction, but their efforts ultimately support Lady Ill marrow’s goal of reactivating her dragonmark, the extinct Mark of Death. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https://;www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 108/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
An Emerato Claw Arc <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The conflict between adventurers and the Order of the Emerald Claw might follow this broad outline. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 1-4. At low levels, the characters can clash with skeletons and zombies under the Emerald Claw’s control. At the same time, they might build a relationship—professional or <font></font>
personal—with an Emerald Claw spy within the Morgrave University faculty, such as Dala Arand (see the “Cyre Tower” section later in this chapter). This spy could even serve as a patron for <font></font>
the characters’ expeditions. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 5-10. Emerald Claw agents use violence to keep the characters away from treasures the order has claimed. The adventurers might set out explicitly to prevent the Emerald Claw <font></font>
from acquiring powerful magic, or the two groups might be searching for the same sites and treasures. But the Claw agents seem to have inside information on the characters’ activities <font></font>
and plans! <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 11-16. The spy at Morgrave University is uncovered and emerges as a powerful villain—perhaps a mighty necromancer or a vampire—who takes center stage through middle to high <font></font>
levels. Now the Emerald Claw primarily works to get the adventurers out of the way so they can't foil Lady Illmarrow’'s greater schemes. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 17-20. The characters or the Emerald Claw agents acquire information about the Mark of Death, attracting Lady Ill marrow’s attention. The would-be Queen of the Dead plans an <font></font>
elaborate ritual in the heart of Shae Mordai, the elven City of the Dead. The rite will destroy the islands of Aerenal and elevate Lady Ill marrow to divinity—unless the characters stop her first. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
EMERALD CLAW AGENTS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Agents of the Order of the Emerald Claw are soldiers whose obedience to the order and their superiors is absolute. They include the scouts, spies, and warriors described in the Monster <font></font>
Manual, in addition to the stat blocks presented here, and they are often accompanied by Undead troops such as Skeletons, Zombies, and Karrnathi Undead Soldiers (which appear in <font></font>
Eberron: Rising from the Last War). Their leaders include Bone Knights (also in Eberron: Rising from the Last War). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Emerald Claw Commander. Emerald Claw commanders lead large groups of soldiers, inspiring the fanatical devotion of Humanoids and Undead alike. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Emerald Claw Knight. Knights of the Emerald Claw command squads of Humanoid or Undead soldiers. As they draw nearer to death, the chill of the grave suffuses their weapon attacks. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lorps of Dust <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Tales from the ancient history of Eberron tell of a time when the world fell entirely under the sway of evil: the so-called Age of Demons, when fiendish beings called overlords ruled the <font></font>
world. The Lords of Dust are servitors of those lords—lesser (but still terrifying) Fiends who seek to sustain the overlords and release them from their prisons in Khyber. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Goats oF THE Loros oF Dust <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
To sustain the overlords, the Lords of Dust encourage activities that strengthen these lords’ influence. For example, because the overlord Rak Tulkhesh embodies war, his minions work to <font></font>
cause strife across the world. And Sul Khatesh feeds on the fear evoked by the sinister magic of warlocks and evil wizards, so her minions entice cruel folk to enter into pacts with the <font></font>
Queen of Shadows. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Lords of Dust work toward the long-term goal of releasing the overlords from imprisonment by manipulating world events to conform to their interpretations of the Draconic Prophecy. <font></font>
Over the millennia, they sought to collect every possible fragment of the mysterious Prophecy that might pertain to the overlords’ liberation. They try to fulfill the Prophecy in ways that <font></font>
advance that goal. However, they are constantly impeded by newly discovered elements of Prophecy—especially since the dragonmarks that appear on mortals often seem connected to <font></font>
relevant parts of the Prophecy. This drives many Lords of Dust to collect or kill dragonmarked characters. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A Loros oF Dust Arc <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The threat of the Lords of Dust need not become evident until the characters have some experience under their belts and can handle the threat of the overlords’ weakest minions. The <font></font>
conflict might play out according to this outline. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 5-10. A few strange and apparently unconnected events mark the characters’ adventures during these levels. A demon flies into a rage at the sight of a dragonmarked character <font></font>
and attacks only that character. A mysterious figure in disguise tries to hire the characters to carry out a bizarre mission in a very specific way. The characters find their path through a <font></font>
dungeon cleared out ahead of them, with mangled monster corpses left in the wake of whatever horror preceded them—but the ancient relic they seek there is undisturbed. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 11-16. The Lords of Dust try to manipulate the characters to use the ancient relic to kill a dragon, prominent dragonmarked individual, or political figure. The Lords believe that the <font></font>
characters using the relic in this way will fulfill part of the Draconic Prophecy and serve as an important step toward the overlords’ release. At some point, an evil dragon (an agent of the <font></font>
Chamber) warns the characters against this manipulation, explaining the nature and goals of the Lords of Dust to them. If the characters refuse to cooperate with the lords, powerful <font></font>
Fiends attack them to claim the relic and place it in the hands of more pliable adventurers. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Levels 17-20. Apparently by coincidence, the adventurers’ latest expedition—the crowning achievement of their careers—leads to the discovery of an Underdark site where an overlord lies <font></font>
imprisoned. A horde of demons appears and attacks, each one throwing itself on the overlord’s prison when it is slain. As each creature's ichor spills over the prison, cracks appear in the <font></font>
stone surface. Can the adventurers fend off the demons and the ever-increasing manifestations of evil without freeing the overlord and unleashing destruction on the world? <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

ARTIST: CULLEN COLE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
COURTYARDS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Morgrave University is prominently situated in Sharn, the City of Towers—in the Upper Menthis ward. The institution owns the buildings surrounding the campus proper, renting residences <font></font>
to students and faculty. The university also leases business space to essential trades and services, from booksellers and stationers to scribes and cartographers. The school fills Dalannan <font></font>
Tower, crowned by the enormous dome of Lareth Hall. Five slender spires (named after the Five Nations) ring Dalannan Tower and house the students. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
At the bottom levels of the university, several large courtyards among the towers feature grass and small trees. Locals call these courtyards the “ground floor” of the campus, despite their <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
position high above the Menthis Plateau. They're popular sites for student gatherings and class meetings on the rare occasions that Sharn’s notoriously rainy weather lets up. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Beyond the courtyards, the “ground floor” contains lecture halls, study halls, small libraries (mostly focused on books assigned for student reading), dining halls, and faculty offices. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Morgrave is a constant bustle of activity while classes remain in session, but it can feel deserted in the evenings, on weekends, and between terms. Even for nonstudents, the university <font></font>
contains resources and information helpful to nearly any pursuit—if you know where to look and whom to ask. The following locations represent some of the most popular and versatile <font></font>
resources on the campus. Among the location descriptions are snapshots of some of the faces of Morgrave—professors who are colorful characters and might have interesting roles to <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
play in a Morgrave expeditions campaign. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
110/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
DALANNAN TOWER <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The lowest levels of Dalannan Tower hold faculty offices for several core departments, including history, archaeology, anthropology, geography, arcana, linguistics, and the interdisciplinary <font></font>
Xen'drik studies and Dhakaani studies programs. These offices, along with a sprinkling of lecture halls, ring the tower's outer edge. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
At these lower levels, the tower's interior (accessible only from the floors above) contains the vaults of the Dezina Museum of Antiquities, whose public areas fill several of Dalannan’s <font></font>
middle floors. These vaults contain hundreds, if not thousands, of unopened crates holding uncatalogued treasures—some of which have been there for decades. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Above the Dezina Museum, the Morgrave University Library fills the top levels of Dalannan Tower. While it doesn’t match the library at Korranberg in breadth, it is the most extensive <font></font>
collection of books in Breland. It specializes in the history of Khorvaire and is run by gnome expatriates from Zilargo. Students and faculty can use the library for free; others must pay 1 GP <font></font>
per day. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
At the top of Dalannan Tower lies a complex of several buildings with great domes and pillared facades. Lareth Hall, which houses administrative offices, is the largest of these structures. <font></font>
Next to it rises the Great Hall of Aureon, a temple to the Sovereign of knowledge. It’s purported to be a source of divine inspiration—tales say that sleeping on the temple's marble floor <font></font>
provides insight into one’s problems. The temple staff includes scholars and experts from practically every academic field and discipline. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Attached to both Lareth Hall and the Great Hall of Aureon, a slender spire topped with a column-ringed, open-air hall completes the facility. This is the home of Flamewind (see “Faces of <font></font>
Morgrave” below). The sphinx gives the spire its name: Flamewind’s Steeple. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
FLAMEWIND <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Not technically a faculty member, the sphinx called Flamewind (Sphinx of Lore) is certainly one of the most interesting characters on campus. A recent expedition to Xen'‘drik encountered <font></font>
her, and she claimed she had been waiting for the scholars to find her. She willingly returned with them to Khorvaire and took up residence at the top of a spire beside Lareth Hall's great <font></font>
dome. She frequently summons adventurers to her chamber and sends them on cryptic missions. Many say she has oracular powers and has studied the Draconic Prophecy. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Guash Duurkat <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Though he hasn't yet received a professorship (he carries the title of lecturer in the history department), Ghash Duurkat (Lawful Neutral Hobgoblin Captain) remains a popular sensation at <font></font>
Morgrave. A hobgoblin who wears traditional battle armor to every class, Duurkat is a boisterous lecturer. But despite the theatrics, his knowledge of Dhakaani history is unmatched. A <font></font>
rakshasa associated with the Lords of Dust has taken an interest in Ghash Duurkat’s research, which has touched on the location of an imprisoned overlord far beneath Darguun. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
LARRIAN IR’ MoRGRAVE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Larrian ir’Morgrave (Medium Noble Prodigy) descends directly from Lareth ir'Morgrave, founder of the university, and serves as its current master. Though his job involves more <font></font>
administration than scholarship, he nevertheless finds time to publish more papers on giant-made pottery than any other scholar, past or present. If you use rival explorers in your <font></font>
campaign who are connected to Morgrave University, they work hard to curry favor with Master Larrian, and if the adventurers don't put in similar effort, they'll find the school <font></font>
administration hampering their efforts at every turn. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ZoPHIK <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Widely regarded as erratic, Professor Zophik (Medium Warrior Commander)—a human who no longer uses her first name—has earned the right to some eccentricity. Her field of history is <font></font>
the Age of Demons; supposedly she has traveled to ancient Ashtakala and survived—though not unscarred. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Aunpair SPIRE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Most of Aundair Spire holds student dormitories, faculty residences, and offices for the faculty and staff of the botany, agriculture, and related departments. Crowning the spire, the <font></font>
magnificent Hadrill Gardens displays an extraordinary collection of exotic plants, flowers, and even plant creatures from around the world. Access to the more dangerous plants is <font></font>
restricted to botany students with permission from a faculty member. Both students and staff enjoy meditation and even romantic walks within the spire’s large open-air garden and nine <font></font>
greenhouses—including one devoted to orchids from Xen‘drik and Q’barra. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ALAIN GOURTHAN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Professor Alain Gourthan, a young human botanist (Medium Noble), has yet to make any dramatic discoveries—a fact that grates on his nerves. His academic knowledge exceeds that of <font></font>
many colleagues, and students call him an excellent lecturer. But his lack of significant field experience has held him back. Word is circulating that he plans a truly ambitious expedition to <font></font>
the far south of Xen’drik, which almost certainly will either make or break his career. He might hire the characters or their rivals to accompany him on this xpedition 7 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ab oat <font></font>
= i <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
