11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
BRELAND SPIRE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Below many levels of residences for students and faculty, the lowest levels of Breland Spire hold the offices of the mathematics, engineering, siege craft, alchemy, physics, and planar <font></font>
studies departments. Toward the top of the spire sits the Commons, a large, roofed plaza ringed by columns to allow fresh air to blow through. The Commons is a popular gathering spot <font></font>
for the university community. Food vendors set up carts there every morning and sell a wide variety of dishes throughout the day. In the evening, the Commons grows quieter, becoming a <font></font>
favorite spot for those who want to read, meditate, or have a private conversation. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
THE BRIDGE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A long arcing, covered bridge connects Breland Spire to a level near the top of Dalannan Tower. One side of the bridge features shops focused on the needs of Morgrave's students <font></font>
(bookstores, paper makers, clothing and equipment vendors). On the other side, a wall holds announcements and notices (job opportunities, campus events, items for sale, and so forth). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Cyre Tower <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The lowest levels of Cyre Tower contain offices for the departments of art, music, literature, and related fields. The upper levels here house close-knit communities of students and faculty <font></font>
with common interests. Such interests include academic pursuits, hobbies, shared languages, and religious affiliation. In Saava House—one such community—residents enjoy meals <font></font>
heavily laden with academic discourse regarding the history of Xen‘drik. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Data ARAND <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Dala Arand (Medium, Lawful Evil Tough Boss), a young human resident of Shava House, has a distinguished record of expeditions to Xen’drik. She is particularly drawn to legends <font></font>
surrounding powerful Artifacts wielded by dragons and demons as the ancient Age of Demons came to an end. Rumors claim she has solid leads pointing to the location of an item known <font></font>
as the [Tooltip Not Found], somewhere in a vault in Xen’drik. Secretly, she is an agent of the Order of the Emerald Claw; she reserves the most powerful magic she discovers for the order. <font></font>
The students and adventurers she brings on expeditions remain unaware of this connection. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
KARRNATH SPIRE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Zoology, animal husbandry, medicine, and related departments have offices and classrooms on the lowest levels of Karrnath Spire. Near the top lies the Valdain Museum of Natural History, <font></font>
a fascinating collection of stuffed and mounted animals from across Khorvaire and beyond. Despite being underfunded and unappreciated at a university so focused on ancient ruins and <font></font>
relics, the Valdain Museum features exhibits on magical creatures, from blink dogs and displacer beasts to a great bulette. An enormous dragon skeleton hangs from the ceiling just inside <font></font>
the museum entrance. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
SENDOR REDDICK <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Professor Sendor Reddick (Medium Death Cultist) is a taxidermist as well as a noted scholar of animal biology. As curator of the Valdain Museum of Natural History, Reddick pays <font></font>
handsomely for well-preserved specimens of unusual creatures, though he prefers to stuff them himself. Many whisper that the young-seeming elf dabbles in necromancy, and some <font></font>
speculate that he might be connected to the Order of the Emerald Claw. In fact, he is devoted to Sul Khatesh and serves the Lords of Dust. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
THRANE TOWER <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Departments including law, government, economics, sociology, religion, and related fields occupy offices at the lowest levels of Thrane Tower. The rest of the tower contains residences, <font></font>
including several luxurious homes with commanding views at the top of the tower. Most notable is the home of Larrian ir’Morgrave, the master of the university. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
SANA DHUMA <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
People know Sana Dhuma, an aged orc scholar of religion (Medium, Neutral Good Mage), for her extensive work among the benevolent yuan-ti of Krezent (in the Talenta Plains), as well as <font></font>
a few brief forays into territory held by yuan-ti in Xen‘drik. Her office and residence boast an extensive collection of pet snakes. She's fond of making students suspect that one of the <font></font>
snakes is actually a yuan-ti spy. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Betow Morarave <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Teeming neighborhoods of citizens occupy the towers and streets below the university's main tower. Just below the “ground floor” in Middle Menthis, the middle levels of those towers hold <font></font>
the district of Everbright. There, vendors make and sell magic items, spellcasters offer their services, and specialized merchants trade with exclusive clientele. It's a peaceful, well-to-do <font></font>
neighborhood lit with magic lanterns and glittering with other magical effects. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Below Everbright, the tower and spires of Morgrave University above merge together into a single, massive structure full of apartments and ringed with orderly townhouses, forming the <font></font>
heart of a residential district called Center Bridge. Though rowdy taverns, theaters, and gambling dens surround this neighborhood, Center Bridge remains mostly quiet. Many students and <font></font>
some university staff live here, where lodging costs less than in the upper towers. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
DANGEROUS DESTINATIONS t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
From million-year-old shrines in the Demon Wastes to the wreckage of Cyre in what is now the Mournland, Eberron is strewn with the ruins of former civilizations. At these sites, explorer- <font></font>
adventurers can delve into the mysteries of the past. The adventure locations you choose can affect the tone and flavor of your campaign (see “Flavors of Fantasy” in the Dungeon Master's <font></font>
Guide), as well as determine the kinds of ancient relics characters might unearth in their expeditions. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Ace of Demons <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Age of Demons is shrouded in myth, and explorers rarely find ruins from that ancient era. But a few sites remain, particularly in the Demon Wastes and Q’barra, kept relatively intact by <font></font>
the powerful magic of the Fiends who built them or preserved by an archfiend overlord imprisoned beneath them. Adventurers who dare to explore such a site must beware releasing <font></font>
bound Fiends. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The ruins from the Age of Demons are fitting for supernatural horror adventures. The relics and treasures the characters unearth appear evil and grotesque, and the truths they learn about <font></font>
Eberron’s most ancient history might disturb them deeply. In addition, they likely face nightmarish foes, potentially even including a fiendish overlord, as detailed in Eberron: Rising from the <font></font>
Last War. The Lords of Dust are particularly important antagonists in the Demon Wastes. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Use the Age of Demons Finds table to describe an art object, magic item, or historical curiosity the characters discover on their expeditions into these ancient ruins. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Ace oF Demons Finns <font></font>

<font></font>

<font></font>
2 A dagger with a jagged blade whose handle squirms when held <font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>

<font></font>

<font></font>

<font></font>

<font></font>

<font></font>

DHAKAAN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Empire of Dhakaan, ruled by hobgoblin kings for thousands of years in Khorvaire's distant past, united several rival nations to create the continent's first significant empire. Its reign <font></font>
was shattered during the Daelkyr War, when armies of mind flayers, beholders, and other Aberrations spewed from Xoriat, the plane of delirium, and launched an overwhelming attack on <font></font>
Khorvaire. Though the Gatekeeper druids ultimately drove the daelkyr back and held them in check, the war so destabilized the Dhakaani Empire that it soon collapsed. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Ruins of cities and fortifications built during the Dhakaani Empire dot Khorvaire, especially in the southwest—in modern Droaam, Breland, Zilargo, and Darguun—as well as Valenar and the <font></font>
Mournland. The war with the daelkyr particularly scarred Droaam; ancient relics there might hold the secrets of daelkyr flesh-warping, Gatekeeper planar spells, or the military magic of the <font></font>
Dhakaani. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Expeditions into Dhakaani ruins tend toward heroic fantasy. The dangers lurking there range from ancient traps and Construct guardians crafted by long-dead goblinoids to subterranean <font></font>
monsters that lair in the ruins. Adventures involving the remnants of the Daelkyr War also can incorporate elements of supernatural horror, including hideous monsters descended from the <font></font>
daelkyr’s creations. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Ancient sites from the Dhakaani Empire—and the treasures within them—are esteemed by modern-day hobgoblins from Darguun who seek to revive the empire. Explorers in these ruins <font></font>
often must contend with goblinoid rivals who regard Dhakaani antiquities as rightfully Darguun’s. These treasures include armor and weapons, frequently decorated with calligraphy or <font></font>
illustration—or enhanced with brutal spikes or serrated blades. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
KHYBER <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The creation myths of various cultures describe the primordial dragon Eberron restraining and imprisoning the dragon Khyber within her coils. (See “Khyber” in Eberron: Rising from the <font></font>
Last War for details.) Whether one takes this myth literally or figuratively, one cannot deny that untold alien worlds exist within the subterranean reaches that bear the imprisoned dragon's <font></font>
name—not just the unfamiliar underground passageways and caverns of the Underdark, but entire demiplanes teeming with monstrous creatures. Neither can one deny that the expanses <font></font>
of Khyber serve as a prison for fiendish overlords, daelkyr, and other powerful evils. These coils also harbor sealed portals connecting to these creatures’ native realms. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Expeditions into the depths of Khyber often incorporate elements of supernatural horror, given the pervasive evil of the creatures in these subterranean realms. Thanks to the strange <font></font>
demiplanes embedded within Khyber, these adventures also might cross the streams into alien weirdness: characters could venture into a demiplane resembling Wonderland, Oz, ora <font></font>
postapocalyptic wasteland. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Khyber is a prison realm, and the dragonshards found there relate to binding magic. These crystals are essential components of modern elemental vessels, including airships, Lyrandar <font></font>
galleons, and the lightning rail. Thus, many expeditions into Khyber are prospecting missions for this valuable mineral resource—though such journeys rarely risk delving into the weirdest <font></font>
depths of Khyber. Increasingly, House Tharashk prospectors seek partnerships with academic expeditions into Khyber so they can benefit from scholarly expertise about the realms bizarre <font></font>
phenomena. Morgrave University, true to its reputation, happily supplies such experts in exchange for a cut of the substantial profits. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Aside from assisting in such profit-driven expeditions, Morgrave University doesn't often sponsor independent missions into Khyber. Such missions rarely bring back physical objects of <font></font>
any scholarly interest or monetary value. Nevertheless, the professors who study Khyber occasionally lead or send expeditions to research such obscure topics as the binding seals of the <font></font>
Gatekeepers, the spacedistorting effects of planar confluence, and the psychological impact of exposure to daelkyr magic. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https://;www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 113/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
i <font></font>
XEN’DRIK <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Expeditions to Xen’‘drik remain the core of Morgrave University’s archaeological work and the foundation of its reputation—for both good and ill. The school’s founder, Lord Lareth <font></font>
ir'Morgrave, was one of the first explorers from Khorvaire to penetrate the interior of Xen’drik and discover ruins from an ancient giant civilization there. Despite the great distance involved, <font></font>
multiple university-sponsored expeditions operate across Xen‘drik at any time. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The Age of Giants left numerous ruins strewn across Xen’drik’s landscape. But the continent hides its secrets from explorers, as if the relics of the ancient giants weren't meant for the eyes <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
of prying adventurers. No one knows better than the scholars of Morgrave University how to circumvent those barriers and plunder the continent's troves of both knowledge and wealth. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ELEMENTAL AIRSHIPS AID ARCHAEOLOGICAL EXPEDITIONS TO DISTANT LANDS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
, <font></font>
JOURNEYS TO XEN’DRIK <font></font>
A voyage from Sharn to Stormreach—the most accessible settlement in Xen‘drik, used as a launch point for most expeditions into the interior—involves traveling about fifteen hundred <font></font>
miles by sea through the perilous Straits of Shargon. The university typically hires a Sailing Ship (or occasionally a Lyrandar galleon) to carry passengers and supplies to Stormreach, then <font></font>
back again. The expedition members are responsible for refreshing their supplies in Stormreach and journeying to their destination on foot. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A Lyrandar airship can bypass the dangers of the sea voyage and cover the distance faster, but the Straits of Shargon are known for brutal storms. An expedition can save considerable <font></font>
time by using a House Orien Teleportation Circle to get to Stormreach, at the cost of having to purchase supplies and perhaps other transportation options in Xen‘drik, where prices are <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
often inflated. The Xen‘drik Journeys table summarizes these options. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Xen’ DRIK JOURNEYS <font></font>
Transport Cost Time <font></font>
Sailing Ship 150 GP/person 32 days <font></font>
[Tooltip Not Found] 750 GP/person 7 days <font></font>
[Tooltip Not Found] 1,500 GP/person 3 days <font></font>
Teleportation Circle 2,500 GP Instant <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Using the guidelines for travel in the Dungeon Master's Guide, you can think about a sea voyage to Xen‘drik in three stages: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Sharn to Shargon’s Teeth islands (500 miles) <font></font>
<font></font>
Through Shargon’'s Teeth (375 miles) <font></font>
<font></font>
To Stormreach (625 miles) <font></font>
<font></font>
In the first stage, characters might face storms, shifting winds, or other environmental hazards that can extend the journey, perhaps even to the point where supplies begin to run low. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The most common challenge in the second stage involves a confrontation with sahuagin that dwell in the waters around Shargon’s Teeth. Some sahuagin bands prove hostile to all <font></font>
travelers, while others sell their services as guides and lead ships through the hard-to-navigate waterways. Successfully completing this stage of the journey might involve combat, careful <font></font>
negotiation, or both. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The third and longest stage might involve encounters with unexpected monsters—from a Giant Crab to a Kraken —living in the seas or on small islands. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Drow or XEN’ DRIK <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Within the cyclopean ruins of Xen’drik, drow are the self-proclaimed inheritors of the Age of Giants. The descendants of elves who were enslaved by giants, drow now dwell in the homes of <font></font>
their former masters, guarding the relics and holy sites that fate has entrusted to them. With a culture based on rebellion, magic, and combat prowess, drow are deadly enemies to any who <font></font>
would attempt to steal or debase the history of Xen’drik. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 114/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Most drow of Xen’drik worship a fierce scorpion-god called Vulkoor. Some theologians of the Sovereign Host and the Dark Six believe Vulkoor to be an aspect of the Mockery (see Eberron: <font></font>
Rising from the Last War), but the Vulkoori drow show no partiality to the followers of the Mockery or any other god but their own. In imitation of their arachnid god, Vulkoori wear plates of <font></font>
scorpion chitin sewn to leather straps and sheaths. They tattoo their flesh using scorpion venom that leaves white scars on their skin. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Vulkoori Stingblade. Vulkoori drow warriors coat their weapons with the same deadly scorpion venom they use to tattoo their skin. Thanks to their extensive use of poison, they are <font></font>
resistant to the scorpion venom’ full effects. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Vulkoori Venom Priest. Vulkoori venom priests delight in inflicting suffering through poison. Their weaponry and magic are carefully crafted to evoke the presence of the scorpion-god and <font></font>
strike terror into their enemies’ hearts. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Umbragen Shadow Walker. Umbragen drow are the descendants of drow who fled the giants and found refuge underground. For countless generations, they have lived in the lightless <font></font>
caverns of Khyber. There, they tapped into a mysterious magical shadow they called the Umbra. Though some have emerged to haunt the surface in the wake of the giant empire's fall, they <font></font>
retain a spiritual and physical connection to the darkness below. Umbragen shadow walkers weave shadow magic to confound foes. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: JORDAN KERBOW <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
VULKOORI AND UMBRAGEN DROW BATTLE IN A CAVERN FILLED WITH MYSTERIES <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Wonpers oF XEN’DRIK <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Huge expanses of Xen‘drik remain completely unexplored, and even the Skyfall Peninsula outside Stormreach is still wilderness. Most known ruins and important sites in Xen’drik lie in the <font></font>
northern region; the exact locations of most sites beyond the peninsula seem like mere speculation. Sites near Stormreach, built in the midst of a ruined giant city, have become the best- <font></font>
known ruins in Xen'drik. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Bazek Mohl. Ancient giants built their so-called “City of Shadows” underground, in a manifest zone linked to the plane of Mabar, the realm of eternal night. The only indication on the <font></font>
surface of the city’s presence far below is a toppled tower in the jungle, its shattered walls of purplish granite infused with shadow magic. Ancient texts in the archives of House Phiarlan <font></font>
suggest a connection between the ancestors of that house and Bazek Mohl, leading scholars to believe that the Mark of Shadow might relate to the shadow magic of this place. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Mel-Agat. For many years, people considered Mel-Aqat mere legend. Mentioned in a variety of ancient texts, this site was supposedly the prison for a mighty fiendish overlord. Until only a <font></font>
few years ago, most scholars had abandoned hope of finding it. Then it was unearthed by a group of adventurers led by the scholar Janik Martell. Fiendish power suffuses the ruins, but <font></font>
Celestial forces are at work there as well, and the Church of the Silver Flame is interested in supporting further expeditions there. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Obsidian City. The Obsidian City is a ruined giant city now inhabited by a group of drow called the Sulatar (“firebinders’). It lies at least eight hundred miles upriver from Stormreach. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Pra‘xirek. Pra’xirek is a ruined giant city near one of the heads (sources) of the Hydra—the great sound that divides the Skyfall Peninsula from the rest of the continent. Two groups of <font></font>
giants still inhabit the ruins, as does a group of drow. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Pyramid of Ebon Flame. The Pyramid of Ebon Flame is one of several scattered creations of the giant wizard-king Addis-Ro, a necromancer who experimented with powers drawn from the <font></font>
depths of Khyber. This huge monolith formed of unbreakable black glass supposedly marks the passage through which the wizard-king journeyed into the deeps. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Ring of Storms. The Ring of Storms, according to legend, was the mountain refuge of a sect of powerful drow spellcasters during the Age of Giants. The fall of an enormous dragonshard <font></font>
from the Ring of Siberys destroyed this sect. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Tharkgun Dhak. A cliffside fortress built by giants in the northern Fangs of Argarak, Tharkgun Dhak is a gateway to the depths of Khyber. Ogres control this site, now infested with <font></font>
Aberrations. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Throne Gate Ruins. Aside from the ruins beneath Stormreach, the Throne Gate Ruins are the closest ruins to the city, about four hundred miles up the Rachi River. This site served as a <font></font>
temple and arcane laboratory at the height of the giant empire. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
RACE FOR THE CRAB TEMPLE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
An adventure for Level 3 characters <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
THE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
SHARN INQUISITIVE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
RIVALS RACE TO WRETCHED RUIN <font></font>
<font></font>

N called Crab Temple, discovered last month near Stormreach. Which team will be the first to <font></font>
<font></font>
cross the deadly Straits of Shargon, cut through the jungles of the Skyfall Peninsula, and <font></font>

<font></font>
MORGRAVE EXPEDITION ADVENTURE t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This section contains a sample adventure, presented in the same format as those in the Dungeon Master's Guide —except that it features the opening situation and hook as an extract from <font></font>
a broadsheet. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ENCOUNTERS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The characters and their rivals (see “Morgrave Expeditions Conflicts” in this chapter) are competing to reach and explore an ancient ruin in the forest of Xen’drik. The adventure involves a <font></font>
journey to Stormreach, the trek to the Crab Temple, exploration of the site, and then the return trip. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Journey to Stormreach. The university charters a Sailing Ship and a crew to carry the characters to the city of Stormreach and back. You can gloss over this long trip, narrating key details <font></font>
as a sort of travel montage, or you can use the guidance in “Journeys to Xen‘drik” in this chapter to structure challenges and encounters during the voyage. For example, the characters <font></font>
might be challenged by sahuagin (one Sahuagin Priest and four Sahuagin Warriors) as they pass through Shargon’s Teeth. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Trek to the Temple. To reach the temple, the party first needs to trek through the jungle to the village of Last Chance (see sidebar), a journey of 250 miles through the coastal terrain around <font></font>
the bay. About halfway there, while the party rests for the night, a group of Hostile Undead—five Skeletons and five Zombies —approaches. The Order of the Emerald Claw (see “Morgrave <font></font>
Expeditions Conflicts” in this chapter) sent them to interfere with the characters’ mission. The Undead are soaking wet, having waded ashore from the small ship that brought them and <font></font>
their commander from Stormreach. The ship is long gone by the time the Undead reach the shore and attack the party. The quality of the skeletons’ weapons strongly suggests but doesn't <font></font>
prove their link to the Emerald Claw. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The characters can rest and resupply in Last Chance, then head southeast for another 150 miles through forest terrain to the vicinity of the Crab Temple. At the start of each day of this <font></font>
journey, have the party choose a character to make a DC 12 Wisdom (Survival) check using Navigator's Tools. On a failed check, the party gets off course, makes no progress that day, and <font></font>
encounters a group of Hostile drow; roll on the Jungle Mishaps table to determine the encounter (Vulkoori drow appear earlier in this chapter). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Juncte Mishars <font></font>
1d8 Encounter <font></font>
1-3 Four Vulkoori Stingblades <font></font>
4-5 One Vulkoori Venom Priest and two Vulkoori Stingblades <font></font>
6-7 Three Vulkoori Stingblades and one Giant Scorpion <font></font>
8 One Scout Captain, one Vulkoori Venom Priest, and four Vulkoori Stingblades (all drow) just starting a battle against the party’s rivals <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Once the party has traveled 144 miles from Last Chance (6 days of successful checks at a Normal pace, or 8 days at a Slow pace), instead have each character make a daily DC 16 <font></font>
Wisdom (Perception) check to find signs of the Crab Temple's location. On any day where no character succeeds on this check, they have another encounter from the Jungle Mishaps <font></font>
table. (Reroll a repeated result of 8 on the table.) After 3 days where at least one character succeeded on this check, the party at last discovers the Crab Temple. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Racing Rivals. The party's rivals more or less keep pace with them through most of the journey. You might give the characters a chance to interact with their rivals in Stormreach, in Last <font></font>
Chance, or in the jungle (particularly if you roll an 8 on the Jungle Mishaps table). Depending on how these interactions go, you can decide whether the rivals reach the temple before or <font></font>
after the characters, or perhaps fail in their mission entirely. Or you can assume that the rivals find the temple 11 days after leaving Last Chance, and compare that to the time the party <font></font>
spends to determine who wins the race. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Crab Temple. The temple is a partially flooded ruin crafted by drow shortly after the fall of the empire of giants. Its name comes from a statue of a humanoid figure outside the ruins; <font></font>
someone removed the statue's head at one point, and a large crab’s shell became lodged on its shoulders. Use the Dragon's Lair map in the Dungeon Master's Guide to represent the <font></font>
temple. (The statue on a dais in the northeast corner of the ruins gives the temple its name.) The temple rises slightly out of a lake, so everything below level 1 is submerged, including the <font></font>
stairs leading down to level 2. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Ruin Denizens. A band of kuo-toa (one Kuo-toa Monitor, two Kuo-toa Whips, and five Kuo-toa) inhabits level 2 of the ruin. They swim quickly to level 1 and initiate combat if anything <font></font>
disturbs the water at the top of the stairs. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Plundering the Ruins. By diving into the lower levels of the temple, the characters can find abundant ancient relics to make their expedition a success: <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
10 PP, 100 GP <font></font>
<font></font>
Iridescent conch shell, carved with Giant runes, that echoes with the sounds of a maelstrom (worth 250 GP) <font></font>
<font></font>
Giant-size gold ring set with turquoise (worth 250 GP) <font></font>
<font></font>
Waterproof case holding a Periapt of Wound Closure, a Pot of Awakening, and a Spell Scroll of Tasha’s Hideous Laughter <font></font>
<font></font>
Optional: Temple Depths. You can add this encounter in level 3 of the ruins if the characters can cooperate with their rivals to overcome it. In this case, the rivals come prepared with <font></font>
Potions of Water Breathing for themselves and have enough extra to share with the party (for a price). On the bottom level of the ruin, the combined parties encounter a Kuo-toa <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 117/162 <font></font>

<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Archpriest, two Kuo-toa, and four Giant Crabs. For this option, provide an additional treasure on this level: a Mace of Terror or another relic from ancient Xen‘drik that might serve as a bone <font></font>
of contention between the characters and their rivals (or the Order of the Emerald Claw) in a later adventure. It could also presage the involvement of the Lords of Dust later in the <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
campaign. <font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
DAVE SARABIA <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
CONFLICT BETWEEN HOUSES ESCALATES TO THE SKIES <font></font>
AS ELEMENTAL AIRSHIPS JOIN IN BATTLE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
URING THE LAST WAR AND IN ITS WAKE, ARTIFICERS ACROSS KHORVAIRE MADE MANY MAGICAL AND MECHANICAL ADVANCES—BUT NONE QUITE AS IMPRESSIVE AS THE <font></font>
elemental airship. With its intricately carved soarwood hull and distinctive ring of elemental energy, the airship is the most advanced elemental-powered vehicle in all Khorvaire. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The first airships appeared only a handful of years ago, the product of a collaboration between the workshops of House Cannith, dragonmarked heirs of House Lyrandar, and elemental <font></font>
binders in Zilargo. Only a dragonmarked heir with the Mark of Storm can easily use an airship’s magical helm to control the elemental bound to the vessel and pilot the ship, so House <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 121/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lyrandar maintains a monopoly on airship travel and services. Some cunning inventors seek to develop ways to undermine House Lyrandar’s control of the skies, but for now, adventurers <font></font>
look to Lyrandar airships as the foremost means of transcontinental travel. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 122/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
OPERATING AN AIRSHIP t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Whether hiring a means of overland travel or seeking to take a crew of their own to the skies, characters are likely to encounter elemental airships during their adventures in Eberron. <font></font>
Supplementing the rules for vehicles in the Player's Handbook, this section details rules for handling airships. The rules for specific ships appear later in this chapter. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Crew MEMBERS AND STATIONS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The standard crew of an elemental airship consists of one captain, one pilot, and one or more additional crew members to operate the ship's other systems. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Each crew member is assigned by the captain to operate one of the ship's systems at a crew station: either the Helm described below or a station detailed in the ship’s description. Each <font></font>
crew station has its own Armor Class and Hit Points separate from the airship. If a crew station is reduced to 0 Hit Points, it becomes inoperable, and crew members can no longer access <font></font>
any of its actions. If a crew station is the target of the Dispel Magic spell, it becomes inoperable for 1 minute. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Some crew stations may require the crew member operating it to have a specific dragonmark. Other stations don’t require them but afford benefits if their operators bear certain <font></font>
dragonmarks. This represents a dragonmarked crew member channeling their unique talents to bolster a ship’s abilities. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Every airship has the following crew station. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
HELM <font></font>
Small Object <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Armor Class:15 —_ Hit Points: 20 <font></font>
Drive (Requires Mark of Storm). The ship moves up to its Fly Speed in any direction. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Shift Engine (Requires Mark of Storm). The ship’s elemental core is suppressed or reengaged. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The helm of an airship consists of a specialized wheel, levers, and other controls. One creature can operate this station at a time. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The pilot (the crew member operating an airship’s Helm) must have the Mark of Storm to interface with the controls. While operating the Helm, a crew member can take the station's Drive <font></font>
and Shift Engine actions. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Tilt and Roll. When taking the Helm’s Drive action, a crew member can spend an amount of movement equal to half the ship’s Fly Speed to invert the ship on its axis, either turning it upside <font></font>
down or righting it. All creatures and objects on the ship that aren't anchored fall when the ship moves in this way. A creature that succeeds on a DC 15 Dexterity saving throw can grab a <font></font>
fixed object within reach to avoid falling. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
REPAIRS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
In addition to the ship repair rules described in the Player’s Handbook, elemental airships can be repaired in an emergency during travel with specialized means. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
If the airship or one of its crew stations has taken damage but has at least 1 Hit Point, a creature can spend 1 hour or more trying to make repairs. The creature making the repairs must be <font></font>
able to reach the damaged area and have 100+ GP of spare parts to make the emergency repairs. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
After 1 hour of work, the creature makes a DC 16 Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) or Intelligence (Arcana) check using Carpenter's Tools, Smith's Tools, or Tinker's Tools. On a successful check, <font></font>
the airship or crew station regains 2d4 + 2 Hit Points and the parts are consumed. If the check fails, the repair can be attempted again using the same replacement parts. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Once an airship or a crew Station has regained Hit Points from an emergency repair, it can’t do so again until the ship has been docked. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
CRASHING <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When an airship crashes into something, both the ship and the creature or object it strikes take Bludgeoning damage based on the size of the struck target, as shown in the Crash Damage <font></font>
table. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
If a ship crashes into a creature or an object of its size or larger, it stops; otherwise, the ship can continue moving if it has any movement left, and whatever it struck moves to the nearest <font></font>
unoccupied space that isn’t in the ship's path. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When an airship crashes, each creature on board the airship makes a DC 10 Strength or Dexterity saving throw (creature's choice). On a failed save, a creature takes Bludgeoning damage <font></font>
based on the size of the struck target (as shown in the Crash Damage table) and has the Prone condition. On a successful save, a creature takes half as much damage only. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Crash Damace <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Size of Creature or Object Struck Bludgeoning Damage <font></font>
Medium or smaller 2d10 <font></font>
Large 4d10 <font></font>
Huge 8d10 <font></font>
Gargantuan 16d10 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
AvolpiNe A CRASH <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A creature in the path of a crashing airship makes a DC 15 Dexterity saving throw. On a successful save, the creature moves to the nearest unoccupied space that isn’t in the ship's path <font></font>
and takes no damage. <font></font>
<font></font>

11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
ELEMENTAL ENGINES <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Airships are powered by an elemental spirit bound to a core carved from a Khyber dragonshard. This core is placed into a heavily fortified containment chamber connected to an arcane <font></font>
matrix spanning—and powering—the whole ship. When the elemental spirit within the core is activated, its elemental energy flows through the matrix’s arteries and the ship's binding struts, <font></font>
forming the distinctive ring attached to every airship. The appearance of a ring depends on the type of elemental spirit bound to the core; for example, a fire elemental spirit generates a <font></font>
blazing ring of orange flames, while an air elemental spirit creates a ring of lightning and occasionally sends jagged lightning bolts streaking across the ship's surface. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
SUPPRESSING THE ELEMENTAL CORE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
At the command of the airship’s pilot, the elemental core can be suppressed, causing the elemental energy to disappear and flow back into the Khyber dragonshard core. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
An airship’s elemental core and connecting arcane matrix are magical effects. If a section of an airship enters an area of antimagic, such as that produced by the Antimagic Field spell, the <font></font>
weapons and other services in that section of the ship are inoperable. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
If the ship's elemental core enters an area of antimagic, the elemental core is automatically suppressed and can't be reengaged until the core leaves the area of antimagic. If the elemental <font></font>
core is the target of the Dispe/ Magic spell, the core is suppressed for 1 minute and can’t be reengaged for the duration. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
While the elemental core is suppressed, the airship’s Fly Speed is reduced to 5 feet, though it can still hover. The airship's Helm is still operable, but crew station actions that require the <font></font>
elemental core can’t be used while it is suppressed. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
GONMARK <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
BREAKING THE ELEMENTAL CORE <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Shattering the core’s Khyber dragonshard releases the elemental spirit bound to it. The shard has AC 17; HP 10; and Immunity to Piercing, Poison, Psychic, and Slashing damage. If an <font></font>
airship’s elemental core is broken, the ship ceases to move (though it continues to hover if airborne), and crew station actions that require the elemental core are disabled. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
To prevent such catastrophic sabotage, most airships keep their elemental cores inside heavily fortified and guarded containment chambers. Each ship has unique protections for its <font></font>
elemental core; one ship might have its core sealed in a trapped adamantine chest inside a hidden room, while another ship might guard its core with an arcane glyph alarm. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Wino Warps <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
In addition to powering an airship's movement and crew stations, the elemental engine also fuels the wind wards that surround the ship as it travels. These wind wards help provide a <font></font>
comfortable travel atmosphere aboard the ship. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
While the wind wards are active, creatures aboard the airship can breathe normally and the deck is free from winds. In addition, creatures on the ship have Advantage on saving throws to <font></font>
avoid being pushed off the deck of the ship, as the wind wards provide a buffer. If the elemental core is suppressed or destroyed, creatures aboard the airship might suffer the effects of <font></font>
high altitude and strong wind (see the Dungeon Master ’s Guide). <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https://;www.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 124/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
AIRSHIPS IN COMBAT t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: NOOR RAHMAN <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Though few airships are designed for warfare, the skies of Khorvaire are rife with potentially dangerous encounters. The following rules are designed to make aerial combat simple and <font></font>
exciting—whether it's combat between two airships, combat between characters on an airship and one or more flying creatures, or combat between characters and other creatures on the <font></font>
deck of an airship. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
MANEUVERING AN AIRSHIP <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Maneuvering the ship during combat is the responsibility of the crew member operating the Helm crew station. If the crew member operating the Helm has the Incapacitated condition or <font></font>
chooses not to use any of that station's actions, the ship moves up to its Fly Speed in a straight line at the start of the Helm crew member's turn, continuing its last direction of travel. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
If no crew is operating the Helm, the ship doesn’t move. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
BoarDING <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When one airship moves to within 5 feet of another airship, creatures can move safely from one ship's deck to the other until one of the ships pulls away. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A ship that has enough movement can pull alongside another vessel, deploy a boarding party, and then move away, provided the members of the boarding party took the Ready action and <font></font>
positioned themselves so they can move onto the other vessel once it's close enough. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Monster TACTICS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Flying monsters such as dragons, rocs, wyverns, and harpies sometimes attack airships—or, more often, the people aboard them. A monster that’s looking for food is most likely to try to <font></font>
pick creatures off the deck and carry them away to eat, while a monster that’s defending its territory might attack the ship itself, heedless of the people on it. A curious monster might <font></font>
simply land on the deck of an airship to look around, enjoying a brief respite from flying. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
If a monster attacks an airship and fails to overcome the airship's damage threshold, the monster knows whether it’s likely to ever deal enough damage to cause real harm to the ship. If it <font></font>
can't hurt the ship, it might turn its attention to a crew station or crew members instead. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Cuaracters IN AirsHip ComBat <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When an airship engages in combat, characters have two options for how to participate in the battle: They can use their own spells, weapons, and other features to join in the battle as they <font></font>
would in any other environment, or they can take up a crew station and wield the ship's armaments and other capabilities. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Some players enjoy the challenge of having new things to do in combat, while others prefer to stick with the familiar options their characters bring to any fight. As DM, learn your players’ <font></font>
preferences and try to build encounters that allow players to do what they most enjoy. A Fighter character whose player has no interest in using the ship's armaments might relish the <font></font>
opportunity to repel boarders, while a Wizard could delight in hurling Fireball spells at an enemy airship. Dragonmarked characters might particularly enjoy crew stations that allow their <font></font>
dragonmarks to shine. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 125/162 <font></font>

<font></font>

<font></font>
VEHICLE STATISTICS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The descriptions of airships in this chapter present the same types of information that <font></font>
appears on the Airborne and Waterborne Vehicles table in the Player's Handbook. <font></font>
Refer to that book for details. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Note that airships’ speed isn’t affected by either wind or water currents. The <font></font>
descriptions here include a speed for overland journeys as well as a tactical speed for <font></font>
use in combat. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Airship crew stations use the same format as siege equipment (see "Siege Equipment" <font></font>
in the Dungeon Master's Guide). <font></font>

LYRANDAR AIR CRUISER t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARTIST: DAVE SARABIA <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Haloed by their enormous rings of elemental energy, Lyrandar Air Cruisers cast imposing shadows as they sail through the skies. These airships are the largest and most iconic means of <font></font>
air travel in Khorvaire, ferrying the wealthy and elite across the Five Nations and beyond. Though airships have become marginally more common since their introduction a few years ago, a <font></font>
fully operational air cruiser is still an impressive sight. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
LYRANDAR AiR CRUISER SUMMARY <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
AC: 16 Crew: 10 <font></font>
HP: 600 Passengers: 40 <font></font>
Damage Threshold: 20 Cargo: 2 tons <font></font>
Speed: 10 mph (Fly Speed 100 ft. [hover]) Cost: 50,000 GP <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Crew STATIONS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
In addition to a Helm, a Lyrandar Air Cruiser has the following crew stations. <font></font>

<font></font>
LigHTNING TuRRET (6) <font></font>
Medium Object <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Armor Class:17 —_ Hit Points: 50 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Lightning Blast (Requires Elemental Core). Ranged Attack Roll: +9, range 80/320 ft. Hit: 22 (4d10) Lightning damage. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This turret swivels to shoot lightning blasts and can accommodate up to one Medium creature. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A creature inside the turret has Three-Quarters Cover against attacks and other effects that originate outside it. While inside, a creature can take the Lightning Blast action to aim and fire <font></font>
the turret. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
SHIELD STATION <font></font>
Large Object <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Armor Class:15 Hit Points: 25 <font></font>
Calibrate (Requires Elemental Core). This action prepares the ship’s wind wards to become shields, enabling the Activate Shields action. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Activate Shields (Requires Elemental Core and Calibrate). The ship and its crew stations gain a +5 bonus to AC, and all creatures on the ship’s deck have Half Cover. These effects last for <font></font>
1 minute, until this station is destroyed, or until the crew member that took this action loses Concentration or leaves this station. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This station is located on the airship's aft controls and augments the ship's wind wards. It can accommodate up to two Medium creatures. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The crew member operating this station can take the Calibrate action and then take the Activate Shields action. The ship can benefit from only one bonus to AC from a Shield Station at a <font></font>
time. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Mark of Warding. \f a creature with the Mark of Warding occupies this crew station, that creature can take the Activate Shields action as a Bonus Action. <font></font>

A recent innovation, the Lyrandar Skyskiff is a nimble and compact airship used for short-range travel and rescue missions. When larger airships are marooned in the sky due to elemental <font></font>
engine failures or other malfunctions, crews often enlist a fleet of skyskiffs to tow the marooned ships to a docking tower for maintenance. <font></font>

<font></font>
UPGRADING AN AIRSHIP t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Privateers, adventurers, and wealthy patrons who acquire an airship of their own may upgrade or personalize its capabilities to better suit their needs. This section details how to improve a <font></font>
ship’s statistics or add new crew stations to an existing airship. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
While an airship is being upgraded, it must remain docked for the duration. If the ship leaves, the work must start over, but you don't need to pay the cost for materials a second time. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Armor Cass <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Protective runes generate additional wards to protect the ship. With 30 days of work and 15,000 GP worth of raw materials, the ship's Armor Class increases by 1. A ship can receive this <font></font>
upgrade up to five times. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Hit Points <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Special alchemical processes infuse a ship's hull with more resilience. With 7 days of work and raw materials equal to one-tenth of the ship’s total cost, the ship's Hit Point maximum and <font></font>
current Hit Points both increase by 20. A ship can receive this upgrade up to five times. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Appitionat Crew STATIONS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
If an airship has the room (at the DM’s discretion) and the captain or owner has sufficient funds, new crew stations can be installed on the vessel. Installing a new crew station takes 14 <font></font>
days of work. Replacing an existing crew station with a different one halves the cost of the new crew station. An airship’s Helm can’t be removed or replaced. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
At the DM's discretion, most of the siege equipment shown in the Dungeon Master’s Guide can also be installed on an airship as a crew station. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ELEMENTAL THRUSTERS (7,500 GP) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Huge Object <font></font>
Armor Class:18 Hit Points: 20 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Boost (Requires Elemental Core). The ship's Fly Speed increases by 50 feet until the start of this crew member's next turn. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This set of metal cylinders connects directly to an airship’s arcane matrix, releasing blasts of elemental energy that propel the airship forward. It can accommodate up to one Medium <font></font>
creature. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A creature occupying this station has Three-Quarters Cover against attacks and other effects that originate outside it. While inside this station, a crew member can take the Boost action. <font></font>
The ship can benefit from only one increase to Fly Speed from an Elemental Thrusters station at a time. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Mark of Passage. |f a creature with the Mark of Passage takes the Boost action, the ship’s Fly Speed increases by 100 feet instead. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
Large Object <font></font>

<font></font>

<font></font>
<font></font>
This station facilitates quick repairs aboard an airship. It can accommodate up to three Medium creatures. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
A creature inside the Repair Clamps station has Half Cover. A crew member operating an airship’s Repair Clamps requires the Mark of Making to interface with the controls. While <font></font>
operating the Repair Clamps, a crew member can take the Quick Repair action. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Sy Vel. (15,000 GP) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Medium Object <font></font>
Armor Class:15 Hit Points: 25 <font></font>
Charge (Requires Elemental Core). A crew member must expend one level 1+ spell slot to charge the Sky Veil. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Cloak (Requires Elemental Core and Charge). The ship has the Invisible condition, and while on board it, creatures are also Invisible. These effects last for 1 hour, until this station is <font></font>
destroyed, or until the crew member that took this action loses Concentration or leaves this station. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This marvel of arcane engineering sits on the prow of an airship and channels powerful illusion magic to allow a ship and its passengers to pass undetected. It can accommodate up to <font></font>
one Medium creature. The crew member operating this station can take the Charge action and then take the Cloak action. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Mark of Shadows. If a creature with the Mark of Shadows occupies this crew station, that creature can take the Cloak action as a Bonus Action. <font></font>

11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
JOURNEYS IN THE SKIES t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Traveling across the continent in an airship is different—but no less thrilling—than trekking overland. This section builds on the travel rules in the Dungeon Master's Guide to help make <font></font>
airship journeys more dynamic. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
TRAVEL PAce <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Airships (like other vehicles) use their speed in miles per hour to determine their rate of travel, so characters traveling in an airship don't choose a travel pace. An airship's crew determines <font></font>
how many hours per day the ship can travel; the ship’s pilot is subject to the rules for extended travel (beyond 8 hours per day) in the Dungeon Master's Guide. Commercial airships often <font></font>
have two or even three pilots, allowing the ship to make progress for more than 8 hours per day. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Airships are unaffected by travel terrain, but when traveling through areas of heavy precipitation, the airship travels at half the normal speed. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ARIAL CHALLENGES <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
One of the great advantages of airship travel is that it isn’t subject to many of the challenges and obstacles that interfere with travel on the ground. House Orien was sorely hampered by <font></font>
the collapse of the bridge connecting Thaliost and Rekkenmark, and then the devastation of Cyre in the Mourning; it is impossible for the lightning rail to get from Thrane or Breland to <font></font>
Karrnath and other points west, and nearly impossible for carts or mounts to cross the dual barriers of Scions Sound and the Mournland. Airships, by contrast, soar across the sound with <font></font>
ease and can cross the Mournland at significantly less risk than travelers face on land. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
That said, airship journeys aren't without their challenges, which might include encounters, navigation, weather hazards, and searches. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ENCOUNTERS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Airships may encounter all sorts of flying monsters, environmental effects, or other vessels during their journeys. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Encounter Distance. The tremendous range of visibility under clear conditions on an airship journey means that an encounter can occur at great distances, allowing characters and crews <font></font>
to prepare for combat, seek to make friendly contact, or evade encounters in the sky. The Airship Travel Conditions table shows typical encounter distances for different sky conditions. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Airsuip TRAVEL CONDITIONS <font></font>
<font></font>
Conditions Encounter Distance Navigation DC <font></font>
<font></font>
Day, clear skies 1,000 ft. 10 <font></font>
<font></font>
Day, light precipitation 500 ft. 13 <font></font>
<font></font>
Day, heavy precipitation 250 ft. 15 <font></font>
<font></font>
Night, clear skies 500 ft. 10 <font></font>
<font></font>
Night, light precipitation 250 ft. 15 <font></font>
<font></font>
Night, heavy precipitation 6d6 x 10 ft. 20 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Encounters at 250 feet or more might stretch the definition of an encounter. Bear in mind the range at which most spells and weapons are effective: <font></font>
<font></font>
250 feet is long range for a Ballista and most Ranged weapons and beyond the range of most spells. <font></font>
<font></font>
500 feet is long range for a Mangonel or a Longbow and beyond the range of most Ranged weapons. <font></font>
<font></font>
1,000 feet is long range for a Cannon and beyond the range of most siege equipment and crew stations. <font></font>
<font></font>
The Dungeon Master's Guide includes suggestions for tracking distances between creatures (or airships) when an encounter begins at very long range. It's often best to wait until the two <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
parties in an encounter are closer together before rolling Initiative for individual creatures; in the meantime, let the whole ship and its crew act at once, taking turns with whatever the ship <font></font>
encounters. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 133/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Random Encounters. You may use the following table to generate random encounters as adventurers navigate the skies. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
AIRBORNE ENCOUNTERS <font></font>
1d10 Encounter <font></font>
1 A gleaming Lyrandar Air Cruiser helmed by a Friendly but pompous Noble Prodigy or Lyrandar Scion (described in chapter 5) <font></font>
2 A Strider Airship with a Hostile crew of 2d4 Pirates and one Pirate Captain <font></font>
3 2d6 Lyrandar Skyskiffs gathered for a race; the ships’ pilots are Indifferent but disgruntled toward anyone who interrupts their activities <font></font>
4 A derelict Lyrandar Air Cruiser, stranded in the sky after its engine’s elemental core shattered <font></font>
5 1d4 Air Elementals making cloud sculptures <font></font>
6 2d6 Griffons defending their territory <font></font>
7 Two Sphinxes of Secrets, trying to figure out how the airship flies <font></font>
8 Three Wyverns hunting for prey <font></font>
9 A flock of 2d4 Perytons hiding in a cloud, waiting to ambush unsuspecting travelers <font></font>
10 One Roc looking for food to carry to its nest <font></font>
NAVIGATION <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Guiding an airship through the sky is often as difficult as charting an oceangoing vessel’s journey across the seas. Seasoned airship captains know how to use a combination of maps and <font></font>
other navigational tools, the moons’ and stars’ positions, and the bird’s-eye view of the landscape to ensure their vessel stays its course. Even so, inclement weather interferes with vision, <font></font>
and distinguishing landmarks from high in the air isn’t always easy. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
When an airship is at risk of getting lost—such as when traveling through dense fog or over unfamiliar land—the crew member operating the ship's Helm makes a Wisdom (Survival) check; <font></font>
the DC for this check is determined by the Airship Travel Conditions table. Another crew member who has proficiency in the Survival skill can take the Help action to assist this check as <font></font>
normal. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
On a failed check, the ship goes off course. The DM decides what the final consequences of this are, but at base, the airship’s journey is extended by 1d4 days. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
WeatHeR Hazarps <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
With no terrain obstacles to worry about, the most significant obstacles in airship travel are environmental effects caused by weather. The Environmental Hazards table suggests obstacles <font></font>
an airship might have to overcome. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
ENVIRONMENTAL HAZARDS <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
1d4 Encounter <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
1 The airship experiences rough turbulence. Each creature aboard the ship must succeed on a DC 10 Constitution saving throw or have the Poisoned condition for 1d4 hours due to <font></font>
motion sickness. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
2 The airship travels at extreme altitudes for 1d4 hours. During this time, the ship is in an area of extreme cold (see “Environmental Effects” in the Dungeon Master's Guide) and <font></font>
reduced gravity: creatures’ and objects’ weights are halved, and creatures’ High Jump and Long Jump distances are doubled. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
3 The airship passes through a lightning storm, which takes 1d4 hours. During this time, the area around the ship is Heavily Obscured, and any creature that starts its turn on the <font></font>
ship's deck must succeed on a DC 10 Dexterity saving throw or be struck by lightning and take 4 (1d8) Lightning damage. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
4 The airship enters an area of strong wind (see “Environmental Effects” in the Dungeon Master's Guide), which lasts for 1d4 hours. When the ship first enters this area, the crew <font></font>
member operating the ship’s Helm must succeed on a DC 15 Constitution saving throw, or the ship inverts. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
SEARCHES <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
As with a journey on the ground, sometimes characters spend part of their journey searching for something—usually something on the ground. When characters are searching the ground <font></font>
from the air, use the Search DC for the terrain on the ground from the Travel Terrain table in the Dungeon Master's Guide, but feel free to give characters Advantage on Wisdom (Perception) <font></font>
checks if their aerial vantage point makes it easier for them to see what they’re looking for. <font></font>
<font></font>

BOOTS OF THE WINDING PATH t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Wondrous Item, Uncommon (Requires Attunement) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
While wearing these boots, you can take a Bonus Action to teleport up to 15 feet to an unoccupied space you can see. You must have occupied that space at some point during the current <font></font>
turn. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 136/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
DAZLINGWEAPON <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Weapon (Any Simple or Martial), Rare (Requires Attunement) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This magic weapon grants a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls made with it. While holding it, you can take a Bonus Action to cause it to shed Bright Light in a 30-foot radius and Dim <font></font>
Light for an additional 30 feet. You can extinguish the light as a Bonus Action. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The weapon has 4 charges. You can take a Reaction immediately after being hit by an attack roll to expend 1 of the weapon's charges and force the attacker to make a DC 15 Constitution <font></font>
saving throw. On a failed save, the attacker has the Blinded condition until the end of its next turn. The weapon regains 1d4 expended charges daily at dawn. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 137/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
HELM OF AWARENESS t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Wondrous Item, Uncommon <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
While wearing this helmet, you have Advantage on Initiative rolls. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 138/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
MANIFOLD TOOL be <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Wondrous Item, Common (Requires Attunement) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This tool takes the form of a wrench, a screwdriver, or another basic tool. As a Magic action, you can touch the item and transform it into a type of Artisan’s Tools of your choice. Whatever <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
form the tool takes, you have proficiency with it when you use it. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 139/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
MIND SHARPENER | 1 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Ring, Uncommon (Requires Attunement) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The item has 4 charges. When you fail a Constitution saving throw to maintain Concentration, you can take a Reaction and expend 1 of the item’s charges to succeed instead. The item <font></font>
regains 1d4 expended charges daily at dawn. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 140/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
REPEATING SHOT be <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Weapon (Any Simple or Martial with the Ammunition Property), Uncommon (Requires Attunement) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This magic weapon grants a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls made with it when it’s used to make a ranged attack, and it ignores the Loading property if it has that property. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
If the weapon lacks ammunition, it produces its own, automatically creating one piece of magic ammunition when the wielder makes a ranged attack roll with it. The ammunition created <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
by the weapon vanishes the instant after it hits or misses a target. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 141/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
REPULSION SHIELD <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Armor (Shield), Uncommon <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
You gain a +1 bonus to Armor Class while wielding this shield. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
The shield has 4 charges. While holding it, when a Large or smaller creature within 5 feet of you hits you with a melee attack roll, you can take a Reaction to expend 1 of the shield’s charges <font></font>
and push the attacker up to 15 feet straight away. The shield regains 1d4 expended charges daily at dawn. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 142/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
RETURNING WEAPON t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Weapon (Any Simple or Martial with the Thrown Property), Uncommon <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
This magic weapon grants a +1 bonus to attack and damage rolls made with it, and it returns to your hand immediately after you use it to make a ranged attack roll. <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
https:/Awww.dndbeyond.com/sources/dnd/efota 143/162 <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
11/25/25, 7:47 PM Eberron: Forge of the Artificer <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
SPELL-REFUELING RING t <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
Ring, Uncommon (Requires Attunement by a Spellcaster) <font></font>
<font></font>
<font></font>
While wearing this ring, you can recover one expended spell slot as a Bonus Action. The recovered slot can be of level 3 or lower. Once used, the ring can’t be used again until the next <font></font>
dawn. <font></font>
<font></font>
